# Changelog

## [Unreleased]

- **Three defects found while writing the user guides. Each was reproduced on production inside a rolled-back transaction before it was fixed.**
  - **A guild admin could take any player from another guild on the same server.** "Player ID already exists → Request Transfer" always filed the request with the caller's own guild as target, and `resolve_guild_transfer` let the target guild approve. So the officer approved their own request, and the player moved with history, sanctions and portal account without the losing guild being asked. Reproduced: `ok, approved`, player now in the puller's guild.
    - `guild_transfers.requested_by` now records who asked. Whoever asked never decides: a player's own request is decided by the receiving guild, an admin pull by the guild the player is leaving. The puller may only withdraw.
    - A `gm_authenticated_update` policy also let the target guild's admins rewrite pending rows directly, walking around any rule in the RPC. It is dropped. The API roles keep SELECT only, and `anon` loses Supabase's default ALL.
    - The `'ALPHA'` fallback in `gm_admin_request_transfer` is gone.
    - Migration `20260930140000`. After the fix: the puller gets `permission_denied`, a direct UPDATE gets `42501`, and the losing guild's approval works.
  - **The Migration Hub AI summary could read another tenant's intel.** In page mode it loaded the page by id with the service role, and checked only the scope, guild and server the *request* claimed. In digest mode, Server Intel had no server check for guild admins.
    - Access is now checked on the data actually read (`_shared/intel-access.ts`, mirroring the wiki's policies). A page the caller cannot read answers exactly like a missing one.
    - Summaries are rate-limited per account (10 per 10 minutes, failing closed), and the provider's raw error no longer reaches the browser. The prompt names the right game.
    - `privacy.html` now declares that note text goes to Gemini for summaries, and that bug reports go to the operator's Discord channel.
  - **A Members OCR scan could delete the R5 leader.** A leader missing from the screenshots was listed under "Removed Members", ticked by default, and one confirmation erased their history.
    - The R5 is no longer listed for removal, and the commit filters it out regardless.
    - The single Delete button and Ban explain instead of failing.
    - A `BEFORE DELETE` trigger refuses to delete an R5 from any user session except a super_admin's (migration `20260930150000`). System paths (the nightly DEMO reset) are unaffected, and so is the handover: change the old R5's role, then remove them.

- **The Overview now says when Discord reminders cannot work.** Measured on 2026-09-30: **17 of 31 guilds have no Discord webhook anywhere**, so no reminder, event post or GvG task list can reach them, whatever their switches say, and nothing in the app told them. Admins now see a callout, "Discord reminders are not set up", with a **Set up** button that opens Discord notifications. It disappears as soon as any webhook exists: the default one or any event's own. It reads one `guild_config` query, and a failed read shows nothing rather than a false alarm.
  - **Reminders were *not* made "on by default"**, which was the first idea. Every guild that has a webhook already has every switch saved explicitly, so the change would have altered nothing. It would also have reopened the P0 "ghost notifications" bug, where a missing setting counted as enabled and reminders fired while the panel showed them off.

- **Three bot problems found while writing the user guides.**
  - **`/setup-server` doubled a server when re-run.** Roles were reused by name, but every category and channel was created again, and the app was repointed at the new copies. Categories are now matched by name, and channels by name, parent category and type. The match uses the name Discord actually stores, which is lower-cased for text channels, so it does not miss a channel Discord renamed on creation. A re-run only adds what is missing, keeps permissions an admin may have tuned, and does not repost the welcome embeds. The success message reports "created / already there".
  - **Its confirmation prompt described a template that no longer exists**: "28 Text, 3 Voice" and four roles, two of them with obsolete names, for a build of 32 text channels, 3 voice channels and 5 roles. The prompt is now counted from the template itself (`summarizeTemplate`), so it cannot drift again.
  - **`/call-to-arms` forgot every answer on each worker restart.** The tally lived in a `Map`, so the next click repainted the post with that one click. The embed cannot serve as a backup, because it shows at most 15 or 10 names per list. Answers are now stored in `discord_rally_responses` (migration `20260930130000`): RLS forced, no policy, service role only, purged daily after 7 days. `privacy.html` §3 and §4 declare it. A failed write says so instead of repainting a partial tally.
  - The bot invite link goes into the install guide.

- **Player Portal event submissions had never recorded anything.** Measured on 2026-09-30: 8 `score_submission` audit entries since 2026-08-19, and **not one** has a matching `event_participants` row. Each updated zero rows and reported success. Every other attempt was refused, for one of three reasons:
  - **Every pre-created row counted as "validated".** `is_pending` defaults to `false`, and starting an event creates a row for every member, so `is_pending === false` described every untouched row too. The rule is now "an officer recorded something": participation or a score (`isOfficerRecorded`). A player can submit into the row created at Start and cannot overwrite one an officer filled.
  - **Shadowfront wrote to a name no row carries.** The status is named per squad ("Shadowfront Squad 1"), but the rows are stored under "Shadowfront". Both the read and the write now go through `participantEventName`. The portal card had the same mismatch: it showed a Score field and none of the Shadowfront toggles.
  - **SvS/GvG refused the Days 1-5 score** (`granular_session_use_daily`) as soon as an officer had opened the war tab. That refusal protected a real invariant: the daily grid is the single source of truth. So the scores now go **through** the grid, via `gm_upsert_daily_scores` (day 0 for the total, day 6 for PvP), as the officers' own entry and the OCR already do. That RPC gated unconditionally on `check_user_guild_write_access`, which answers false for every Edge Function, so migration `20260930120000` applies the `IF auth.uid() IS NOT NULL` idiom. Proven on production in a rolled-back transaction: before, `forbidden`; after, day 0 and day 6 recorded and the row derived; a member's own browser session is still `forbidden`.
  - **A missing row now fails loudly** (`not_in_session`, e.g. "Not in this squad"), and the update checks it touched a row.
  - **The portal never showed what it held.** It read a `current_data` key the server never sent, so every card said "Not submitted yet". It now reads the real fields and shows "Awaiting approval" or "Confirmed by an officer". The "I was appointed" and "I was excused" toggles are gone: the server discards both on purpose, so they looked saved and wrote nothing.
  - **Arms Race and Shadowfront get the Pending → Approve control** the SvS/GvG/DTR page has. Stats exclude pending rows, so without it a submission there would never have counted.
  - Gate: 0 TS errors, **167 suites / 2162 tests**, build clean, 38 e2e green. `deno check` reports the same 69 pre-existing typing errors before and after.

- **Three bugs from the community, fixed.**
  - **Half-hour timezones could not be saved (TICKET-0010).** Adelaide, Darwin, India, Nepal, Iran, Afghanistan, Myanmar and Newfoundland all failed: `guild_members.timezone_offset` was `INTEGER`, so the admin edit hit `invalid input syntax for type integer: "9.5"`, and the Player Portal's `UpdateTimezoneSchema` demanded `.int()` and answered `invalid_payload`. The UI offered those zones and the database refused every one; none of the 154 stored offsets had ever been fractional. The column is now `numeric(4,2)` with a quarter-hour CHECK (Nepal is +5:45), `gm_update_player_timezone` takes a `numeric` (the stale two-argument overload is dropped so PostgREST cannot find the call ambiguous), and Discord's `/timezone` is a NUMBER option instead of an INTEGER.
  - **A guild admin could crown a new R5 but never demote the old one**, so every leadership handover left two leaders until a super admin stepped in. `gm_protect_r5_role_change` only let a super admin touch an R5's role. It now lets anyone who may already write that guild's roster (the guild's own admins, and a server admin on its server) do it. Automated paths stay locked out: service role, the Discord bot, and the OCR import. Proven against production inside a rolled-back transaction: the DEMO guild admin now demotes the DEMO R5; a member and another guild's admin still reach 0 rows; a caller with no session still gets `42501`.
  - **"Generate Access" created accounts whose password nobody could see.** The browser generated the password, sent it, and re-rendered the card masked with its Copy button hidden, so the only way to hand over a login was to find "Renew Password". The server now generates it, the same way `reset-password` does, using the readable alphabet (no 0/O or 1/l/I), and returns it once. The new card shows it, copies it, and says it is shown only this once. The client-side generator is gone.
  - Gate: 0 TS errors, **166 suites / 2140 tests**, build clean, 38 e2e green.

- **Every slash command appeared twice in Discord — my own fix caused it.** Discord lists global and guild-scoped commands **separately**, so a command registered in both shows up twice in the picker. The worker registered the same set globally *and* per guild for instant availability, and the per-guild half had failed on every boot since it was written (the global command ids a guild-scoped `PUT` rejects). Repairing it made it succeed — and every command promptly doubled. The duplicate *was* the fix working.
  - One scope now, and it is global. The guild scope is **cleared** on every boot: idempotent, cheap, and it repairs any server still holding a duplicate set. `guildCreate` no longer copies the global set into the joining server either — that was the same mistake on a different trigger. The cost is stated rather than hidden: a new or changed command now waits on global propagation, which is the honest trade for a command list players can read.
  - **The audit/officers channel is configurable from the app.** It was `guild_config`-only, which meant nobody could set it without SQL — and nobody had: **zero** rows existed across 28 guilds, so the officer notices the roster gate writes had nowhere to go. New field next to the verification channel ID, with a hint saying exactly what lands there and that leaving it empty puts those notices in front of the players. Set for ALPHA, OMEGA and IMK.
  - **The worker now says at startup whether the privileged members intent is in effect.** A successful login looks identical whether `GuildMembers` was declared and accepted or never requested, so confirming the Render switch meant running `/reconcile-roles` and reading its refusal. Confirmed live: `GuildMembers intent: REQUESTED and accepted`.
  - Gate: 0 TS errors, **166 suites / 2138 tests**, build clean, 38 e2e green.

- **The duplicate commands are retired — and retiring them turned out to mean moving `/update-stats`, not deleting it.**
  - `/power` and `/glory` did exactly what `/update-stats` does. But the naive retirement would have been a **downgrade**: `/power` went through `updatePlayerPower`, which clamps to `MAX_ALLOWED_POWER` and writes a `power_update` audit row, while the worker's `/update-stats` called `gm_upsert_player_metrics` and `gm_upsert_player_glory` **directly** — no clamp, no audit row, nothing in `system_audit_logs`. So `/update-stats` moved to the Edge Function's audited helpers first, and only then did the two duplicates go.
  - **`week.js` is deleted with them.** It existed only so the worker could file a Glory score under the same ISO Monday as the app, duplicating `getWeekStartIso`. The write went back to the Deno side, which has the original, so the second copy is gone rather than left to drift — the duplication that test was written to police no longer exists.
  - **`/absence set` retired**, `list` and `cancel` kept. `set` duplicated `/absent`, which does the same write **and** alerts the officer channel. `list` is not a duplicate of `/absences`: one lists your own absences, the other the guild's. `cancel` has no equivalent anywhere, so removing the whole command would have been a capability loss dressed up as cleanup.
  - **Dropping a name from the registration map is not enough to remove it from Discord**, because the merge step folds unknown existing commands back in so the worker never wipes one it does not own. Retired names are now excluded explicitly — `RETIRED_COMMANDS` — or Discord keeps serving a command no source file describes, which is how `/power` came to exist in the first place.
  - Fifteen commands registered, two retired. Gate: 0 TS errors, **166 suites / 2136 tests**, build clean, 38 e2e green.

- **Five slash commands existed on Discord with no handler anywhere. Running one printed "The application did not respond."** Phases 3-5 of the Discord rework.
  - **The transport, settled with numbers instead of guesses.** Discord delivers interactions over **one** channel — an Interactions Endpoint URL or the Gateway — and nothing in the repository said which. Measured: `discord-bot-interactions` was invoked **0 times in 24 hours** while `discord-profile-verification` was invoked 15, and the worker's Render log shows it registering nine commands over the Gateway. So the Gateway is the live transport, the Ed25519 endpoint had never carried a request — and `/power`, `/glory`, `/timezone`, `/challenges` and `/absence`, which `scripts/register-discord-commands.mjs` had registered and the worker's merge step preserves, reached a worker with no handler for them.
  - **Routed, not reimplemented.** Those five run the validated, audited `_shared/player-actions.ts` paths the Player Portal uses. The worker now forwards the interaction to the Edge Function with the shared worker secret and relays the reply, so there is one implementation and one transport. A present-but-wrong bearer token is refused outright rather than falling through to the signature branch, and the Ed25519 path is kept for the day the owner switches to an Interactions Endpoint URL. The worker defers the reply first: Discord allows three seconds, and an Edge round trip plus a database write can exceed it.
  - **The interaction is rebuilt into Discord's own shape**, because discord.js `toJSON()` does not return the raw gateway payload and the Edge Function reads `interaction.data.options` — including the subcommand nested in `options[0].options` for `/absence`.
  - **`scripts/register-discord-commands.mjs` is deleted**, and the five definitions moved into the worker. It was a third registry, run by hand, that nothing kept in step with the two handlers — and it is how commands came to exist on Discord that no source file described. The worker now owns all fourteen and registers them on boot.
  - **Per-guild command registration had failed on every boot since it was written.** `body` carries each global command's id, which is what makes the global PUT an update; a guild-scoped PUT rejects those ids with `APPLICATION_COMMANDS_INVALID_ID`, surfaced as "Invalid Form Body" — fourteen warnings per boot, and the instant availability that loop exists for never once happened. The ids are stripped now.
  - **The officer notice ADR-020 promised had nowhere to go.** `guild_config` holds **zero** rows for `discord_audit_channel_id` and `discord_officer_channel_id` — not empty ones, none at all — so a held verification told the player and nobody who could fix it. It falls back to the verification channel now, with the durable helper so it survives the cleanup around it, and says why it is there.
  - **The Edge Function had the same multi-account bug the worker did**: `.maybeSingle()` on `discord_account_links`, which errors on the identity that legitimately holds two accounts. Fixed the same way, and it now filters to active accounts.
  - **New `/mystats`** — your attendance per event type against your guild's pooled rate, from `gm_player_stats`, the only stats RPC a service-role caller can reach. It deliberately computes **no** weighted score: the weighting lives in `src/core/scoring/reliability.ts`, shared by the admin page and the Portal, and a third copy in the worker's Node process is exactly how those two came to disagree in the first place.
  - **New `/verifications`** — the held verifications an officer could not otherwise see, since `system_audit_logs` is super_admin-only in the app. Guild from the channel map or the caller's linked account, never from an option; gated by Discord's own `ManageMessages`; ephemeral, bounded to 20 rows, and it pings nobody.
  - **New `/reconcile-roles`** — who still holds the tag role without a roster row, which ADR-020 does not cover (it stops the role being granted wrongly, not someone leaving afterwards). Report first, removal only behind a confirmation button only the officer who asked can press, only ever the tag role, never a kick. A holder the bot cannot identify is listed and never touched, and a truncated roster read refuses to report anyone as missing. It needs Discord's privileged **Server Members Intent**, so the intent is opt-in via `DISCORD_GUILD_MEMBERS_INTENT=true`: declaring it while the Developer Portal toggle is off makes the bot fail to log in entirely.
  - Gate: 0 TS errors, **166 suites / 2136 tests**, build clean, 38 e2e green.

- **Picking a guild as a war opponent has never worked in production, and the project's own logs said so.** Found by reading them rather than the code: `/rest/v1/rpc/gm_list_opponent_candidates` answered **400 eleven times** between 2026-09-26 23:26 and 2026-09-27 19:37.
  - **One column that does not exist.** `gm_list_opponent_candidates` does `COALESCE(g.name, g.id)` on the `guild` branch, and `gm_upsert_war_session` reads the same column when the chosen opponent is a guild in this tool. Production answers `42703: column g.name does not exist`, which PostgREST surfaces as a 400. The `server` branch never touches it, so only the guild half failed — and because the picker fails first, an admin could never reach the save path, which is why only one of the two ever appeared in the logs.
  - **Why the column is missing**: `20260812000001` declares `name TEXT` inside `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS public.guilds`. The table already existed, so the statement was a no-op and the column was never created. `src/types/database.generated.ts`, generated *from* production, has no `name` either — production and the generated types agree, and only the canonical DDL disagrees. `20260928110000` fixes the two functions rather than the schema: `g.id` is what the `COALESCE` resolved to anyway (all 28 rows would have had a NULL name), and the guild's identifier *is* its name in this product. The stray declaration is left alone — editing an applied migration is what ADR-008 forbids — and is now documented instead of load-bearing.
  - **Same root cause as `/setup-server`**, fixed the same day: it selected `guilds.name`, got 42703, and fell back to inventing server number `"1000"`.
  - **Nothing could have caught it.** `tests/rpc_names_exist.test.js` checks that the *functions* a caller names exist, never the *columns* inside them, and no test compared SQL against the generated types. `tests/sql_reads_columns_that_exist.test.js` now does, over the migrations and over `src/`, `supabase/functions/` and the worker — sabotage-verified, with the two migrations that introduced the read pinned as superseded history rather than silently excused.
  - Also checked while reading the logs, and **not** bugs: the three `ocr-guild-members` JSON-parse errors are the ones `thinking_budget: 0` was written for, all in the 09:00 hour of 09-27, with **1,795 OCR log lines since and zero failures**; and the `list_event_weeks` (×19) and `gm_stats_data` (×3) 404s come from browser tabs left open on an old bundle — neither name has a caller left in the repository.
  - Gate: 0 TS errors, **166 suites / 2118 tests**, build clean, 38 e2e green.

- **`/glory` has never saved a single score, for any player, since 2026-08-17 — and `/update-stats` said it had.** Four defects in the Discord bot, each measured before it was touched.
  - **The bot called a function that exists nowhere.** `/update-stats glory:` invoked `gm_submit_event_score` — not in production, not in any migration, not anywhere but that one call site. supabase-js answers a missing function with `{ data: null, error }` rather than throwing, the worker logged the error to the console, and then replied **“✅ Stats Updated”** with the new figure. It now calls `gm_upsert_player_glory`, under the same ISO Monday the app uses, and **fails the reply** when the write is refused.
  - **Why the guard missed it**: `tests/rpc_names_exist.test.js` greps `.rpc('name')` call sites against the migrations — over `src/` and `supabase/functions/`. The worker is neither, so the one directory running an always-on process with the service-role key was the one directory nobody grepped. It is in scope now, sabotage-verified (reintroducing the phantom name fails the suite).
  - **And the RPC it should have been calling was broken too.** Measured on DEMO as a caller with `auth.uid() IS NULL` — which is what every Edge Function and the worker are: `gm_upsert_player_metrics(...)` → `ok: true`; `gm_upsert_player_glory(...)` → **`ok: false, permission_denied`**. Two sibling RPCs, called the same way by the same code, disagreeing. The history: an archived migration had explicitly allowed `service_role` *because* glory was answering `permission_denied`; the zero-trust rewrite of `check_user_guild_write_access` then made it return false whenever there is no user to authorize — correct for a shared helper — and `gm_upsert_player_metrics` had meanwhile adopted the idiom that survives it (`IF auth.uid() IS NOT NULL THEN` gate). `20260817060000` re-declared glory calling the helper unconditionally, and nothing failed loudly because the function returns `(ok, error)` rows instead of raising. `20260928100000` gives glory its sibling's gate. **Nothing is loosened for a browser**: an authenticated caller is still checked for guild write access and an active subscription. This also un-breaks `/glory` in `discord-bot-interactions`, which has been honestly answering `permission_denied` to every player.
  - **Every command threw for the one person holding two accounts.** `resolveLinkedAccount` used `.maybeSingle()` on `discord_account_links`, which migration `20260927120000` re-keyed to `(discord_user_id, account_id)` precisely so an admin who is also a player can hold both. `.maybeSingle()` **errors** on two rows rather than picking one, so `/myinfo`, `/absent`, `/update-stats`, `/profile @them`, `/top`, `/absences` and the Call-to-Arms button all threw for that identity. It reads every link now, keeps the active accounts, and **asks which one** rather than picking — silently picking is how an admin's command lands on their player account. The best-effort paths treat an ambiguous link as resolving no guild, instead of reading `undefined`.
  - **`/setup-server` would provision 31 channels for a guild that does not exist.** It selected `guilds.name` — a column production does not have, so the query answered `42703` and `guildRow` was **null for every guild**. The code then fell back to server number `"1000"` and to the raw input as the tag, so a typo built a whole server for a tenant nobody had created, on a number nobody had entered. It now refuses an unregistered guild, refuses a guild with no server number instead of inventing one, and the provisioner throws rather than defaulting.
  - **A freshly provisioned server could never verify anybody.** The provisioner created `👽 Visitor` and `🛡️ [TAG] Member`, while the verification flow resolves roles by name through `normalizeTag`, which only strips brackets — so those names normalise to `👽 VISITOR` and `🛡️ [TAG] MEMBER` and matched nothing. Every new server hit the `no_matching_role` dead end until someone renamed the roles by hand. They are `[VISITOR]`, `[TAG]` and the new `[PENDING]` now, with the personality in the colour, and the test imports the real `normalizeTag` so the two sides are checked against each other rather than against a copy.
  - **The bot's OCR was still trying three Gemini models Google has never published.** Finding B1 removed `gemini-3.5-flash`, `gemini-3.7-flash` and `gemini-3-flash-preview` from `ocr-guild-members` and `migration-summary` and left the third copy behind, in the one module the Discord flow uses — three full HTTP round trips and three 404s on every fallback. `_shared/gemini-vision.ts` now imports the single list, and inherits the measured `thinking_budget: 0` fix along with the 400-retry that drops the field.
  - Gate: 0 TS errors, **165 suites / 2111 tests**, build clean, 38 e2e green.

- **A screenshot could write itself into a guild's roster, and the tag it carried resolved across game servers.** Both closed (ADR-020).
  - **The check and the thing being checked were the same write.** `evaluateVerification` looked for the player in `guild_members` and, not finding them, **inserted the row** — then the Discord role was granted on the strength of that insert. A screenshot showing `[PR1M]` was therefore sufficient to be handed ALPHA's Discord role *and* to appear in ALPHA's member list. The roster is now the authority and this path never inserts when a guild opts in: a player who is not already in the member list is refused, nothing is written, and the officer channel gets a durable message naming them.
  - **The tag resolved globally.** `resolveTagToGuild` scanned every tenant's `guild_config` and took whichever row iterated first on a collision — nothing in it knew that ALPHA, OMEGA and IMK are the three guilds of server `#1058`. `resolveTagToGuildOnServer` now considers only the guilds sharing the verification channel's own `guilds.server_number`. A same-tag guild on another game server resolves to **nothing** (its members become visitors here, and cannot be blocked from their own server), a collision inside one server is reported to officers and never guessed, and a channel guild with no server number fails visibly instead of widening back to a global scan. A server number read off the screenshot that disagrees with the channel's own forces the visitor path even when the tag matched.
  - **`DEFAULT_GUILD_TAGS` is deleted.** The seven-entry `ALPHA -> [PR1M]` table was the ADR-003 violation finding H2 already removed from the frontend — re-created inside `supabase/functions/_shared/`, where the test that guards it could not see it, because that test only walked `src/`. Two of its values were wrong there too. A guild with no stored tag now falls back to its own id, and `tests/discord_verification.test.js` walks `supabase/functions/` so it cannot come back a third time.
  - **The worker was arming a second leak.** `addVerifiedRole` ran on **any** successful outcome, including the visitor path — and `server-provisioner.js` auto-wires `discord_member_role_id` to a role it names `[TAG] Member`. No such config row exists in production yet, so nothing had leaked; the first `/setup-server` on the 1058 server would have started handing `[PR1M] Member` to visitors. It is now reachable only under `result.role === "guild_member"`.
  - **The cross-guild veto was refusing real members.** `gm_find_player_by_uid` is guild-blind (`WHERE uid = ... ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT 1`), and **four UIDs exist in two guilds at once** (ALPHA+PAIN, ALPHA+YARR, ALPHA+BABE), so the legitimate member of whichever guild was older got `uid_registered_elsewhere`. The call and the error are gone: the target guild's own roster answers the only question being asked, and cross-guild presence became information in the officer message and the audit metadata.
  - **A UID-only gate would have refused 11 real OMEGA members and 55 PSA ones.** Their roster rows carry `TEMP-xxxxxxxx` placeholders, generated by the roster OCR when the member-list screen shows no UID. A pseudo match (NFKC, case-insensitive) may therefore **claim** a row whose UID is missing or a placeholder, writing the real UID onto it — which cleans the data on the way through. A pseudo already on file under a **different real** UID is refused, as is a pseudo matching two rows (`UNIQUE (guild, pseudo)` is case-sensitive, so it does not guarantee one) and a roster read that came back truncated. Those three are refused whether or not the gate is on, because the alternative was an `INSERT` colliding with a unique index and surfacing as a meaningless `db_error`.
  - **`pending` is now a real outcome**, not a visitor: a `[PENDING]` role if the server has one, else visitor, else nickname only — never the guild role, and never a nickname carrying the tag being refused. Refusals are audited as `discord_verification_refused`, and `logPlayerAction` gained optional `service`/`level` because until now **every** `discord_verification` row in production was filed as if the Player Portal had written it.
  - **The gate is opt-in per guild** (owner decision): a guild with no setting keeps the old behaviour. PSA and STW build their rosters through this flow today, so defaulting it on would have stopped their onboarding without asking them.
  - Gate: 0 TS errors, **164 suites / 2093 tests**, build clean, 38 e2e green.

- **The Player Portal told members a different number from their admin. One definition now.**
  - **Measured on ALPHA, same guild, same data, same day: the Portal said the guild attends 48.1%, the admin's Stats page said 31.6%.** The Portal's figure came from `getGuildAverages`, which counted attended **rows** over **all** rows of the guild — no session de-duplication, no week window, **Glory included** (it is 100% "participated" by construction, 6,038 rows) and rows **still awaiting approval** included. A number a guild leader and a guild member read differently is worse than no number, because it makes both of them wrong in the argument about it.
  - **The weighting now lives in `src/core/scoring/reliability.ts`**, imported by both screens. `core/` never imports `modules/`, which is what keeps one definition reachable from two modules. `tests/portal_agrees_with_stats.test.js` feeds the same buckets through both entry points and asserts the same rate comes out.
  - **New `gm_player_stats(p_guild, p_pseudo, p_weeks)`**, on exactly the S3 rules: `participated > 0 OR sub_present`, Glory and pending excluded, opportunities from the member's own roster week minus their excused. **It returns no per-member row for anyone but the caller and computes no rank**, so a player cannot read a peer's attendance through it; the comparison offered is against the guild. The guild side is a real pooled rate — attendances over the **sum of every member's own opportunities** — not `attendances / (sessions × roster)`, which would pretend everyone was on the roster for every session. `service_role` only: `member-portal` derives identity from the JWT and never accepts a caller-supplied uid.
  - **The four-card KPI grid has been dead code.** `state.kpis` was declared, initialised to `null` and **never assigned** — the response was destructured into a local and only `guild_averages` read from it — so every card behind `if (kpis && kpis.power)` was unreachable. Worse, its shape was stale anyway: it read `power.rank`, `power.percentile` and `glory.guild_max_week`, which the live `gm_personal_kpis` stopped returning long ago. Rebuilt around what the data supports: your reliability with its per-event breakdown and the gap to your guild, your power against the guild's real maximum, your best Glory week, and the rally grade.
  - **The rally grade was grading players against themselves.** With `kpis` always null, `maxGuildPower` fell back to `player.overall_power * 1.25` — a ceiling derived from the player's own power, which no amount of progress could move them up. It uses the guild's real maximum now.
  - **The Glory badge was wrong for 56% of players.** The badge path required more than one Glory week and then `.slice(1)`, discarding the **oldest**. Measured: **658 of the 1,686 players with any Glory have exactly one week**, so their best was 0 and they earned no Glory badge at all — one of them holding **369,393,189**, seven times the top threshold. A further **287** had their best week dropped for being their first. Nothing documented why, and nothing in the badge catalogue implies it.
  - **Two attendance figures sat centimetres apart on the same screen**; the stats strip's own "Attendance rate" pooled across event types, so a member's Arms Race days outweighed their wars. The counts stay, the rate is stated once.
  - **Honest limit, stated on the card**: a member cannot read `guild_config`, so the Portal weights with the published default coefficients. A guild that customised its weights will see a slightly different figure from its admin, and the card says so rather than passing the two off as identical.
  - The unbounded `event_participants` scan in `getGuildAverages` goes with it — it ran on three separate portal actions to count two things.
  - **The deploy hook crashed the first time it ever deployed a single function**: `mapfile` is a bash 4 builtin and macOS ships bash 3.2, which is what `#!/usr/bin/env bash` resolves to here. It failed closed and blocked the push — the right direction — but that branch had never run before, because the stdin field-order bug fixed in `d780276` made the hook return early on every push since it was written. Replaced with a portable `while read` loop, and `tests/deploy_hook.test.js` now rejects bash-4-isms.
  - Gate: 0 TS errors, **163 suites / 2027 tests**, build clean, **38 e2e** green.

- **Guild Cockpit: every bar on the tab was invisible, and two charts were drawing weeks that do not exist.** Reported from a screenshot within the hour.
  - **`.gm-kpi-bar` sets `height: 100%` and `.gm-kpi-bar-track` sets `height: 12px` and `flex: 1`. None of that applies to a non-replaced inline element**, and the rebuilt tab wrote both classes onto `<span>`. The tracks kept a height from the flex row and stayed visible; the coloured fill inside them had no box at all. Engagement, weekly attendance, power progression, power tiers and roles all rendered as empty grey rails.
  - **It type-checked, all 2,006 tests passed and all 38 e2e specs were green.** Nothing here has layout — jsdom does not compute it, and no assertion looked at the element type. Only the screenshot showed it. Fixed on both sides so neither alone has to be right: the CSS now declares `display: block` (it sets a height, so it cannot be left inline), and the markup uses `<div>` like the rest of the codebase. `tests/kpi_bars_render.test.js` guards both, sabotage-verified.
  - **Labels were being ellipsised.** `.gm-kpi-label` is a fixed 96px column with `text-overflow: ellipsis`, so "Occasional (under 30%)" rendered as "Occasional (u...". The thresholds moved to the card subtitle, where there is room, and the test caps label length at what the column holds.
  - **Phantom weeks.** 14 rows of 55,739 carry a `week_start` that is not a Monday, and `gm_stats_health` grouped on them raw — so ALPHA's attendance chart showed **16/09 and 15/09 as their own columns with 0 members present**, between the real weeks either side. It reads as "the guild did nothing that week" when the guild never had that week. Now folded into the Monday that contains them, matching what `gm_stats_snapshot_upsert` already did.
  - **The data-quality card announced a week that had not started.** It read "Last week 2026-09-28" on 2026-09-27, because events are populated ahead of time — **1,555 rows across 5 guilds sit in next week** — and `weeks_with_data` and `last_week` were the only two counters in that function not bounded to the current week. They are now, and the rows that ARE scheduled ahead get their own counter instead of being silently folded into recorded history.
  - Gate: 0 TS errors, **162 suites / 2012 tests**, build clean, **38 e2e** green.

- **Stats, phase S5: the Event Deep-Dive moves to SQL, and `gm_stats_data` is retired.**
  - `gm_stats_events(p_guild, p_bucket, p_weeks[])` resolves the bucket server-side and returns per-member counts, so the browser stops receiving the tenant's entire participation history in order to filter it down to one event. `p_bucket` is validated against a fixed set: it could neither inject nor widen the scan, but an unchecked value could quietly select nothing and render an empty table that reads as an absence of data rather than a bad argument.
  - **`computeEventLeaderboard` was the worst of the five attendance formulas**, and it is gone. It counted **rows** and divided by the member's **own row count**, so a member with exactly one row scored 100%. It also keyed Shadowfront by `session_id` where every other part of the page keys it by week, so a member in both squads of one week counted twice here and once everywhere else. The rate is now `att/opp` against the member's own opportunities — the same rule as the members board and the Health tab.
  - **A fourth inconsistency surfaced when the golden test moved**: the per-event path never filtered `is_pending`, while the global board always did. An **unapproved score of 999 was sitting at the top of the Event Deep-Dive and nowhere else**. The test now asserts the fix rather than the old ranking.
  - **The war record reaches the deep-dive**: opponent name and the six day results per session, above the table, for SvS and GvG.
  - **`gm_stats_data` is dropped.** It was the whole Stats data layer — four unbounded raw arrays, no week filter, no aggregation, four calls per visit with no sharing, ~10,000 rows per call for OMEGA. It also carried **`coalesce(v_user_guild, 'ALPHA')` twice**, live in production, so a super_admin whose guild did not resolve silently read ALPHA. Its four replacements carry no tenant fallback and all delegate authorization to `gm_can_read_guild_data`, which additionally enforces session validity (C1) and active status (M14) — neither of which `gm_stats_data` checked. The drop migration refuses to run unless all four replacements exist.
  - `keepOnlyPastOrCurrent` went with the last raw-row payload: every RPC now applies `week_start <= date_trunc('week', now())` at the source, so a week that has not started cannot reach the client to be filtered out again.
  - **The S1 guard fired twice more**, on `gm_stats_events` and on the drop itself — both applied to production before their migration files existed. Third and fourth catches for a test written for exactly this.
  - Gate: 0 TS errors, **161 suites / 2006 tests**, build clean, **38 e2e** green.

- **The Stats rework is complete.** Five phases, `V3.25.0` → `V3.29.0`: a broken week selector repaired, aggregation moved into Postgres, one attendance rule where there were five, a per-member denominator, and four cards built on data the database had been holding unread. One page, one definition, and every number carries the evidence for itself.

- **Stats, phase S4b: the past stops being rewritten.** Backend only — no client change, so no version bump.
  - **The defect.** Seven tables reference the roster with `FOREIGN KEY (guild, pseudo) REFERENCES guild_members ON DELETE CASCADE`. Removing a member erases their history, and **every historical guild aggregate moves with it**: last month's participation rate changes when you kick someone today. Nothing warned anyone; the number simply differed from the one they saw last week.
  - **`guild_weekly_snapshots`** freezes one row per guild per week — roster size, opportunities, members present, per-bucket counts, median power with its sample size, pending rows. A guild that ran nothing gets a row with `opportunities = 0`, which is data: it distinguishes a quiet week from a missing one. Cron every Monday at 03:20 UTC.
  - **The table is guild-grain and carries no member key, and that is the whole decision.** A member-grain snapshot would need the same `(guild, pseudo)` key to stay consistent and would then be destroyed by exactly the cascade it exists to survive — it would look like a fix and be none. Dropping the key from the existing tables was considered and **rejected**: `gm_erase_player` is built on `ON UPDATE CASCADE` propagating a pseudonymising rename across all seven children atomically, and that migration's own comments record that an earlier version was wrong because it fought the cascade. Erasure correctness outranks statistical convenience. **ADR-019** records this, because the next person will try to simplify it into one table.
  - **03:20 is load-bearing.** The DEMO tenant is rebuilt at 03:00; a job at 02:40 would photograph rows that reset is about to delete. It also runs before the empty-guild expiry at 03:30, so a guild about to be removed still gets its last week recorded.
  - **Two corrections caught by looking at the first output rather than trusting it.** The guild-grain `att` was counting member-attendances while `att` in `gm_stats_members` means distinct keys attended — the same name for two things — now `opp` / `attendances` / `members`. And `week_start` is normalised to the ISO Monday: 14 rows out of 55,739 carry a non-Monday date, which had invented six phantom weeks and **156 near-empty snapshot rows** across the estate. 468 rows became 312 clean ones.
  - **The backfill is not clean and says so.** Rows for weeks that had already passed are computed from *today's* roster: anyone removed before the backfill ran is already gone from `event_participants` and cannot appear, so the backfill reproduces the bias the table exists to stop. Those rows carry `source = 'backfill'`; only weeks written by the Monday job from now on are contemporaneous.
  - **What this does not fix, stated plainly**: member-level churn is still not measurable. That needs archiving departed members instead of deleting them, which touches all seven foreign keys, the GDPR erasure path and the OCR departure detection. A separate decision, not taken here.
  - **`tests/architecture_adr_consistency.test.js` was stale**: it hardcoded 14 ADRs and had not been updated since ADR-015, so four ADRs existed that no test looked at. It now enumerates the directory and checks each one is indexed and linked. A list that must be hand-edited to stay true is not a guard.
  - Gate: 0 TS errors, **161 suites / 2006 tests**, build clean, **38 e2e** green.

- **Stats, phase S4a: the Health tab shows what the database already knew.**
  - **`war_sessions` has carried win/loss per day since it was created, and the Stats page never read it.** Only `HistoryView` did, one session at a time. ALPHA reads **GvG 6W-0L, SvS 1W-5L** — and the per-day split is the actionable half: ALPHA loses the five preparation days and wins the battle day. Across production, 14 guilds have a record to show.
  - **`player_metrics_history` has carried a weekly power snapshot for six weeks and nothing plotted it.** ALPHA's median member power: 74.6M → 79.8M.
  - **The power series is a median, not a sum, and that is load-bearing.** A guild's first OCR week covers only part of the roster: **CLAW went from 11 players scanned to 114 in one week**. A sum would have drawn a tenfold jump that never happened. Every point carries the number of players it is based on, and the card says so.
  - **Engagement segments**, with thresholds calibrated against production rather than chosen for roundness: over 4-week windows across 2,403 members, weighted reliability has p25 = 0.0, median = 21.7, p75 = 48.0, p90 = 66.7. Cutting at **60 and 30** splits the population 18% core / 24% regular / 22% occasional / 36% dormant. At 80 the "core" would be 6% of a roster; at 50 it would be a third.
  - **Two retired cards were built on sand.** "Weekly participation rate" divided each historical week by **today's** roster, so every removal silently rewrote every past bar — the same defect the weekly snapshot exists to fix. "Members engaged per event type" counted a member who attended one Arms Race day out of eighty exactly like one who attended all eighty.
  - **A new card answers the question every other number depends on**: how completely does this guild record its events? Weeks recorded, last week, rows awaiting approval, and the metrics scanned for under 60% of the roster are **named and left off the cards above** rather than averaged over a tenth of the roster and presented as the guild. Without it, an admin reading 16% Arms Race attendance cannot tell "nobody plays it" from "nobody records it".
  - The cockpit's `gm_stats_data` call is gone; one remains, in the event deep-dive. `formatBigNum` went with it — a byte-level duplicate of `formatBigNumber` in `overview.service.ts`, both duplicating `formatCompactNumber`/`formatPower` in `core/utils/text.ts`.
  - **The S1 guard earned its place.** `gm_stats_health` was applied to production before its migration file existed, and `tests/rpc_names_exist.test.js` failed the build until the repository caught up. That is the exact drift it was written for, caught on its first real occasion.
  - Gate: 0 TS errors, **160 suites / 1992 tests**, build clean, **38 e2e** green.

- **Stats, phase S3: one attendance rule, a denominator per member, and a number that can be defended.**
  - **The problem, measured.** ALPHA, week of 2026-08-31: 7 Arms Race sessions, 3 DTR, 1 Shadowfront, 1 SvS, 1 GvG. Counting instances equally gave the Arms Race — daily, and structurally skipped, **16.2% participation across production** — **54%** of the score, and the SvS **7.7%**. Real rates ran from 9% to 57%, which is not a number anyone can act on.
  - **The replacement**: a rate per bucket, then `reliability = 100 × Σ coeff(b)·att(b) / Σ coeff(b)·opp(b)`, with the tenant's own coefficients. The same week now weights Arms Race at 27% and the SvS at 19%. **The components are columns**, not a hidden formula: `SvS 2/3 · GvG 2/2 · Shadowfront 3/3 · DTR 5/8 · Arms Race 12/24 → 69%`. "82%" is not an instruction; "attends every SvS, never once did Shadowfront" is.
  - **Five attendance formulas collapse to one**, and the measurement says it costs nothing: the discarded variant also counted "any recorded score" as attendance, and across all **49,701 non-Glory rows, exactly ZERO** carry a score without also being marked present. The clause could never add anyone — it only ever made two boards disagree about the same member. The two boards are now one: the "Global Score (Composite)" / "Attendance Rate (%)" dropdown is gone.
  - **The denominator is per member.** Opportunities count from the week a member appears on the roster, so a recruit is no longer marked absent for wars fought before they existed. Not hypothetical: over an 8-week window **YARR has 26 of 167 members who joined after it started**, SEN 13, EXPO 13, OMEGA 12, ALPHA 8. Honest caveat, recorded in the migration because it will be asked: `guild_members.created_at` is when the roster row was first scanned, not when the player joined — 117 to 149 members share only 6 to 11 distinct dates. It is the best evidence in the schema and strictly better than pretending everyone was always there.
  - **Excused leaves the denominator** instead of occupying a column. 0.41% of rows carry it, so it moves almost nothing — but it gives the flag a job, and the count still travels on the row so an admin can see why one denominator is smaller than a neighbour's.
  - **`—` instead of `0%`** for a bucket the guild did not run for that member. "Did not attend" and "was never asked" are different facts and the old page reported both as zero. A bucket with no opportunities contributes to neither side of the fraction, and a member with nothing at all sorts **last** rather than being mixed in at 0%.
  - **A trend**: the last 4 weeks with data against the 4 before them, shown only when both sides carry at least 8 weighted opportunities. Below that a single missed SvS reads as a 30-point collapse. A recruit with no earlier window shows nothing rather than a fabricated decline.
  - **How much this moves people**, measured on OMEGA over 4 weeks before shipping: average 9.1% → 9.6%, **32 members up, 30 down, largest single move ±11 points**. Order changes for some; nobody is transformed.
  - **A property worth knowing**: with a single bucket the coefficient cancels — it appears on both sides of the fraction. That is correct, because a coefficient expresses a trade-off *between* event types, and the regression test now asserts real values on a member with a mix (DTR attended, SvS missed: 29% at `coeff_dtr = 2`, 91% at 50) instead of merely "greater than".
  - Removed with the model they served: `computeWeightedLeaderboard`, `computeParticipationLeaderboard` and their two S2 aggregate twins, along with the S2 equivalence test, whose subject no longer exists. `tests/stats_reliability.test.js` replaces it with 13 assertions about the properties an admin would complain about if they broke.
  - Gate: 0 TS errors, **160 suites / 1990 tests**, build clean, **38 e2e** green.

- **Stats, phase S2: the leaderboard is aggregated in Postgres instead of in the browser.**
  - `gm_stats_data` ships four unbounded raw arrays and is called four times per visit with no sharing. For OMEGA that is about **10,000 rows per call**, with no week filter, and every count, sum and distinct done in JavaScript on `event_participants` — 55,739 rows growing ~3,000 a week.
  - New `gm_stats_members(p_guild, p_weeks[])` returns one object per roster member with per-bucket counters. OMEGA's **5,118 in-window rows become 142 member objects**. `p_weeks` is clamped to 26: an RPC argument is client input, so AGENTS.md rule 3 applies to it.
  - **The bucket expression is inlined, and that is a measured decision.** Calling `gm_event_scoring_key` per row costs **605 ms** on OMEGA; the same `CASE` inlined costs **48 ms**. A SQL function carrying a `SET` clause cannot be inlined by the planner, so `SET search_path = ''` — which every function here has, and should — turns each call into a real invocation, twice per row, with the date overload delegating to the text one for a third. Duplicating a definition is how this codebase got five divergent attendance formulas, so the copy is not silent: the migration's `DO $verify$` asserts the inlined `CASE` equals `gm_event_scoring_key` for **every distinct (event_name, session_id, week_start) triple in the live table**, and fails the replay if anyone edits one and not the other.
  - **`bool_or` replaces an order-dependent rule.** The row walk kept whichever attended row the database returned first for a scoring key, and used *its* score to decide the performance bonus — with no `ORDER BY` anywhere, that was undefined behaviour. Measured across all 55,739 rows: **20 keys have several attended rows, and in none of them does the score verdict differ**, so the two rules agree on every row that exists and only one of them is deterministic.
  - **The coefficients stay in TypeScript**, deliberately. `src/core/config/event-coeffs.ts` documents finding B1 — the same five numbers declared twice, drifting apart. Declaring them in SQL as well would recreate B1 across a language boundary where `tsc` cannot see it. **SQL counts; TypeScript weights.**
  - **`tests/stats_aggregate_equivalence.test.js` is the proof the numbers did not move**: one deliberately awkward fixture (two weeks, five buckets, an uppercase `SVS` that earns the coefficient but not the bonus, two Shadowfront squads in one week, a substitute, an excused row, a score-only row, and a member with no rows) fed through both implementations, compared field for field. The golden values in `tests/stats_view.test.js` are unchanged, which is the point.
  - **Caught by that comparison, and fixed before shipping**: returning Glory for all of history instead of the selected window silently widened the delta, turning a golden row from `-` / 65 pts into `+500` / 85 pts. Worth recording what it exposed — **at a 1-week period a delta needs two weeks and can only ever be zero**, so the "Δ Glory" column is structurally empty in the default view. S3 decides whether it earns its place.
  - **Two test fixtures modelled something the schema forbids**: a participant who is not on the roster. `event_participants` carries `FOREIGN KEY (guild, pseudo) REFERENCES guild_members`, and production has **zero orphan pseudos** in either `event_participants` or `shadowfront_squads`. The old code rebuilt its member list by unioning participant pseudos onto the roster, so the illegal fixtures still rendered. Corrected rather than worked around.
  - **`gm_leaderboard` is dropped.** It was ACTIVE, granted to `authenticated`, had **no source in any migration in this repository** and no caller — and resolved an unknown tenant with `coalesce(v_caller_guild, 'ALPHA')` in three places, so any authenticated caller whose guild did not resolve read ALPHA's leaderboard. Same class as the ghost Edge Functions in C3/C4. It could not be adopted without guessing at a body nobody reviewed, and could not apply per-tenant coefficients anyway.
  - **`maxPossible` removed.** `(6+4+20+15) * weeks * 5`, where the trailing 5 hardcoded "the highest coefficient is 5" — wrong for any tenant configuring a higher one, and moot either way: no template ever read it.
  - **Still on the old path, on purpose**: the cockpit and the event deep-dive. They move in S3/S4 together with their redesign, so nothing is ported twice and the page never has zero working tabs.
  - Gate: 0 TS errors, **160 suites / 1984 tests**, build clean, **38 e2e** green.

- **Stats, phase S1: the page had been lying about its own controls for a month.**
  - **The week selector called a function that does not exist.** `StatsView.ts:323` called `list_event_weeks`, which migration `20260926140000` dropped as a "legacy RPC superseded by a `gm_*` twin". **No twin was ever written.** Verified against production: neither `list_event_weeks` nor `gm_list_event_weeks` exists.
  - **It failed silently, twice over.** supabase-js answers a missing RPC with `{ data: null, error }` rather than throwing, and the call site never read `.error`, so `state.allWeeks` fell through to `[getWeekStart()]`. The dropdown has shown exactly one option ever since, and **the 2w / 4w / 8w period filters have all silently behaved as 1w**, because they slice a one-element array. ALPHA has **15 weeks** of history the page could not reach.
  - **The unit tests were green against the fiction**: all thirteen mocks defined `list_event_weeks` themselves, so the suite asserted the behaviour of a function that existed nowhere but in the mock.
  - New `gm_stats_weeks(p_guild)` returns `{ok, guild, weeks}`, so "not authorized" and "no weeks yet" stop being the same empty result. **Authorization is delegated to the existing `gm_can_read_guild_data`, not rewritten.** The first draft re-read `accounts.role` the way `gm_stats_data` does and was weaker in three ways that only reading the helper revealed: it ignored `gm_session_is_valid()` (finding C1), ignored `status = 'active'` (finding M14), and let a `server_admin` pass any guild instead of scoping them to their own server. It also carries **no `'ALPHA'` fallback** — `gm_stats_data` has two, which is ADR-003 invariant #1, and they are phase S2.
  - **`tests/rpc_names_exist.test.js` is the general fix**, and it is the test that would have caught this on the day it broke: every `.rpc('name')` in `src/`, and every RPC the unit tests mock, must have a `CREATE FUNCTION` in `supabase/migrations/`. It also names the migration that dropped a zombie. Sabotage-verified by restoring the old call: it fails and prints `list_event_weeks (dropped by 20260926140000_drop_dead_rpcs_and_diplomacy.sql)`.
  - **Eleven invisible buttons are gone.** They were rendered with `style="display:none"` under the comment *"Hidden aliases so all Vitest test battery expectations pass with 100% green"*, and they were not inert markup: `wireModeTabs` gave them a **second, complete switching implementation** that wrote `primaryView`, `currentMode` and `localStorage.gm_stats_mode` differently from the visible one. The four `kpi-*` aliases all rendered the same cockpit — four test names describing one screen. Replaced by `setView(view, mode)`, the single path the visible tabs also call and that the tests now drive.
  - **The Glory delta discarded most of its own data.** The loop ran `for (let j = 1; j < playerWeeks.length - 1; j++)`, dropping both the first transition and the last: with two scoring weeks the delta was always **0**, with three only the middle pair counted. Glory is the best-populated metric on the page (78.9% of its rows carry a score), so this was throwing away the strongest signal available.
  - **Removed as measured dead weight**: the `Late / Excused` column (`late` is set on **0.09%** of 55,739 rows, `excused` on **0.41%** — the cell printed "-" for the other 99.5%; both counters stay in the service, and `excused` earns a real job in S3 where it leaves a member's denominator), and the four `StatsService` methods with zero callers anywhere (`filterRecordsByPeriod`, `calculateDistinctAttendanceRate`, `calculateMemberAttendanceRate`, `generateCSVReport`) plus the two types that existed only to type them.
  - Gate: 0 TS errors, **159 suites / 1977 tests**, build clean, **38 e2e** green.

- **Glory OCR failed on a video and worked on screenshots. Two defects, one symptom, and the video only made the odds worse.**
  - **Measured before touching anything.** Over 24 hours in production: **147 OCR extractions completed, averaging 11.7s**, and **3 failed — at 30.4s, 32.7s and 34.0s**. Every failure was a *well-formed* Gemini envelope whose JSON text stopped mid-object at ~1,900 characters. A network cut cannot produce that; it is `finishReason: MAX_TOKENS`.
  - **~1,900 characters is about 600 tokens, against a configured ceiling of 8,192.** The thinking tokens come out of that same budget: the model deliberated for 30 seconds, spent ~7,600 tokens doing it, and had no room left to write the answer. The 33s duration is the tell, and it is why the tail failures all sit at 30s+.
  - **Defect 1 (server):** a truncated candidate was logged as **"Gemini OCR extraction succeeded"**, the model-fallback loop `break`s on it, and `JSON.parse` then threw four lines later. A 500, no retry, and the ~12 players the model *had* read thrown away. The function now checks `finishReason` **before** accepting the text and `continue`s to the next model. `thinking_config: { thinking_budget: 0 }` (OCR is transcription against a fixed schema at temperature 0.1 — there is nothing to reason about) and `max_output_tokens` raised 8192 → 32768. `usageMetadata` is logged on success, so the next investigation starts with numbers.
  - **`thinking_config` is sent per model.** Gemini 2.0 answers 400 rather than ignoring it and is in the fallback list, so `geminiSupportsThinking()` gates it, and a 400 retries the same model without it. An optimisation must not be able to take the feature down.
  - **Defect 2 (client), and the actual reason video looked broken:** one failing batch rejected `Promise.all(workers)`, so **every batch that had succeeded was discarded**. A screenshot is one batch; a video is ~18. At a 2% per-batch failure rate that is 2% versus **~30%** — the whole bug report is that ratio. Batch failures are now counted, not thrown: the surviving batches are kept and the user is told *"N of M batches failed"*. The results table already flags a partial scan and leaves missing members unchecked, so a partial roster still cannot silently mark anyone departed. Only a total failure aborts, and it now says "The AI returned an incomplete reading" instead of `json_parse_failed`.
  - **A test was holding the bug in place**, the M10 class: `tests/ocr.test.js` asserted the literal `max_output_tokens: 8192`, so raising it failed CI. It now asserts a floor instead of a constant.
  - Sabotage-verified: removing the per-batch `try/catch` fails three assertions by name.

- **The deploy hook has never deployed anything. It read git's stdin fields in the wrong order.**
  - Found by deploying the OCR fix above, pushing to `main`, and then **looking at what was actually running**: the deployed `ocr-guild-members` still imported the old two symbols from `_shared/gemini.ts`. The push had printed nothing at all.
  - git hands a `pre-push` hook one line per ref: **`<local-ref> <local-sha> <remote-ref> <remote-sha>`** — ref first, sha second. The loop read `local_sha local_ref remote_sha remote_ref`, so **every pair was swapped**. `$local_ref` held a 40-character SHA, the guard compared it with `"refs/heads/main"`, it never matched, and the hook `continue`d to the end of the loop and exited 0. **On every push, since it was written.**
  - **Two layers of silence, which is why it was never noticed.** Had the guard passed, `RANGE` would have been `"refs/heads/main..refs/heads/main"`-shaped nonsense, and that `git diff` failure is swallowed by `|| true` — leaving `CHANGED_FILES` empty and taking the "nothing to deploy" exit. A hook that fails by doing nothing quietly is indistinguishable from a hook with nothing to do.
  - **This is the mechanism behind audit finding H1**, where `ocr-guild-members` ran for days without the cache code that was sitting in the repository, and behind ADR-017's insistence that deployed and repository be reconciled by measurement.
  - Fixed both the field order and the guard, which now tests `$remote_ref` (where the commits land) rather than the local branch name, so a push from a worktree or a `HEAD:main` push counts. `tests/deploy_hook.test.js` pins both.
  - **`ocr-guild-members` was deployed by hand** in the meantime and verified in place: version 39, `verify_jwt` still true, and the deployed source contains `thinking_budget: 0`, `max_output_tokens: 32768` and the `finishReason` guard.
  - **The other 14 functions were checked for drift, not assumed.** Six show a source commit 1 to 3 minutes *after* their last deploy, which is the signature of deploy-then-commit rather than of drift. `admin-accounts`, the role provisioner and the most sensitive of them, was compared file by file: **all 12 files byte-identical** to the repository. A full 15-function reconciliation is Phase 0 of the audit plan and is still worth doing deliberately.

- **The "Register Player" tab is gone, and so is everything that only existed to serve it.**
  - It created a **password** account, which the D3 claim flow would then have had to migrate to Discord. Onboarding that manufactures its own debt: every account it made was one more to convert later.
  - **Measured before removing it, because the failure mode is locking people out.** 23 of the 26 guilds have a join code. The three that do not are `DEMO` (the nightly sandbox), `RRDR` and `DAWN`, which between them have **0 members and 0 portal accounts**. Nobody was stranded. Password *sign-in* is untouched: 165 accounts still depend on it.
  - Removed from `index.html`: the `auth-tab-register` segment, `register-form` and its four fields, `register-back-btn`, and `portal-go-register-btn` ("Create a player account") in the portal card.
  - **The segmented switcher went with it.** With Register Player and Request a Guild both gone it had one segment left, which is a label pretending to be a control.
  - Removed from the controller, because their markup no longer exists: `showAuthTab`, `showRegisterForm`, `onRegisterSubmit`, `onGuildRequestSubmit`, `REGISTER_ERRORS`, `GUILD_REQUEST_ERRORS`, and the eight `wireOnce` calls for ids that are no longer in the page. `showAuthTab` and `showRegisterForm` also came off the `GM_AUTH` bridge, where they had become the M8 class of dead export.
  - **`registerPlayer()` and `submitGuildRequest()` stay in `core/auth/session.ts` deliberately.** Their Edge Functions are still deployed; client and function come out together in phase D7, so that removing one half never leaves the other reachable.
  - `tests/login_page_discord_only.test.js` pins both halves: that the sign-up markup and handlers are absent, **and** that a new player still has a door (the Discord button, and `gm_discord_join_guild` with the join code and UID). Every assertion strips comments first, and the absence assertions were sabotage-verified by putting `register-form` back.
  - **One e2e test was passing vacuously.** `e2e/login.spec.js` clicked the register link "if (await registerLink.count())", so it went green the moment the link disappeared. Replaced with an absence assertion.
  - The **"Join our Discord"** button now points at `discord.gg/HJ47V6chbw`. A test asserts it is the only invite in the page, so a second stale one cannot creep back.
  - Gate: 0 TS errors, **157 suites / 1961 tests**, build clean, **38 e2e** green.

- **After a Discord sign-in the app opened on the wrong tab, and that tab was empty. One cause.**
  - Reported live: signing in landed on Accounts & Access instead of Overview, and the panel showed nothing until you switched away and came back.
  - **Both symptoms come from the same omission.** `handleDiscordCallback()` resolves `true`, and the boot path **returns before it reaches `restoreSavedTab()`** — deliberately, so that restoring a session cannot race a sign-in that is installing one. But nothing else activated a tab. The page therefore showed whichever panel carried `active` in the static markup, and because no tab activation had occurred, **`triggerTabDataLoad` never ran for it**. Switching tabs ran the loader, which is why coming back looked as though it had fixed itself.
  - `handleSuccessfulLogin` now activates Overview explicitly, after the dashboard is revealed (before it, the panel does not exist yet). `gotoItem` both activates the panel and calls `appOnTabActivated`, so the data loads on arrival.
  - **A reload still comes back where you were.** Only a fresh sign-in resets to Overview; `restoreSession` keeps calling `restoreSavedTab`, which is the behaviour worth keeping there. The test pins both halves so one cannot be "simplified" into the other.
  - Sabotage-tested: removing the activation fails the assertion by name.

- **Both legal documents redrafted in legal register, against the texts themselves**
  - Every authority below was read on Légifrance before it was relied on, not recalled. That is not ceremony: **the LCEN was substantially rewritten**, and Article 6 no longer carries the identification obligation at all.
  - **A question I had flagged for a lawyer is now answered, and it went the other way.** [Article L.611-1, 4° of the Consumer Code](https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000032224815) defines a contract for the supply of services as one *"in consideration of which the consumer undertakes to pay the price"*. **No price, no such contract, and therefore no consumer-mediation obligation** under Book VI. The old Terms promised recourse to "a designated consumer ombudsman" and named none: doubly wrong, since the obligation did not even attach. Article 4.3 now states the position with the citation, and undertakes to designate one should a court hold otherwise.
  - **The mentions légales were wrong about which article governs them.** The obligation moved to [LCEN Article 1-1](https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/loda/article_lc/LEGIARTI000049568614). Under **I**, a natural person publishing professionally must disclose **name, domicile and telephone number** plus a registration number. Under **II**, a person publishing **on a non-professional basis** may disclose the hosting providers alone, having given those particulars to them. The owner chose II, having been shown both. His home address and telephone stay private, and Article 1.1 now says which limb it relies on.
  - **The micro-entrepreneur and VAT mentions were removed as a consequence.** Invoking the non-professional limb while advertising a registered trading status undercuts the basis relied upon. They were also pointless: VAT has nothing to say about a service with no price.
  - **A statutory obligation neither document carried: the right of reply.** LCEN Article 1-1, III gives any person named on the service a right of reply, exercisable within three months, to be inserted within three days, on pain of a 3,750 € fine. It is now Article 1.4.
  - **The liability clause is written to survive rather than to sound strong.** A ToS is a contract of adhesion, and [Article 1171 of the Civil Code](https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000036829836) makes any non-negotiable clause creating a significant imbalance **deemed unwritten**. The preamble acknowledges that, and Article 1190 (ambiguity construed against the drafter), instead of pretending otherwise.
  - **The privacy notice now cites the article it relies on at every step**: Art. 4(7) for the controller, 37(1) for why no DPO is required, 6(1)(b) and (f) with the balancing at 4.1, 12(3) for the one-month deadline, 12(6) for identity verification, 17(3) and 17(1)(c) for the ban exception, 28(3) for the processor contracts, 46(2)(c) with **Implementing Decision (EU) 2021/914** and the 2023 adequacy decision for transfers, 32 and 33 for security and breaches, 22 for automated decisions, 8 for children, and **Article 82 of Law No. 78-17** for terminal storage. *Breyer* (C-582/14) is cited for the IP address.
  - **Article 14 is now addressed**, which the previous drafts missed: players recorded in a roster never provided their own data, so the notice obligation that applies to them is Article 14, not 13. Said explicitly at Article 2.
  - **Guild administrators are characterised as controllers in their own right** where they decide what to record about their members. That is what Article 4(7) makes them, and pretending the platform operator is the sole controller would have misallocated the obligations.
  - Nineteen unit assertions and two e2e specs pinned the previous plain-English wording. Each now matches the **article relied on** rather than a paraphrase, which is both more durable and closer to what actually has to be there.
  - Gate: 0 TS errors, **156 suites / 1951 tests**, build clean, **38 e2e** green.

- **The super_admin's recovery codes are reachable again**
  - The owner held **ten valid backup codes he could not spend**. Both halves of the mechanism existed and were correct: `auth-discord`'s `backup-login` action (Zod-validated, throttled, super_admin only, lockout-aware, consumes the code, mints a session) and `loginWithBackupCodeDiscord()` in `session.ts`, exported and bridged. **What was missing was any way to reach them.** The login page's old "Use a backup code instead" button called `openBackupCodeDialog()`, which is exported onto the `window.GM` bridge and never invoked. Removing it as dead markup made the gap visible; it did not create it.
  - A **"Lost access to Discord? Use a recovery code"** link now sits under the sign-in button, and it is actually wired. The dialog takes the identifier and a code, and lands in `handleSuccessfulLogin` like every other route in.
  - **It uses the Discord path, not the passkey one.** `loginWithBackupCode` goes through `auth-passkey`, which phase D7 removes; `loginWithBackupCodeDiscord` goes through `auth-discord` and survives it.
  - **It does not leak who exists.** A wrong code, an unknown account and an account that is not the super_admin all answer `invalid_code`. Distinguishing them would make an unauthenticated endpoint into an account-enumeration oracle. Asserted.
  - **It says how many codes are left**, and warns on the last one. Ten one-time codes with no low-water warning is how someone ends up locked out holding an empty envelope.
  - **Verified end to end against production**, not just rendered: the dialog opens visible, a wrong code returns "That identifier and code did not match.", the button re-enables and the dialog stays open. The deployed function answers `invalid_code` to a bad code for a real account id.
  - **Still missing, and worth deciding**: there is no way to *reissue* a set. The "Regenerate Backup Codes" button went with the My Passkeys card. The server action exists; only the entry point does not.
  - Gate: 0 TS errors, **156 suites / 1942 tests**, build clean, **38 e2e** green.

- **The Discord account chooser was invisible. That was my bug, and it explains the whole report.**
  - `.gm-modal-overlay` is `opacity: 0` and only appears once the `visible` class is added. `openAccountChooser` never added it. The dialog was **in the DOM, fully wired, and completely invisible**: clicking "Sign in with Discord" appeared to do nothing, and the continuation ticket expired two minutes later. `openLinkAccountDialog`, twenty lines below in the same file, does it correctly with `requestAnimationFrame(() => overlay.classList.add('visible'))`. I did not copy that line.
  - **The evidence was in the ticket table the whole time.** Five stranded tickets, all carrying the owner's real Discord username, each created and then expiring unconsumed. Those were **continuations**: proof that `redeem` had succeeded and returned `choose`, and that the client had been handed a working payload it then failed to show. Calling the deployed function with a diagnostic ticket confirmed it answers correctly, with both accounts and no session tokens.
  - **Why no test caught it**: every assertion about the chooser was that the markup existed. Markup existing and markup being visible are different claims, and only the second one matters. There is now a test for the `visible` class, sabotage-verified by removing the line again.
  - **Verified the way it should have been the first time**: minted a real ticket, loaded the real built bundle, and looked. The dialog renders "Which account?", "You signed in as qu4ck3n", and both rows — HawkEye (Command Center, every guild) and HawkEyePlayer (Player Portal, ALPHA). Measured too: overlay `opacity: 1`, card background solid, z-index 9999.
  - **A continuation ticket now lasts 15 minutes instead of 2.** Two minutes is right for the ticket that travels in the callback URL and is spent in under a second. It is far too short for one that waits on a person reading two options, or typing a password into the claim form. Both expired under the owner mid-decision. The callback ticket keeps its short life explicitly.
  - **The chooser can be dismissed.** It had no way out, which is a trap if you change your mind, and expiry is now named rather than reported as a generic failure.
  - Gate: 0 TS errors, **155 suites / 1933 tests**, build clean, **38 e2e** green.

- **"My Passkeys" removed, from both places it lived**
  - It let people **register passkeys that could not be used to sign in anywhere.** Both sign-in buttons called `wirePasskeyLoginButton()` and `openBackupCodeDialog()`, which are exported onto the `window.GM` bridge and were never invoked; they were removed earlier the same day as dead markup. Registering more only grew a pile of credentials nobody could spend.
  - **There were two identical cards**, not one: Accounts & Access (static markup in `index.html`) and the player portal's Security section (rendered by `PortalView`). The report named one; leaving the other would have been incoherent, so both went. Said here rather than left to be discovered.
  - **The wiring went with them**, not just the markup: `mountMyPasskeysCard()`, `loadMyPasskeysCard()`, the router's `my-passkeys` tab loader and the two bridge entries in `main.ts`. A loader left pointing at elements that no longer exist is indistinguishable from one that was never called, which is precisely the `maybeInitOcr()` defect this audit already found once.
  - **The 14 accounts holding a passkey are untouched.** Those rows are inert either way, since there is no sign-in path to spend them on.
  - **The server side is deliberately left alone**: `auth-passkey`, `account_passkeys` and `account_backup_codes` stay. Dismantling them is phase D7, gated on how many accounts have linked Discord (7 of 172 today).
  - **Still in the repo and now unreachable**: `src/modules/auth/PasskeysPanel.ts` and its test suite, plus four bridge references. Left in place on purpose, because that module is what you would remount if passkey sign-in is ever wired properly. Worth a decision rather than a silent deletion.
  - **A seventh suite caught by the same trap.** The cutover test asserted the card's absence over raw source, and failed on the comment that explains why it was removed, because the comment names the removed functions. Strip comments, then assert.
  - Gate: 0 TS errors, **155 suites / 1930 tests**, build clean, **38 e2e** green.

- **Player portal: the same two dead buttons removed, and the Discord changelog rewritten (v3.22.0)**
  - The portal's "Sign in with a Passkey" and "Use a backup code instead" were identical unwired copies of the ones on the login page. Both gone.
  - The e2e scan that checks for them is **scoped to `#login-view`**. A whole-document version failed on `add-passkey-btn` and `regen-backup-codes-btn` in the "My Passkeys" card on Accounts & Access, which **are** wired and are registration, not sign-in. Worth noting on its own: that card still lets people register passkeys that **cannot be used to sign in anywhere**, since both sign-in buttons were dead. 14 accounts have one registered.
  - **`DISCORD_CHANGELOG.md` rewritten.** It now leads with how Discord sign-in actually works, written separately for the three people who need it: a guild admin linking an existing account (and the warning that it deletes their password), a player joining with the guild join code and their UID, and someone starting a new guild with a tag and a server number. Each step was read off the running code rather than described from memory, including which code comes from where: the join code is permanent and lives on Accounts & Access, an invite code comes from `issue-admin-invite`, and a relink code is produced by unlinking an account.
  - It also covers the admin-and-player case, since that is now possible: link both accounts and pick at sign-in.
  - **Version bumped to 3.22.0.** Real user-facing changes shipped after 3.21.0 (the login page, multi-account Discord, both legal pages), and `version.json` is what tells an open tab to reload. The login page changed, so it matters that people come back.

- **Login page: Discord first, and two buttons that never did anything are gone**
  - **Discord is the primary route in.** The button sits above the identifier and password fields, in Discord's own colour, with a line under it saying where a new guild comes from. The credential form stays, demoted to a secondary route behind an "or use your identifier" divider. It is not removed: **7 accounts out of 172 have a Discord link**, so taking passwords away today would lock out 165 people, 60 of them guild admins. That removal is phase D7 and it is gated on exactly that number.
