  - **"Sign in with a Passkey" and "Use a backup code instead" were dead markup.** Not deprecated, not broken by a recent change: **never wired at all**. `wirePasskeyLoginButton()` and `openBackupCodeDialog()` are exported onto the `window.GM` bridge and never called, so both buttons looked like features and did nothing when clicked. That is finding M8's failure mode exactly, and it means **there is no passkey sign-in on this page today**, despite 14 accounts having registered one. Both removed rather than left to look functional.
  - **The same is true of the player portal's copies** (`portal-passkey-btn`, `portal-backup-code-btn`): also unwired, also inert. Left alone for now because that screen was not in scope, but they should go the same way.
  - **"Request a Guild" is gone from the login page.** It filed a request for a super_admin to approve by hand and then relay a generated password over Discord. Creating a guild now runs through Discord sign-in, which already asks for the **guild tag** and the **server number** and provisions the guild on the spot. The review screen stays for the 5 requests filed before today, with its description corrected to say so.
  - **"Want a guild space for your guild? Join our Discord" is now "Join our Discord".**
  - **Two CSS rules that looked applied and were not.** `.gm-login-discord-primary` lost to `.gm-login-discord-btn` on source order (both single-class), so the button stayed grey; it needs the compound selector. And swapping `gm-btn-primary` for `gm-btn-ghost` on the password button changed nothing, because `.gm-login-submit` hardcodes the primary fill. Caught by looking at the rendered page rather than at the stylesheet.
  - `e2e/guild-request.spec.js` is replaced by `e2e/login-discord-first.spec.js`, which asserts the new shape: Discord **above** the identifier field by measured position, the hint text, the absence of the two dead buttons, the renamed invite, and that the credential route still works.

- **Sidebar footer: the two legal links were running together on one line**
  - Reported from a screenshot: the sidebar read **"Terms & ConditionsPrivacy Policy"**, no space, no separator. `.gm-sidebar-legal-link` is an anchor and had no `display` rule, so it was inline. One link looked fine; adding a second put it flush against the first.
  - Now stacked, in the order asked for: **version, Privacy Policy, Terms & Conditions**, one per line. Verified against the real bundled CSS rather than by reading the rule, by rendering the actual footer markup in the browser and measuring: two distinct `top` values, `display: block`, correct order.
  - **Nothing in the suite could have caught this**, which is the point of the two assertions added to `tests/app_version.test.js`: one pins the order of the three elements, the other pins the `display: block` rule. Sabotage-tested by putting the links back inline.

- **Both legal pages rewritten: plain English, a free community app, and a binding open-source promise**
  - **Written like a person, on request.** No em dashes anywhere in either document (checked, zero), no "seamless", no "robust", no three-item flourishes. Short sentences, first person for the operator and second person for the reader, with the defined terms stated once in Section 1 so it stays a contract rather than a blog post.
  - **The Terms are a full rewrite, not an edit.** The old document was written for a paid SaaS and read like it: nine block-capital sections, "irrevocable acceptance", "exclusive territorial competence". It now covers who you are dealing with, what the tool is and is not, that it is free, that it is not the game publisher, conduct, whose data is whose, the code, what is promised about the service, what happens when something goes wrong, stopping, changes, and which law applies.
  - **Two real legal defects fixed, not just the tone.**
    - The old §9.3 assigned **"exclusive territorial competence"** to the Court of Appeal of Paris. Against a consumer that is an unfair term under French law and would not have bound anyone. Replaced with the ordinary rules, and an explicit statement that a consumer keeps the right to sue where they live.
    - The old §7.3 capped liability at "the total amount actually paid", a figure that is always zero and therefore not a cap at all. Now it says plainly that no cap computed on sums paid can apply, and lists what French law does not allow to be excluded (death or personal injury, *faute lourde*, *dol*).
  - **The promise, Section 7.** The owner chose both triggers. The source code becomes **Apache 2.0** on the earliest of: 2 August 2029 (the existing Business Source License change date, kept); the day he publicly announces he has stopped maintaining the tool; or the day **twelve consecutive months** pass with no deployment and no reply from the contact address. The third trigger is the point of the whole clause: a promise that requires him to be present is worth little at exactly the moment a guild would need it. Stated as irrevocable, with no reserved right of withdrawal.
  - **Mirrored in `LICENSE`, which is what makes it operative.** A promise in the Terms that the licence file does not grant is two documents that can be read against each other. The BSL parameter block now carries an "Accelerated Change Date" section with the same two triggers, in the same words.
  - **The Privacy Policy says the same things, better.** Same obligations, same legal bases, same retention periods, same processors. What changed is that it reads like someone explaining their own service: "Yes, this is personal data", "Please do not put anything private in them", "I would rather fix it than have you go elsewhere". The ban-record exception is still disclosed rather than hidden, and still says he will look at an objection properly and give a reason.
  - **Thirteen tests and two e2e specs were pinning the old prose.** Each was updated to assert the *property* rather than the wording: that the retention period is 12 months, not that it is written with a dash; that the payment statement exists, not that it is Section 4. One exact icon count became a floor, since an exact figure only ever measures how recently someone edited the page. New assertions were added for the two defects above and for the open-source undertaking in both files.
  - **Still to do, and it is the owner's call**: have a lawyer read both pages, in particular whether consumer mediation under Article L.612-1 of the Consumer Code applies here given the micro-entrepreneur status, in which case a mediator has to be named. The page currently says a consumer keeps that right where the Code gives it, which is accurate but stops short of designating one.
  - Gate: 0 TS errors, **155 suites / 1928 tests**, build clean, **35 e2e** green.

- **Discord sign-in: one identity, several accounts, and a choice (reported live)**
  - **Reported**: signing in with Discord as HawkEye landed on the Player Portal instead of the Command Center. **Nothing was broken.** The Discord identity was linked to `HawkEyePlayer` (role `member`), so a member session was minted and the routing was correct. The gap was structural: `discord_account_links` had `discord_user_id` as its **primary key**, so one person who is both an administrator and a player could only ever attach one of their accounts. And there is no admin-to-portal route in the UI, so no administrator could reach the portal by any path.
  - **The key now covers the pair**, `(discord_user_id, account_id)`. `redeem` answers `state: "choose"` with a continuation ticket and the list of accounts; a new `choose` action spends it.
  - **What was deliberately NOT relaxed**: `UNIQUE (account_id)` is added, so an account still belongs to exactly one Discord identity. Without it two people could both sign in as the same administrator.
  - **The cost was stated before the change, and then measured.** The Discord migration plan leaned on that primary key as an implicit "one guild per Discord account". It turns out **not** to be load-bearing there: `gm_discord_create_guild`, `gm_discord_join_guild`, `gm_claim_account_with_discord` and `gm_redeem_admin_invite` each refuse when the identity already holds **any** link, which is an explicit check untouched by this change. The migration asserts all four still have it, and aborts if one is ever lost.
  - **One function would have broken silently, and that is why this is a migration and not an ALTER.** `gm_redeem_relink_code` did `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (discord_user_id) DO UPDATE`. That clause names a column that is no longer a unique constraint, so it **raises** — relinking would have started failing for everyone with nothing in the change to hint at it. Rewritten, and the migration asserts the clause is gone.
  - **`.maybeSingle()` would have made it worse.** It *errors* on more than one row rather than picking one, so the very first multi-account sign-in would have failed outright. The lookup reads every row now.
  - **The chooser is not an oracle.** Picking spends the continuation ticket, and the account id is re-verified against that identity's links server-side — naming someone else's account answers `account_not_linked`, exactly as naming one that does not exist. Status is re-checked at selection time, not only at listing time, because an account can be suspended in between. Only active accounts are ever listed, and the buttons disable on the first click since the ticket is single-use.
  - **The dialog says where each account leads** ("Command Center, every guild" / "Player Portal, ALPHA") rather than showing two ids and leaving the choice to memory.
  - **Applied to production**: both links now exist for the owner's Discord identity, and `auth-discord` is deployed. Its `verify_jwt = false` is unchanged, as declared in `config.toml`.
  - **Still open, and worth deciding separately**: attaching a second account requires an **admin-issued relink code**. There is no self-service path, which is deliberate, but it means no other admin-who-is-also-a-player gets this until a code is issued to them.
  - Gate: 0 TS errors, **155 suites / 1925 tests**, build clean, 35 e2e green.

## [3.21.0] - 2026-09-27 — audit remediation (phases 1-9), **deployed**

> **Shipped to production on 2026-09-27** and verified there, not assumed: `/sw.js` now returns the self-unregistering tombstone with zero push handlers, the CSP is down to `script-src 'self'` with no `*.supabase.co` wildcard, `privacy.html`/`terms.html`/`legal.css` all serve 200, and the app boots with **zero CSP violations**. The 15 Edge Functions were already current with this branch — verified behaviourally, by probing the phase-4 CORS fix from the bypass origin — so none were redeployed and all seven `verify_jwt = true` functions stayed gated.
>
> **Version bumped from 3.20.2 to 3.21.0 deliberately.** The first push shipped under the *previous* release's version string, so `version.json` was unchanged and no long-lived tab would ever have been prompted to reload. This release in particular needs clients to come back: a tab that never reloads keeps the old CSP and never fetches the new `/sw.js`, so the service worker on that device is never retired.

- **HawkEye is now `accounts.guild = 'ALPHA'` — owner decision, applied to production (data change, no code change)**
  - The super_admin is also the admin of guild ALPHA on server 1058, and the account row now says so. **`server_number` is deliberately left NULL**: `gm_admin_list()` derives it with `COALESCE(accounts.server_number, guilds.server_number)`, so ALPHA's own number (1058) is used and there is no second copy to drift if ALPHA ever changes server.
  - **The risk was never the database, it was the client.** The tenant switcher renders only on `isSuperAdmin && !restriction`, so if anything had set a restriction from the account row, the platform owner would have been pinned to one guild — **silently**, with no error and nothing failing. Checked before applying: both the login path and the session-restore path key their `accounts.guild` lookup on `role === 'guild_admin'`, the server lookup on `server_admin`, and everything else falls through to a branch that explicitly nulls both. `ensureGuildRestriction()` returns early for a super_admin, before it reads the table.
  - **Measured before and after, identically.** Still `super_admin`; still reads and writes a *different* tenant (`OMEGA`); still sees all 26 guilds and 3,005 members; session still valid. `accounts` has **no triggers at all**, so no `session_version` bump and no forced sign-out.
  - **One expectation of mine was wrong and is worth stating**: I labelled a check `is_server_admin_still_false` and it returned **true**. Not a regression — the function keys on `role IN ('super_admin','server_admin')` and ignores `guild` entirely, so it was true before the change as well. The label was my error, not a behaviour change.
  - **Side effect, and a welcome one**: the "Unassigned Server" bucket on the Super Admin accounts screen is now **empty** — HawkEye was the last account in it. 172 accounts listed across 23 real servers.
  - **`tests/super_admin_not_scoped_by_guild.test.js`** pins the invariant that makes this safe, because "we only read `accounts.guild` for guild admins" is precisely what a later refactor simplifies away. Sabotage-tested: removing the `guild_admin` gate fails it by name. Its ordering assertion also had to be corrected — a whole-file search matched `normalizeRole`'s `if (s === 'guild_admin') return 'guild_admin'`, a pure string mapping, and compared against the wrong position.
  - Gate: 0 TS errors, **153 suites / 1896 tests**, build clean, **35 e2e** green.

- **Phase 9 — the documentation was confidently wrong, and now has a test (unreleased)**
  - **What the docs claimed, against what the system is.** Each of these was carried forward unchecked, and each would have misled the next reader:
    - `ARCHITECTURE.md`: **13 guilds / ~1,540 members / ~33k participations**. Live: **26 / ~3,005 / ~55,760** — wrong by roughly a factor of two. Also **&ldquo;45 table definitions / 42 persistent&rdquo;** against a real **38**, because it counted `CREATE TABLE` statements in the migration chain, which also contains the drops.
    - It asserted in a warning box that **`gm-revolut-webhook` did not exist** and told readers to keep it out of deployment matrices. It existed, ACTIVE, `verify_jwt = false`, with no source in this repository. Corrected in Phase 1; ADR-017 now records the rule that would have prevented it.
    - `README.md` sold **&ldquo;plans from one month to lifetime, purchased securely in the app&rdquo;** — and the price list it described belonged to **Revolut**, the flow removed before Stripe. It also advertised opt-in push notifications, and omitted **six shipped modules**: `migration` (1,465 lines, the largest feature in the app), `ocr`, `tickets`, `audit`, `badges`, `help`.
    - `AGENTS.md` required a gate of **&ldquo;712+ tests&rdquo;** against a suite past 1,800.
  - **Reconciliation that came out clean, and is worth stating**: **15 Edge Functions deployed, 15 in the repository, zero drift in either direction.** `gm-revolut-webhook` and `webauthn-spike` — the two that were live with no source — are gone, as are the three payment functions. `admin-accounts` is now genuinely `verify_jwt = true`, matching `config.toml` (finding M1).
  - **Four new ADRs**, each recording an invariant that was learned the hard way rather than decided in the abstract: **ADR-015** permanently free (and why `is_subscription_active()` deliberately survives as `SELECT true`), **ADR-016** the PWA/push retirement *and the two-release ordering that makes it safe*, **ADR-017** every deployed Edge Function must have versioned source, **ADR-018** GDPR inventory, retention and erasure — including that erasure must be an `UPDATE`, because the seven `ON UPDATE CASCADE ON DELETE CASCADE` children make a delete-then-anonymise ordering silently anonymise nothing.
  - **ADR-012 gets an addendum rather than a quiet edit.** It described an OCR cache that was documented, unit-tested, and consuming the Gemini quota it was meant to save **while absent from the deployed binary** — for the plainest of reasons: the function was last deployed two days *before* the migration that created the table it reads. An ADR that silently becomes fiction is worse than no ADR, so the failure is recorded in the ADR itself.
  - **The five agent skills were rewritten against the current system.** They pointed at `src/core/store/store.ts` (deleted in Phase 3), described a three-role model, required `220+ tests`, and — in the architect skill — **enumerated 13 tenant ids by name in the same paragraph that forbids hardcoding a tenant**. There are 26, and the list was stale the day a guild was created. `.kilo/agents/` (11 definitions nothing read) is deleted.
  - **A precise count in a document is the next stale fact**, so the gate commands are now stated without one. The numbers that remain — the volumetrics — carry an explicit measurement date and an instruction to re-measure rather than adjust.
  - **The CHANGELOG's unversioned tail is marked, not fabricated.** The plan called for retro-assigning versions from 3.17.2 to 3.20.2; measurement says those releases are **already documented**, and what is actually unversioned is the older history *below* 3.17.2, where one section spans several versions. Inventing a mapping would put fabricated release history into the file this project uses as its memory, so the boundary is labelled instead and the reason recorded.
  - **`tests/docs_accuracy.test.js`** makes the enforceable half of this permanent: the Edge Function count must equal the directory count, every documented function must exist, no dropped table may be listed as live inventory, no removed feature may be re-advertised, the agent instructions may not hardcode a test count or enumerate tenants, and the four ADRs must exist and be indexed. Sabotage-tested: drifting the count, re-adding a subscriptions section, and renaming a documented function each fail it by name. The first version of the dropped-table check also failed **correctly-written prose** that named a dropped table while saying it was dropped — scoped to the inventory bullets now.
  - **What no test can cover is stated as a runbook step**, not pretended: nothing in CI can see the platform, so deployed-vs-repo reconciliation lives in ADR-017 and the deploy runbook. A green CI is not a reconciled platform.
  - Gate: 0 TS errors, **152 suites / 1891 tests**, build clean, **35 e2e** green.

- **Phase 8 — GDPR: the compliance page was making claims the database contradicted (unreleased)**
  - **The real finding was not the missing privacy policy.** It was that `terms.html`, a published legally binding document, stated two things that measurement disproved.
    - **&ldquo;Zero Personally Identifiable Information ... Stored data is limited to publicly visible in-game pseudonyms, UTC timezone offsets, aggregate game statistical values, and isolated synthetic authentication tokens.&rdquo;** The live column catalogue also holds **sign-in IP addresses**, **Discord user ids and usernames across six tables**, Discord handles on guild requests, in-game player UIDs, bcrypt hashes, reversibly-encrypted passwords, and free-text ticket descriptions. An online identifier is personal data under Art. 4(1), and so is an IP address. The claim is withdrawn, and Section 6.1 now enumerates what is and is not collected.
    - **&ldquo;all corresponding foreign-key records, participation histories, and assigned rosters are permanently purged via automated database cascades (`ON DELETE CASCADE`)&rdquo;.** Only five auth-adjacent tables cascaded from `accounts`; the two wiki tables were `NO ACTION` and would have **blocked** the delete outright — which is presumably why no account-deletion path was ever written. And participation and roster records have no foreign key to `accounts` at all. A false statement in a published legal document is worse than a missing one.
  - **`privacy.html`** — a real GDPR notice: controller, the exhaustive data inventory, purposes with their Art. 6(1) legal bases, retention periods, processors, transfers, rights and how to exercise them, the CNIL, breach notification, children. Every factual statement was read off the running system, not described from memory.
  - **Retention now exists and is enforced by a scheduled job**, where before only the 24-hour throttle purges did. 12 months for the audit trail and the pseudonym history, 90 days for a closed guild request's Discord handle. **It also closes a gap the audit missed**: `gm_cleanup_expired_ocr_cache()` existed and had been revoked from `anon`, but **no cron ever called it** — expired transcribed player names simply accumulated. Measured first, then asserted in the migration: every window deletes **zero rows** on its first run, and the migration aborts rather than go live by destroying a backlog nobody looked at. Verified by running it: all four counters zero.
  - **Erasure exists — and my first implementation was wrong, which testing on DEMO is what revealed.** It deleted the roster row and then anonymised the statistics, and the anonymisation matched **zero rows every time**. Seven tables declare `FOREIGN KEY (guild, pseudo) REFERENCES guild_members(guild, pseudo) ON UPDATE CASCADE ON DELETE CASCADE`, so the delete had already cascaded them away — and the function still reported success. **The schema was describing the right design**: `ON UPDATE CASCADE` means renaming the pseudonym on the roster row propagates it to all seven children atomically. Erasure is an UPDATE, not a DELETE. Re-tested: roster size unchanged, old name gone, uid and timezone cleared, and **40 participations, 5 metrics and 5 weekly scores carried to the token** with the statistics intact.
  - **Ban records are deliberately retained**, identifier included — a ban list cannot stop a specific player rejoining without it (Art. 17(3)). That is **disclosed in §6.5** rather than applied as a silent carve-out.
  - **Account deletion was impossible before this and nobody had noticed**, because the two wiki foreign keys were `NO ACTION`. They are `SET NULL` now, with the author pseudonym overwritten separately — the FK is not the identifying part, the name beside it is.
  - **Sentry is live in production, and checking the local build would have concluded the opposite.** `SENTRY_DSN` falls back to `''` and `initSentry()` returns false without one, so a local `npm run build` contains no Sentry host at all. The **deployed** bundle carries an `ingest.de.sentry.io` DSN injected at build time. It is a real processor and is disclosed as one, on EU infrastructure.
  - **No consent banner is required, and the reason is verifiable rather than assumed**: zero `document.cookie` in `src/`, no analytics, tag manager or pixel anywhere, and browser storage limited to session and display state — strictly necessary, so exempt under Art. 5(3) of Directive 2002/58/EC. The page states the exemption it relies on.
  - **The processor list is enforced by the CSP.** `connect-src` in `vercel.json` is the authoritative list of third parties the browser may reach, so the test maps each host to a disclosed name and fails on any that is missing. Sabotage-tested: adding `api.acme-tracker.example` to `connect-src` fails it by name, and removing &ldquo;Sentry&rdquo; from the page fails it for both Sentry hosts. Gemini and Render are disclosed too — no CSP can reveal a server-side call.
  - **`legal.css`**: the terms page's 270-line inline `<style>` block became a shared root stylesheet rather than being duplicated into a second page. Still not a Vite entry point — finding H3 records why that would inject the whole app bundle into a static legal document. Three phase-5 assertions were updated to follow it rather than deleted, since the property they pin is unchanged.
  - **Two things caught before they shipped**, by checking rather than assuming: `pgcrypto` lives in `extensions`, not `public`, so `public.digest(...)` under `SET search_path = ''` would have failed at call time and never at create time; and `guild_transfers` has no `guild` column at all — a transfer spans two tenants, so it keys on `source_guild`/`target_guild`. Also: my applied migrations initially omitted `NOTIFY pgrst, 'reload schema'`, which would have left the new RPCs returning **404 from the API while existing in the database**. Corrected, and the repo convention now covers the file.
  - **Nine heading icons on the new page were paths I wrote from memory and drew half-shapes** — the same defect class as the 42 blank icons found in phase 6. Replaced with real glyphs extracted from the installed `@phosphor-icons/web` package.
  - **DEMO was used as the erasure sandbox**, as designed: two DEMO players were erased during testing. `gm_reset_demo_tenant_data()` rebuilds that tenant nightly at 03:00. No other tenant was touched, and both erasure RPCs were confirmed to refuse an unauthenticated caller before any data was written.
  - Gate: 0 TS errors, **151 suites / 1871 tests**, build clean, **35 e2e** green. `anon` cannot call any of the three new functions; the retention job is not callable by `authenticated` either.

- **Phase 2 — the PWA and Web Push are retired; the last step is a DEPLOYMENT, not a code change (unreleased)**
  - **Most of phase 2 was already done, and the database side is now verified clean against production.** `push_subscriptions`, `player_push_prefs`, `save_push_subscription`, `gm_get_push_prefs` and `gm_set_push_prefs` are all dropped; no surviving function body and no cron command names any of them; `database.generated.ts` is regenerated; no Edge Function reads a VAPID key. The manifest, `PushService`, `pwa.ts`, the five install-only PNGs (612 KB) and the push payload module are gone from the repo.
  - **That closes C2 completely, and the advisor agrees.** `save_push_subscription` was the only `anon`-granted function that performed a privileged write, with `coalesce(v_guild, 'ALPHA')` sending unauthenticated subscriptions into a real tenant. Anon-executable `SECURITY DEFINER` functions are down from **four to one** — `gm_report_client_error`, which is intentional (ADR-011). `push_subscriptions` has also left the RLS-enabled-no-policy list.
  - **Step 2 — deleting `src/sw/` — must NOT be done yet, and this is measured, not assumed.** The tombstone has never been released: `ae702d6` is not an ancestor of `main`, and production still serves the **old push worker**:
    ```
    curl -sS https://guildmanagement.vercel.app/sw.js | grep -c unregister   →  0
    ```
    It still installs `push` and `notificationclick` handlers and still points its notification icon at `/icon-192.png`, a file the repo no longer contains. Deleting the source would 404 `/sw.js` and leave that worker running on every member's device **forever** — a worker outlives the page that registered it, and goes away only when the browser can fetch a new `/sw.js` from that exact URL and watch it unregister.
  - **The kill switch is verified ready.** The built tombstone is 456 bytes, differs from the live worker by hash, calls `registration.unregister()` once, and installs neither a `push` nor a `notificationclick` nor a `fetch` handler. The deployed `/sw.js` is served `max-age=0, must-revalidate`, so the browser **will** re-fetch it and see the changed bytes.
  - **Three dependencies that make the tombstone reach anybody were unpinned, and now are.**
    - **The trio is all-or-nothing**: the source, the esbuild step in `scripts/build.js`, and the dev plugin in `vite.config.ts`. Half-deleting them is silent — `/sw.js` starts 404ing, no test notices, and the old worker becomes unreachable and immortal.
    - **`/sw.js` must be served revalidating.** Nobody had checked this. Serve it `immutable` like every other asset and the cache keeps handing back the old worker; the kill switch never arrives and nothing fails loudly.
    - **The offline copy must not promise a sync** (H6), backed by an assertion that nothing in `src/` could implement one — no IndexedDB, no `caches.open`, no `sync`/`periodicsync` handler.
  - **I had to sabotage this test twice before it was worth anything.** The first version searched `build.js` for the path `src/sw/service-worker.ts` — and passed its own sabotage, because the file's **comments** name that path while explaining the tombstone. The second version stripped comments and still passed, because a `console.log` **string** names it too. Only asserting the entry point's own assignment plus the `entryPoints: [swEntry]` call catches all four half-deletions. Verified: repointing the entry, deleting the esbuild block, deleting the source, and breaking the dev plugin each fail it by name.
  - **`vapid_private_key` deliberately STAYS in the log scrubber's denylist**, and that is now pinned as intentional. A stale entry in a redaction list is free and can only ever mask something; pruning it alongside the feature is a cleanup whose sole possible effect is to make logs less safe.
  - **Owner actions still outstanding**: deploy release *n* (this branch) and let one full cycle pass before phase 2 step 2; purge the VAPID secrets from the vault; and enable leaked-password protection in Supabase Auth, which the advisor still reports off.
  - Gate: 0 TS errors, **150 suites / 1838 tests**, build clean, **31 e2e** green.

- **M7 — the state store moved into `core/`, and became the single write path (unreleased)**
  - **This unblocks what phase 3 recorded as blocked.** The store sat in `modules/app/state/`, so `core/auth/roles.ts` could not use it and read the raw `window` globals itself — which is how the store and the actual active tenant could disagree. The module **imports nothing at all**, so moving it to `src/core/state/app-state.ts` is legal by construction, and a test asserts that it still imports nothing rather than trusting it to stay that way.
  - **`roles.ts` now resolves the tenant through the store.** `getActiveGuild()`, `canWriteGuild()` and `guildRestrictionFor()` read and write one place. `guildsData` is typed as `Record<string, { server_number?: string | null }>` instead of `unknown`, which lets `canWriteGuild()` compare server numbers without a cast.
  - **Two real bugs in the mirror, found by writing the test rather than by reading the code.**
    - **A read used to write.** `getAppState()` called `ensureWindowMirror()`, so merely asking for the active guild **created `window.GM_COLLAPSED_ROLES`**. Anything that then wrote into that object was writing into a mirror the store would overwrite on its next patch. The mirror is established once at boot and refreshed on every write; reads are pure.
    - **The mirror was sticky.** `ensureWindowMirror()` ran *before* `hydrateFromWindow()`, so deleting a global was undone — the stale in-memory value was written back and read as if nothing had been cleared. Hydration is absence-aware now: a deleted global clears the field.
  - **Every direct writer is converted**: `ShellView`'s two setters, `members.render`'s view mode, `bootstrap.ts`'s tenant seed, and `accounts.controller`'s guild map. `ShellView`'s four readers go through the store too, so neither direction bypasses it. `gm-bridge.ts` installs before `main.ts` calls `initAppState()` and consumers read `window.guildsList` in between, so it now calls `initAppState()` — idempotent, and it establishes every key rather than patching one global by hand.
  - **The single-write-path check is a source scan, and it is verified to bite.** A stray `window.currentGuild = …` is invisible to `tsc` and to every behavioural test: it simply wins, silently. So the test scans `src/` for an assignment to any of the nine state keys outside the store, with a guard-the-guard assertion that at least five modules do write through `patchAppState`. Sabotage-tested: restoring one direct assignment in `ShellView` fails the scan by name.
  - **Making the store fully authoritative — dropping hydrate-on-read — is NOT done, and the cost is why.** 67 places across `tests/` and `e2e/` seed state by assigning `window.*` directly. That is a deliberate affordance, not an accident, and rewriting all of them is a separate decision rather than the tail of a move.
  - Gate: 0 TS errors, **149 suites / 1825 tests**, build clean, **31 e2e** green. Coverage floor ratcheted to 57 / 46 / 64 / 61.

- **Reported bug — every admin under "Unassigned Server" (unreleased)**
  - **The data was never wrong.** All 26 guilds carry a `server_number`, all 61 `guild_admin` rows carry a guild, and the only NULL guild is the super_admin's — which is why that one account correctly reads "All Guilds". Checked against production before changing anything, including that the phase-4 RLS work was not the cause: `guilds` reads back 26 rows for the super_admin with a current token, with a legacy token, and for a `guild_admin`.
  - **Two pre-existing causes compounded.** `gm_admin_list()` — the RPC behind the accounts screen — never returned `server_number`, so `acc.server_number` was `undefined` for every row. Its fallback reads `window.guildsData`, which only `fetchGuilds()` fills, and **no loader for the accounts or Super Admin tabs called it**. Reaching those tabs without having passed through some other path that happened to fetch guilds left the map empty, so every admin fell into the "Unassigned Server" bucket and the "Create Admin Account" guild dropdown was empty.
  - **Fixed at the source**: `gm_admin_list()` now returns `COALESCE(accounts.server_number, guilds.server_number)` — the same derivation `validateCallerAuth` and `check_user_guild_access` already use — so no entry point has to reconstruct it from a separately fetched map. 43 of 61 `guild_admin` rows have no number of their own, which is normal: theirs comes from their guild. `fetchGuilds()` is also wired into both admin tab loaders, which is what refills the guild dropdown.
  - **It also repaired a filter that had been silently inert**: `admin-accounts` narrows the list for a `server_admin` with `acc.server_number === info.serverNumber`, against a field the RPC did not return — so that half of the condition was always false and the filter rested entirely on its guild-based branch.
  - Verified through the deployed function: the list now carries `server_number`, no account with a guild lacks one, and the grouping resolves to 23 real servers (Server #1058 = ALPHA, IMK, OMEGA) plus the super_admin's own group.

- **Phase 3 — dead code: most of it was already gone, and two of the targets were alive (unreleased)**
  - **Already done before this phase started**, verified rather than assumed: `store.ts` and the dead `appStore`, `pwa.ts`, the four orphan UI components (`Checkbox`/`Input`/`Switch`/`Table`), the divergent `escapeHTML` copies — `StatsView`'s local `esc` now delegates to the single boundary — and the unreachable `getSupabaseConfig`/`validateSupabaseEnv` paths. In the database: all eight removed-feature RPCs (Leader Hall, the three matchups, cross-guild ranking, the three diplomatic-treaty functions), `list_event_sessions`, and the `diplomatic_treaties` table itself. The trigger and maintenance functions are already revoked from `anon` and `authenticated`.
  - **`transfer_guild_member` dropped.** It really was a wrapper: one SQL statement delegating to `gm_transfer_guild_member`, identical signature, no check of its own. Its caller now names the canonical function.
  - **`request_guild_transfer` is NOT a duplicate, and dropping it would have broken the player portal.** The plan listed it beside the other legacy names. They authorise different people: `gm_admin_request_transfer` rejects `role = 'member'` outright, while `request_guild_transfer` also authorises **the player themselves** (`a.uid = p_uid OR a.id = p_uid`) — which is exactly what `member-portal` calls when a member asks to change guild. Repointing the portal at the admin function would have made every player request fail on the member guard. A `COMMENT` on the function now records this so the next cleanup does not try again.
  - **The build's minification loop was dead, and provably so.** Both branches were unreachable: the five root stylesheets are excluded because Vite bundles them through `main.ts`, and the only root `.js` files are the two test configs, excluded for the same reason — so `minifiedCount` was always 0 and the "Minified N JS/CSS files" summary never printed. All the loop has ever done since the cutover is copy three files: `favicon.ico`, `favicon.png`, `terms.html`. Removing the branches was verified the only way that means anything: **`dist/` comes out with the same 11 files at identical byte sizes.** A test block asserted that minification pipeline by reading source text for code that never ran; it now asserts what the loop does.
  - **Six comments in `main.ts` were correct history and one was an active lie**: *"NOT auto-initialized while app.js still wires its own listeners"*, in the present tense, about a file that has not existed for months — while `initAuthForms()`/`initTabNavigation()` are called at the bottom of the same file. Two more still credited "ShellView's lazy registry", which phase 7 removed. Rewritten to say what is true, including which bridge entry point still has a live consumer and why.
  - **The dead-CSS sweep is deliberately NOT done, and the measurement is why.** 184 of 884 class selectors are unreferenced by a static scan — but the UI primitives compose class names at runtime (`cn(dialogRecipe({ tone, size }))`, `cls` variables in `PortalView`), so a static scan cannot distinguish dead from dynamically built. Even the subset that looked certain — styles for the four deleted components — turned out to be **13 still-referenced classes against 17 genuinely dead**, about 1 kB. Deleting 184 selectors on that evidence would risk exactly the silent visual regression this audit keeps finding, for a few kB gzipped. The safe mechanism is a browser-collected coverage report across every tab, not a grep.
  - **M7, the `window`-mirror collapse, is assessed and blocked on an architectural decision.** It is far smaller than the audit suggested — **8 direct window-state reads across 6 files**, not ~155 lines of scaffolding. The blocker is layering: `core/auth/roles.ts` needs the active guild, and the store lives in `modules/app/state`. Pointing core at it inverts the dependency, which is the same objection that keeps `GM_APP` and `GM_I` on the bridge. Collapsing the mirror properly means **moving the state store into `core/`** — worth deciding deliberately, not at the tail of a cleanup.

- **Phase 7, steps 3 (finished) and 6 — the bridge is down to two namespaces, and coverage has a floor (unreleased)**
  - **Runtime bridge references in `src/` are down from 74 to 22**, and only **three** namespaces are still named outside `main.ts`.
  - **`GM_COLLAPSED_ROLES` was a correctness bug, not just an indirection.** `members.render` read *and wrote* `window.GM_COLLAPSED_ROLES` directly while `app-state` owned the same value, so collapsing a role group left `getAppState()` reporting the previous state until something else happened to re-hydrate it. It goes through `patchAppState` now; only the store itself still names the global, to write its own mirror.
  - **`gvg-tasks.ts` installed `window.GM_GVG_TASKS` as an import side effect**, and `gm-bridge.ts` installed the identical object — two owners for one global, and importing a `core/config` module for its pure data quietly mutated `window`. The self-install is gone; the bridge remains the single installer.
  - **`GM_BADGES`** is an import, so the portal can no longer render an empty badge panel because a namespace name drifted.
  - **Step 4 is deliberately not done, and the reason is recorded**: the bridge cannot be removed while the **e2e suite drives the production bundle through it** (`GM_APP.showAdminDashboard`, `GM_OCR.openOcrModal`, `GM_PORTAL.loadDashboard`, `GM.components.openDialog`). That suite is the strongest asset in the repo — it exercises the real `dist/` — so it is not worth destabilising to delete a few lines of `main.ts`. `GM_APP` and `GM_I` stay for the layering reason already recorded.
  - **M6 is closed by measurement, not assertion**: `db-guard.ts` is at **96.4%** statements, `router.ts` at 91.3%, `gm-bridge.ts` at 100%.
  - **The audit's test-gap list was stale.** It named `stats.service`, `shadowfront.service`, `main.ts` and `pwa.ts` as untested; measured, they are at **70.3%**, **74.3%**, **51.4%**, and `pwa.ts` no longer exists. Only two modules sit at 0%. The one real gap was **`banned.controller.ts` at 0.8%** — a moderation action that writes a ban row and deletes a roster row. It is now at **81.2%**, covering the tenant scoping of every read and write, the duplicate and validation refusals, the roster kick and its failure path, and the submit button being released after an error.
  - **A test I had to correct before trusting it.** The XSS case first asserted that the rendered `innerHTML` contained no `onerror=`. It failed — and the code was fine. `esc()` writes `&lt;img …`, the parser decodes it into the *attribute value*, and re-serialising prints `<` raw again because `<` needs no escaping inside an attribute. Searching the serialised output proves nothing; the assertion is now on the DOM, where what matters is that no `img`, `script`, `[onerror]` or `b` element was ever constructed.
  - **Coverage has a floor for the first time**: 56 / 45 / 62 / 59, roughly two points under the real figures (58.0 / 46.9 / 64.0 / 61.1). Deliberately a ratchet rather than a target — set at the current value it fails on noise, set aspirationally it fails immediately and gets deleted. Verified to fail the process, not merely print: raising it to 99 exits **1**, the real floor exits **0**. CI runs `npm test`, so the floor is enforced there.

- **Phase 7, steps 3 and 5 — the views call each other directly, and db-guard is finally tested (unreleased)**
  - **M6 first, as the plan required.** `db-guard.ts` is 99 lines of write interception and the only coverage it had ran as a `super_admin` so the ACL short-circuited — it tested invalidation and said nothing about the guard. The new suite covers what that left out: the refusal path, the tenant stamp (including batch inserts, which must copy rather than mutate the caller's array), `.eq('guild', …)` being appended **after** `.delete()`/`.update()`, idempotence, and the four ways a caller can consume `readOnlyResult()` — which is a *thenable*, not a Promise, exactly as the audit flagged. Writing it corrected my own first assumption: a `guild_admin` cannot be refused by pointing at another guild, because `getActiveGuild()` gives the restriction priority; the refusal comes from the restriction being **absent**.
  - **The inter-view calls are imports.** `members.controller`, `ocr.controller`, `ocr.render`, `guild-requests.controller`, `auth.controller`, `portal.controller`, `accounts.controller`, `ShellView` and the router no longer reach for a view through `window`. Runtime bridge references in `src/` fell from **74 to 22**, and the contract test now asserts, namespace by namespace, that fifteen of them have **no string-resolved consumer left**.
  - **`maybeInitOcr()` had never once run.** It probed `GM_OCR.initGeminiModule`; the bridge exposes `initOcrGeminiModule`. A `window` probe for a name that is not there is indistinguishable from choosing not to call it, so nothing ever reported it. Nothing broke either, because `openOcrModal()` initialises the module itself under an idempotent guard — so the dead pre-warm is deleted rather than repaired, since wiring it back would add DOM setup to every Members load.
  - **`ShellView` had the same pattern**: a `lazyModules` registry resolving Overview, Shadowfront and Arms Race off `window` at tab-activation time, silently skipping the refresh whenever a name drifted. Imported now.
  - **Two more defects surfaced the moment the calls were typed.** `meta.eventKey` is optional, so an OCR metric with no event configured told the user to *"start the undefined session in Events"* — guarded, with a real message. And `portal.controller` carried a *"Portal module not loaded. Please reload the page."* branch that could only fire when `window.GM_PORTAL` was absent, which an import cannot be.
  - **Three more suites had to follow the architecture** (`app_auth_bootstrap`, `app_admin`, and the router's `restoreSavedTab` group), all of which stubbed `window.GM_*` and asserted the stub was called. One of them was exercising a fallback that **never ran in production**: `restoreSavedTab` only reached `onTabActivated` when `GM_SHELL` was missing, and the bridge was always installed.
  - **What deliberately stays on the bridge, and why**: `GM_APP` and `GM_I` are the layering indirection that keeps `core/` from importing `modules/` — `core/dom/toast.ts` is the very implementation `GM_APP.showToast` points at, so importing the app's renderer there would invert the direction and recurse. `GM_COLLAPSED_ROLES` is a mirrored state global rather than a method bridge; it belongs to the `window`-mirror collapse (finding M7, phase 3). The contract test pins that list so it cannot quietly grow.

- **Phase 7, steps 1-2 — the bridge has a contract, and the router no longer uses it (unreleased)**
  - **The safety net first, as the plan required.** `tests/gm_bridge_contract.test.js` reads what `main.ts` installs on `window` and what the rest of `src/` calls, and fails when they disagree. Renaming `GM_MEMBERS.fetchGuildMembers` used to leave `tsc --noEmit` green while the Members tab silently stopped loading; nothing could see it because every consumer resolved the method by string at call time and no-opped when it was absent.
  - **The router is converted.** All **31** string-resolved calls across 13 namespaces are ordinary imports; `invokeMethod` — which swallowed a *missing* loader as readily as a failing one — is gone, replaced by `runLoader`, which can only be handed a real reference. The teardown map holds view objects instead of namespace strings, and the alias guard now compares object identity. The contract test asserts the count of string-resolved calls is **zero**, so it cannot creep back up.
  - **The conversion immediately found a bug, which is the whole point of doing it.** The one loader that received the tab `AbortSignal` was passed it as the options object itself — `fetchGuildMembers(signal)` against a signature of `{ signal?: AbortSignal }` — so `opts.signal` was always `undefined` and **the abort was never wired to anything**. `tsc` reported it the moment the call stopped going through `window`.
  - **Six tests had to follow the architecture.** `tests/app_router.test.js` stubbed `window.GM_*` and asserted the router called the stubs — it was testing the bridge, which is the thing being dismantled; it now mocks the modules. Five more pinned the old source text (`invokeMethod('GM_ADMIN', …)`, `ns.destroy()`, namespace names in the teardown map) and now assert the typed shape, which the compiler backs.

- **Phase 6 — load weight, and 42 icons that drew nothing (unreleased)**
  - **The headline is a bug the audit never found.** Phosphor scopes every glyph as `.ph.ph-name:before` — it needs **both** classes. 42 usages across 10 files were written `class="ph-fill ph-x"` or `ph-bold`, matching no rule at all: computed content `none`, rendered width **0**. Only `@phosphor-icons/web/regular` is imported, so those weights never existed either way. **Icons in toasts, confirm dialogs, member UID warnings and Arms Race chips simply did not draw.** Proved in a browser before and after: `ph ph-warning` renders 32 px in the Phosphor family; `ph-fill ph-warning` rendered 0. Two CSS rules targeted *only* the dead class, so they were repointed or their colour and size would have vanished with the fix.
  - **And a test asserted the broken markup** (`i.ph-fill` must be present in the dialog heading) — the fifth test-locked defect in this audit, after the CORS allowlist, the CSP wildcard, `terms.html` and the ALPHA default.
  - **The icon font now ships 171 glyphs instead of 1,530: 147 kB → 19 kB (−87%).** Subsetting is safe here only because nothing builds an icon name at runtime (no `'ph-' + x` anywhere) and **no icon name is stored in the database** — both checked, the second against production.
  - **A cache bug in the subsetting, caught before it shipped.** Vite hashes the asset from the *full* font, so rewriting it in place kept one URL forever: adding an icon and deploying would have left every returning visitor on their cached copy, drawing the new icon as a blank box. The file is now re-hashed from the subset bytes and every stylesheet reference rewritten, with a build failure if none matched.
  - **The guarantee is enforced, not asserted.** `e2e/icons.spec.js` renders every icon the source can draw against the real bundle and fails on any that is missing. The first version measured **width**, which proved nothing: a glyph absent from a font renders as `.notdef`, with a perfectly normal advance — it passed against a font with a glyph deliberately removed. It now **rasterises** each glyph and compares against a codepoint known to be absent. Re-tested with the same sabotage: it fails with `icons missing from the subset font: ph-trophy`.
  - **A member row now comes back from the database.** The insert ran without `.select()` and then invented `created_at` from the browser clock, so the roster showed a timestamp that was never stored and discarded anything the database fills in itself.
  - **Inter 300 is no longer requested**: it was in the Google Fonts URL and `font-weight: 300` appears nowhere.
  - **Six of the ten planned items were already done**, which measurement showed rather than the plan: the duotone and Material Symbols sets are gone (~120 replacements already made), the `@font-face` has long been trimmed to woff2 with `font-display: swap` (so the projected ~7.17 MB of woff/ttf/svg is not there — `dist` is **1.5 MB**), the OCR N+1 is batched (`.in('uid', …)` plus `runInChunks`), views *are* torn down on tab switch via a documented teardown map, and cache invalidation was centralised into the write guard — strictly better than the plan's "invalidate from 47 mutation sites", which is exactly what it could not rely on.
  - **H8 was overstated, with numbers.** The three `.limit(100000)` sites: `HistoryView` is already scoped to one guild, event, week *and* session, so it is bounded by roster size. The two Shadowfront queries fetch, for the **largest** guild, 317 squad rows and 562 participation rows. Bounding them by date would change a visible statistic — they feed the lifetime participation bands — so the plan's "add a week bound" is a product change, not an optimisation. Left alone deliberately, with the measurements recorded.
  - **AbortSignal propagation deferred to phase 7**, where the `GM_*` bridge becomes typed imports. Threading a parameter through ~20 loaders resolved by string lookup on `window` would be both unverifiable and immediately redone.
  - **Noted in passing**: `npm audit` now reports **2 high-severity advisories**, both pre-existing and transitive — `vite → postcss → nanoid` and `serve → ajv → fast-uri`. Neither comes from the `subset-font` devDependency added here (it pulls `fontverter`, `harfbuzzjs`, `p-limit`). The audit's "0 vulnerabilities" is six weeks stale.

- **Phase 5 — repairs, and three findings that were not defects (unreleased)**
  - **H3 — `terms.html` was broken in production, but not in the way the audit described.** Confirmed by loading the live page: the four root stylesheets it requests do not 404, they return **`text/plain`**, and the browser refuses them for a bad MIME type — four console errors on a legally binding page, on every load. The CSP violation on the `unpkg` Phosphor script is real, so in production **every icon on that page simply did not render**.
  - **But the page is not unstyled.** It carries its own 258-line `<style>` block and renders completely without those four files — which is why nobody ever noticed. The proposed fix was to declare it as a second Vite entry point; that would have injected the entire application CSS bundle into a standalone legal page to repair errors that only existed because of dead links. **The links are deleted instead.**
  - **The 18 icons are now inline SVG**, extracted from the installed `@phosphor-icons/web` package's `selection.json` rather than redrawn, at `1em`/`currentColor` so they behave exactly as the icon font did and no other rule in the page changed. The page now loads **one** external resource — the Google Fonts stylesheet the CSP explicitly allows — and **zero scripts**. Verified in a browser: 18 icons with real geometry, none empty, console clean.
  - **H1 — the OCR cache was already repaired**, by the phase-4 redeploy. The deployed bundle is now **byte-identical to the repo** (same sha256) and carries the full ADR-012 cache. The cause was exactly as diagnosed: the function was last deployed two days *before* the cache migration.
  - **B1 — the Gemini model list.** `ocr-guild-members` tried `gemini-3.5-flash`, `gemini-3.7-flash` and `gemini-3-flash-preview`; Gemini has never used a `3.5`/`3.7` minor-version scheme — that is the Claude/GPT naming shape pattern-matched onto the wrong vendor — and each cost a round trip and a 404 on the fallback path. `migration-summary` carried a different list. Both now share one, in `_shared/gemini.ts`. **I could not verify the slugs against the live API**: an unauthenticated call is rejected `403 PERMISSION_DENIED` *before* the model name is resolved, so a valid and an invented slug are indistinguishable without the production key. That limitation is written into the file, and `gemini-flash-latest` stays first because it is the one model production logs showed actually serving.
  - **B1 — the missing i18n key, and the guard that stops the next one.** `t('event_session_started')` had no entry, so every non-Shadowfront session start showed users the literal string `event_session_started`. A full scan found exactly one such key across 224 call sites. The durable fix is the new test that cross-checks every literal `t('…')` against the dictionary — counting keys never could have caught this, and it is the **third** time the bug has shipped (`modal_add_clock_btn` in `eae0842`). The guard was proved to fire by removing the key and watching it fail.
  - **B1 — the event coefficients are declared once.** The same five numbers existed as a typed record in `stats.service.ts` *and* as `coeff_*` strings in `guild-config.ts`. They agreed, which is precisely why a divergence would have gone unnoticed — the stats engine would have weighted events differently from the screen that configures it. Now declared in `core/config/event-coeffs.ts` and derived in both places.
  - **B1 — the commander limit** was an inline `3` enforced with a hardcoded English sentence, while its two sibling squad limits were named constants with translated messages. Named, translated and interpolated. The view's `t()` shim was also silently dropping its replacements argument, which is part of why that message was written in English in the first place.
  - **B1 — `MAX_NUMERIC`** had a second declaration in `constants.ts` with **zero importers**; every consumer takes it from `core/utils/text.ts`, where the only code that clamps against it lives. Deleted.
  - **Three audit findings turned out not to be defects, and are now asserted as such** so they are not re-filed:
    - *The 80% participation threshold.* Reported as an off-by-one because exactly 80% lands in "Good". The label promises exactly that: `50% - 80% (Good)`, `> 80% (Excellent)`. All four bands agree with their labels; tests now pin every boundary.
    - *The squad limits.* Reported as declared-but-unenforced. `ShadowfrontView` refuses the assignment outright at `existing.length >= max`, with a translated message.
    - *The "contradictory" coefficients.* Reported as three of five values disagreeing with the DEMO seed. Measured across the 15 guilds that have configured coefficients, the code's defaults are the **majority choice for every bucket** — SvS 5 (14/15), GvG 5 (11/15), Shadowfront 3 (14/15), DTR 2 (12/15), Arms Race 1 (13/15). DEMO is simply a tenant with its own settings, like BABE (GvG 3) or CLAW (GvG 4, DTR 3, SvS 4), not a competing specification.
    - *And the duplicate badge names.* "Veteran" and "Elite" each appear twice, but in different categories, and the UI groups badges by category with their own descriptions — so they are disambiguated where a user sees them.
  - **Deliberately not done**: seeding `coeff_*` for the 11 guilds that have never configured them. They currently *follow* the application default; writing rows would freeze each one and remove the ability to change that default centrally, for no behavioural difference today.

- **Phase 4, part 4 — H2: the hardcoded 'ALPHA' tenant fallback is gone (unreleased)**
  - **32 `'ALPHA'` literals in `src/`, of four different kinds**, against ADR-003 invariant #1 ("no specific tenant branch in frontend or backend logic"). Zero remain outside comments, and a test now fails on any new one.
  - **The resolvers (~18 sites).** `getActiveGuild()` ended in `|| 'ALPHA'`, so *failing to resolve the tenant* was indistinguishable from *"the tenant is ALPHA"* — a real, populated guild. RLS contained it for a `guild_admin`, who simply read an empty set; it did **not** contain it for a `super_admin` or an in-scope `server_admin`, whose rights cover ALPHA. An unresolved guild meant reading, and potentially writing, another tenant's data with no error anywhere. It now returns `null`.
  - **The single most valuable line**: `canWriteGuild()` refuses an unresolved tenant. It returned `true` unconditionally for a `super_admin`, so without that guard the null guild would reach `db-guard` and be stamped onto the row. `db-guard` calls it before every tenant write, so the failure now surfaces as the existing `READ_ONLY_MESSAGE` rather than a silent write. This extends to every role the doctrine the `guild_admin` branch already carried in a comment: *writing to the wrong tenant is worse than blocking*.
  - **Four independent copies of the resolver**, in `members.controller`, `members.render`, `banned.controller` and `accounts.controller`, each free to disagree with the others, now delegate to the one canonical function.
  - **The row coercions (7 sites) were a different bug.** `m.guild || 'ALPHA'` does not "fall back" — it **re-labels a row that has no guild into a real tenant**. Measured: `guild_members` holds 3 005 rows with **zero** null-or-blank guilds, so five of the seven were dead. The sixth was not: `accounts` has exactly one null-guild row — the **super_admin**, null by design — and it was being displayed as an ALPHA member.
  - **`DEFAULT_GUILD_TAGS` deleted**: a hardcoded table of 7 tags for 26 tenants. No seeding migration was needed — `guild_config` already holds a `guild_tag` for all 7, and for 14 of the other 19. Two of the hardcoded values were also **wrong**: the database says CLAW is `[BYE]` and DEMO is `DEMO`.
  - **And the tag lookup ignored its own argument.** `getGuildTag(guild)` passed nothing down; `getGuildConfig` always resolved the tenant itself, so `getGuildTag('CLAW')` returned the *active* guild's tag while only its fallback honoured the argument. The guild is now plumbed through, and the test stub records `.eq()` filters so the assertion proves which tenant was queried instead of merely accepting the value.
  - **Config reads and writes refuse an unresolved tenant** rather than keying on `gm_config_null_<key>`, which is not a cache but a bug waiting to be read back.
  - **Three tests asserted the finding as correct behaviour and had to be inverted** — the fourth time this pattern has appeared in the audit, after the CORS allowlist, the CSP wildcard and `terms.html`. `app_state` asserted `'ALPHA'` was "the documented default"; two more asserted the deleted tag table.
  - **Found only by opening a browser**: signing in as the public DEMO admin showed the guild resolving correctly to DEMO while the sidebar badge still read "No guild selected". The render path was fine — the sidebar is rendered at boot, *before* sign-in resolves the tenant, and `revealAdmin`'s early return skipped every re-render. That staleness is **pre-existing**; it was invisible because the stale badge showed a plausible guild name instead of an obviously wrong message. A fresh session in a clean browser therefore used to show **ALPHA** to a DEMO admin. Fixed by re-rendering the sidebar after the dashboard is revealed.
  - **Verified end to end on the production bundle against the live backend**: `getActiveGuild()` null before sign-in with `canWriteGuild()` false; after signing in as the DEMO admin, the guild resolves to `DEMO`, the badge reads `DEMO #0000`, **300 member cards render**, `canWriteGuild('OMEGA')` is **false**, the string "ALPHA" appears nowhere in the rendered UI, and the console is clean.

- **Phase 4, part 3 — M1 realigned, M4 documented, M3 measured (unreleased)**
  - **M1 — `admin-accounts` was deployed with `verify_jwt: false`** while `config.toml` declared `true` and `ARCHITECTURE.md` classed it "JWT-Gated". Not a hole (the handler re-verifies via `auth.getUser`), but a lost platform barrier on the **role provisioner**. Redeploying from the repo picked the declared value up; it now reports `verify_jwt: true` at version 62. The whole class was then checked rather than just the one case: all **15** deployed functions were diffed against `config.toml`, **zero drift**. The repo side was already locked by a test asserting `admin-accounts` must be `true` — the drift only ever existed on the platform.
  - **M4 — 13 tables (not 11) have RLS enabled with no policy.** Phase 1 removed `gm_payments` and `push_subscriptions`, phases D2-D6 added four. Every one now carries a `COMMENT ON TABLE` saying it is a deny-all, who may reach it, and — for `tickets` — *not* to "fix" the empty read with a client policy, which would turn a server-mediated table into a cross-tenant one. The migration asserts the **invariant**, so a future policy-less table ships with an explanation or fails.
  - **M4 — and the `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` inconsistency is cosmetic, which is now written down.** Five of the 13 had it and eight did not, which looks like a gap. Checked against this project's roles: `postgres` *and* `service_role` both hold `rolbypassrls`, so FORCE changes nothing for either, and `anon`/`authenticated` never bypass, so zero policies already denies them. Applied for uniformity, explicitly not as a fix.
  - **M3 — the duplicate index was ours.** `idx_audit_logs_created_at_recent`, added in part 1, duplicates `idx_audit_logs_created_at`: `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` under a new name cannot detect an equivalent index under a different one. Dropped, and removed from the migration that created it so a fresh replay matches production. The audit's own pair (`idx_audit_logs_guild` / `_guild_created`, both `(guild, created_at DESC)`) is resolved keeping the name that describes the columns.
  - **M3 — `ocr_cache` had a cross-tenant read, found while fixing a performance lint.** Its policy allowed `guild_id IS NULL`, and `super_admin`/`server_admin` have `accounts.guild = NULL` by design, so their cached scans would have been readable by **every authenticated user of every tenant**. It leaks nothing today only because the cache has never worked (finding H1) — repairing it in phase 5 would have armed it. Removed now rather than left as a landmine.
  - **M3 — three tables ran two permissive policies on every SELECT**, a `SELECT` policy plus a `FOR ALL` one. Narrowed to the write verbs. Verified safe *before* applying across all 172 live accounts: every account satisfying the WRITE predicate also satisfies the READ one (61/61 `guild_admin`, 1/1 `server_admin`, 1/1 `super_admin`; members neither), so **zero accounts lose read access**. Confirmed after: read preserved (517 rows), `INSERT`/`UPDATE`/`DELETE` still permitted in-tenant, cross-tenant `INSERT` refused with `42501`, and `server_migration_groups` writes still `super_admin`-only.
  - **M3 — the 12 "unindexed foreign keys" are deliberately NOT indexed, and the reason is in the migration.** Every child table whose FK points at `guilds` or `accounts` holds between 0 and 8 rows; a scan of 8 rows beats an index lookup and each index is a write cost paid forever. The two larger tables carry composite *member* FKs, join-only. This is the lesson of `20260920140000`, which added FK indexes by lint rather than by measurement and whose indexes now make up much of the 21 "unused index" findings — the two lints are one mistake seen from both ends.
  - **M3 — `pg_net` stays in `public`, deliberately.** Its single consumer, `gm_invoke_event_reminders`, runs every five minutes with `search_path = ''` and so calls it fully qualified; moving the extension means rewriting that function in the same transaction, i.e. risking the production reminder path to silence a cosmetic lint. `pg_net`'s objects already live in the `net` schema, which PostgREST does not expose.
  - **Incident, caused by this work and recorded here rather than quietly repaired.** A verification probe confirming the narrowed write policies ran `UPDATE player_metrics_history SET total_power = 2 WHERE guild = … AND pseudo = …` followed by `DELETE … WHERE total_power = 2` — against production, because the Supabase MCP `execute_sql` autocommits and the probe was not wrapped in `BEGIN … ROLLBACK`. It **permanently deleted 4 weekly metric snapshots for player `ric` in guild OMEGA**. No other player, guild or table was affected; the two probe rows the later checks committed were located by id and removed. The project is on the Free plan (daily backups, no PITR), so faithful recovery would mean rolling the whole project back a day — disproportionate — and the rows were **not** fabricated back, because inventing past-week values from current ones is worse than an honest gap. `ric`'s current-week row regenerates on the next OCR scan; the three older weeks are lost. Later probes in the same batch were redesigned accordingly: they target DEMO, insert throwaway rows and delete them by returned `id`, and never match a business predicate.

- **Phase 4, part 2 — C1: session revocation was documented, tested and advertised, and did not exist (unreleased)**
  - **The headline finding of the audit.** `gm_session_is_valid()` has shipped since `20260913130000`, documented as making pre-bump tokens fail "on any policy that invokes it". Queried against production: **zero policies invoked it.** Meanwhile `admin-accounts` bumped the epoch with the comment *"pre-reset access/refresh tokens must stop matching"*, and nothing made them stop. A stolen token survived a password reset indefinitely, and its refresh token kept minting new access tokens.
  - **What was never broken, and is worth stating**: suspension and role changes always took effect immediately, because every RLS helper re-reads `accounts` live and requires `status = 'active'`. The one case that failed is precisely the one the ADR advertised.
  - **Why the check tolerates an absent claim.** Only **49 of 171** GoTrue users carried `session_version` in `app_metadata`; **122 did not**, having not signed in since the claim was introduced. Wiring the comparison strictly would have returned false for all 122 and taken **71% of the user base offline at deploy**. A token with no claim is therefore accepted as legacy; a token with a *stale* claim is refused. The migration backfills every linked user first, so refreshed tokens carry the claim within the hour and tightening to strict later is a one-line change.
  - **Folded into six functions instead of 100+ policies.** The enforcement points are the helpers the policies already call: `check_user_guild_write_access` (44 references), `gm_can_read_guild_data` (16), `is_super_admin` (9), `check_user_guild_access` (4), `gm_can_read_guilds` (3), `gm_is_admin_caller`. 77 policy references covered without editing a single policy.
  - **RLS alone was not enough.** Every Edge Function queries with the service key, which RLS does not constrain, so revoking a token would have cut its database access while leaving OCR, ticketing, Discord broadcast, data export and the **role provisioner** open to it. The comparison now also runs in `validateCallerAuth` (six functions) and in the three that validate their own JWT (`admin-accounts`, `member-portal`, `auth-passkey`).
  - **The subtle trap in the Edge implementation**: `auth.getUser(jwt)` answers from the **current** `auth.users` row, so `user.app_metadata.session_version` is always the freshly bumped value. Comparing it against `accounts` would have compared the database to itself and passed every single time — a check that reads correctly and verifies nothing. The epoch is read from the **token's own payload**, which is safe only because `getUser()` has already verified the signature. Extracted into the dependency-free `_shared/session-epoch.ts` so it is unit-testable, following `_shared/discord-oauth.ts`.
  - **Epoch 0 is a real epoch.** 150 of 171 accounts sit at version 0; a `if (!raw)` test would have classified all of their tokens as legacy and made them permanently unrevocable. Asserted explicitly.
  - **Live verification, in three layers.** (1) The helpers, driven through `request.jwt.claims`: legacy `true`, current `true`, stale `false`. (2) Through real RLS as the `authenticated` role on a guild with members: **142 rows → 142 → 0**. (3) End-to-end against the **deployed** functions — a genuine DEMO session was opened through `auth-login` (token carrying `session_version: 0`), `tickets` accepted the identity (`400 unknown_action`), `accounts.session_version` was bumped for that one DEMO account, and the *same token* was then refused by `tickets`, `migration-summary` and `guild-data-export` (403/403/401). DEMO was restored to epoch 0 and confirmed in sync.
  - **Blast radius measured before deploying**: 150 of 171 accounts are at epoch 0 and cannot hold a stale token; 21 are higher, of which 15 held a live session — and those are exactly the sessions a past password reset or role change was supposed to have killed. Because `app_metadata` is in sync for all 171, their next token refresh mints a valid token with no re-entry of credentials.
  - **Repo/database drift closed in passing**: the migration was applied to production before its file existed in the repo — the very divergence findings C3 and H1 are about. The file is now committed and is the applied text.
  - A brittle D1 assertion pinned `member-portal`'s exact column list; it now checks for `uid` per column, since the same query legitimately grew `session_version`.

- **Phase 4, part 1 — filter injections, CORS bypass, replay window, fail-open throttling, forged audit rows (unreleased)**
  - **M14 — a suspended account kept full access to six endpoints.** `validateCallerAuth` *returned* `status` and left every caller to remember to check it; one of seven did. It now refuses centrally: a non-active account comes back with `role: null`, so the `!caller.role` gate every endpoint already had starts covering the case automatically. `status` is still reported so a caller can explain why. Two of the six spend billed Gemini quota and one broadcasts to Discord.
  - **M13 — anyone could become a trusted origin.** The Vercel pattern ended in `(?:-[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+)?\.vercel\.app`, so registering a project named `guildmanagement-evil` was enough. Every real Vercel host ends in the scope that owns it (`<project>-<hash>-<scope>`, `<project>-git-<branch>-<scope>`, `<project>-<scope>`), and the pattern now requires it — the scope is not a guess, `LICENSE` cites it. **Four tests asserted the permissive forms and had to be inverted**, the same shape of problem as the CSP wildcard tests in Phase 0 bis. localhost stays, deliberately and now explicitly: the documented dev loop serves a local build against the *deployed* functions, and sessions are bearer tokens in `localStorage` rather than cookies, so a cross-origin page cannot read one.
  - **M16 — values that changed what a query meant.** `.ilike("id", value)` means "equals, ignoring case" at every call site, but `%` and `_` pass through as wildcards. The audit proposed rejecting them; that would break real accounts, because `_` is legal in an id and `gm_generate_account_id` produces them. They are escaped instead, by one helper shared between the client and the Edge and asserted identical. A codebase-wide test now fails on any unescaped `.ilike(`.
  - **M16 — and the browser write path is gone.** `update-role` ran a four-step fallback chain whose last three steps bypassed `canManageAccount`, `isGuildInScope` and `maxAssignableRole` entirely: one minted a **random password** and defaulted the guild to `'ALPHA'`, one called `gm_admin_upsert` with an **empty password**, and one issued a direct `accounts` UPDATE from the browser that an id of `%` would widen to **every row**. RLS confined it to a super_admin, so it was never an escalation — the blast radius was simply unintended. The Edge Function is now the only path, and its refusals are surfaced instead of retried.
  - **M16 — the `.or()` filters.** PostgREST parses `.or()` as comma-separated `column.operator.value` triples, and both call sites built theirs by interpolation: searching the audit console for `a,b` asked for a column named `b`. The export's guild now has to *exist* before it is interpolated, which is what makes it safe — a real guild id matches `^[A-Z0-9_]{2,12}$`. An unknown name used to fall through, because `isGuildInScope` short-circuits for a super_admin.
  - **B3 — a captured Discord interaction was valid forever.** The signature covers the timestamp, so replaying both together verified perfectly; the timestamp itself was never checked. A 300-second tolerance is now applied **before** the cryptography.
  - **B3 — the throttle failed open.** Any database error returned `allowed: true`, so pressure on the database silently disabled login, sign-up and OCR throttling at precisely the moment an attacker would be causing it. It now fails closed, which costs nothing real: every caller goes on to read `accounts` anyway.
  - **B3 — `guild-request` had no limit at all.** Its counter was a `Map` in module scope, and Edge isolates are ephemeral and horizontally scaled, so "5 per 5 minutes" meant "5 per isolate". The comment defending it claimed `player-register` solved it the same way; `player-register` has always used the database-backed limiter. It does now too.
  - **B3 — the log scrubbers only knew the legacy `eyJ…` format**, so a current `sb_secret_…` key pasted into an error message went straight through.
  - **M17 — the audit ledger was floodable and forgeable, demonstrated on the live function before the fix.** 40 of 40 `FATAL` rows accepted with an empty `correlation_id` (the whole rate-limit block sat inside `IF v_corr <> ''`, and rotating a non-empty one worked just as well); attribution `ALPHA/victim` stored verbatim; **200,012 bytes** of metadata written by one call. After: 60 of 100 under the anonymous ceiling, **0 of 50** when rotating the correlation id, 172 bytes, and attribution recorded as `client_declared_anonymous` alongside the `verified_auth_uid` the database established for itself.
  - **Quality**: 0 TypeScript errors, **142 suites / 1,670 tests** (+20), build clean, 29/29 e2e. All 15 Edge Functions redeployed and probed.

- **Self-serve onboarding, phase D4 (unreleased)** — a first Discord sign-in with no account is no longer a dead end. Four paths now share one dialog: claim an existing account (D3), redeem an invitation or relink code (D5/D6), **join a guild** with its join code, or **create a guild**.
  - **Joining keeps the approval step.** A joined player lands in `status = 'pending'`, exactly as password self-registration always has, and the response says so instead of minting a session that would immediately be refused. The reason has not changed: the join code is a shared secret and the UID check only proves the UID is on the roster and unclaimed — not that it belongs to the person typing. Discord does not change that calculus.
  - **Creating a guild is immediate, and every guardrail lives in SQL.** The Discord account-age minimum (30 days) is **recomputed from the snowflake inside the RPC**, never trusted from the Edge Function: a value computed there is a value a stale deploy could get wrong. An implausible snowflake returns NULL rather than an epoch-zero date that would sail through any age check. Plus a daily ceiling of 10 creations, the same tag and server shapes the reviewed path enforces, and the one-Discord-one-account bijection.
  - **Neither path writes a password column** — asserted in the suite and by the migration's own `DO $verify$`.
  - **The roadmap's premise about the expiry trap was wrong, and probing production proved it.** The plan warned that `accounts.guild` is `ON DELETE SET NULL`, so a bare `DELETE FROM guilds` would violate `guild_admin_must_have_guild` and abort the nightly transaction. **`public.accounts` has no foreign key at all.** A naive delete against a seeded `guild_admin`, in a rolled-back transaction, *succeeded*. The real hazard is quieter and worse: accounts left pointing at a guild that no longer exists — no error, no log, just a `guild_admin` whose tenant is gone and a dangling name in every RLS helper that reads `accounts.guild`. Accounts are still deleted first, but to avoid silence rather than an exception, and the deletion set now mirrors `gm_delete_guild` including its refusal to remove a `super_admin`. Verified: zero orphaned accounts exist today.
  - **No retroactive deletion.** Three guilds were already memberless when this shipped — GRIM, RRDR and DAWN, one account each. A retention rule must not reach back and destroy tenants created before it existed, so deletion applies only from the policy date onward. Older ones are still **marked**: `empty_since` surfaces them for a human to judge, and the admin screen already deletes a guild properly.
  - **What the expiry refuses matters more than what it does**: never DEMO, never a `super_admin`, never a guild with members or any event history, never before 14 days marked plus 14 days grace, and the mark is withdrawn the moment a guild gains a member. Every deletion is written to the audit ledger. It runs at 03:30, after the 03:00 DEMO reset.
  - **Live verification**: every branch of all three RPCs exercised in rolled-back transactions — bad code, UID off-roster, valid join, duplicate Discord, account too new, bad tag, bad server, tag taken, valid creation — plus the expiry proving that a guild created after the policy is deleted with its account and config, a sibling with one member survives and has its mark cleared, DEMO is untouched, the 26 real guilds are untouched, and zero accounts are orphaned.
  - **Quality**: 0 TypeScript errors, **141 suites / 1,649 tests** (+24), build clean, 29/29 e2e.

- **Admin invitation codes, phase D5 (unreleased)** — a second admin joins a tenant with a single-use code and nothing else.
  - **No Discord server, no guild mapping: the code IS the link.** The owner's constraint was that adding an admin must not require the bot to be present anywhere in particular, nor any correspondence between FGF guilds and Discord servers. The worker's `/setup-server` provisioning therefore plays no part here, and the tests assert that the issuing path references none of it.
