  - **The invited account is Discord-only from birth.** No password column is ever written — asserted both in the test suite and by the migration's own `DO $verify$` block. `establishShadowSession` reads neither column, so sessions work exactly as for a claimed account.
  - **The account id is generated inside the transaction**, against `idx_accounts_lower_id`. Generating it in the Edge Function and inserting afterwards is a TOCTOU race two simultaneous redemptions would lose. `accounts.id` stays the human-readable text key it has always been — it is a textual foreign key referenced from many tables, so replacing it with an opaque id plus a display name is a migration of its own, and that is recorded rather than smuggled in.
  - **Testing with realistic input caught a real defect.** The first version stripped straight to `[A-Za-z0-9_]`, which turned an ordinary Discord name like `Ärès_Actual` into **`rs_Actual`** — unrecognisable to its owner. It now folds accented Latin first, via `translate()` rather than the `unaccent` extension (deterministic, nothing to install, no schema qualification needed under `search_path = ''`). Verified live: `Ares_Actual`, `AEroskobing`, `Strasse`, `Coeur`, `Nono`, `Zizek`.
  - **An invitation cannot mint privilege the issuer lacks.** Authorization reuses the existing matrix rather than inventing one: `maxAssignableRole` caps what may be granted (a `guild_admin` can never mint a `server_admin`), `isGuildInScope` caps where, and the RPC refuses a `member` role independently — the SQL does not trust the Edge Function's check.
  - **The outstanding cap is enforced in SQL under a row lock** (5 open invitations per guild), because two concurrent issues would otherwise both read "4 open" and both write. It is a constant in code, per the owner's decision: raising it is a deploy, not a settings toggle.
  - **One redemption entry point for both kinds of code.** A person holding a code has no reason to know whether an admin issued them a `relink` (D6) or an `admin_invite` (D5), and making them choose invites burning a ticket on a wrong guess. The action classifies by stored kind without consuming, then dispatches; each RPC still owns its own single-use semantics under `FOR UPDATE`, so a race loses nothing.
  - Issuing is written to the audit ledger **and** announced on the guild's Discord audit channel, for the same reason an unlink is: provisioning an admin is a privileged act the guild should be able to see.
  - **Quality**: 0 TypeScript errors, **140 suites / 1,625 tests** (+20), build clean, 29/29 e2e.

- **Discord claim, phase D3 (unreleased)** — the irreversible step. 165 accounts (102 members, 61 guild_admins, 1 server_admin, 1 super_admin) attach their Discord identity by proving they own the password; the password then ceases to exist.
  - **What a claim destroys, in the same transaction that creates the link**: `password_hash`, `password_enc`, every passkey, every in-flight WebAuthn challenge and every backup code, plus a `session_version` bump. One sign-in method at any instant, never two. Because it is one transaction, a crash cannot leave an account with neither a password nor a link — which would be an account nobody can enter.
  - **It also closes finding C0 per account.** `password_enc` is the reversible, plaintext-recoverable column the audit opened with: 172 of 172 accounts carried one. A claim retires it permanently for that account, so the migration is itself the remediation.
  - **The ordering must not be inverted**, and is asserted: `session_version` is bumped *inside* the RPC, `establishShadowSession` is called *after*. The helper re-reads `session_version` fresh, so the token it mints carries the new epoch and older ones die. The reverse order would sign the person out at the exact moment of their claim.
  - **One password check, not two.** The RPC delegates to `gm_verify_password` — the function D1 extracted from `gm_check_login` precisely so this transition could not grow a second, divergent credential check. There is no `crypt()` anywhere in the claim.
  - **Three things stand between this and a usable guessing oracle**, in order of what they cost an attacker: every attempt spends a ticket, and a ticket costs a full Discord round trip; a per-IP budget of 20 per 5 min, tighter than the 60 the other actions share; and a **per-Discord-account budget** of 10 per hour, so rotating IPs buys nothing. That budget lives in a new `discord_auth_throttle` — deliberately **not** `discord_verification_throttle`, which budgets screenshot recognition: sharing one table would let OCR scans throttle sign-ins, or the reverse.
  - **The `super_admin` leaves the request holding fresh backup codes.** The claim deletes the old set, and they have no administrator above them to unlink and reissue — so a new set is generated and shown once, before the session is minted. A failure to issue does not fail the claim: the account has already been converted, and refusing the session would strand it for nothing.
  - **The sign-in dialog now offers both routes**: claim with identifier and password (the 66 who know theirs), or a relink code from D6 (the 99 who have never logged in and will not remember a password they never used). Both spend the continuation ticket, so neither a password nor a code is sufficient alone. The dialog states plainly, before the click, that Discord becomes the only way in.
  - **A `gm_discord_claim_progress` view** gives the cutover metric one definition — `security_invoker`, revoked from `anon` and `authenticated`. **Do not fix a cutover date until `unlinked` nears zero for the admin roles.** Today: super_admin 1, server_admin 1, guild_admin 61, member 102 unlinked (7 members already linked).
  - **Live verification**: every branch exercised in a rolled-back transaction against production — wrong password, correct password, the *same correct password* again (now `invalid_credentials`, because the password no longer exists — the irreversibility made visible), Discord identity already taken, unknown account — with `session_version` 0 → 1, credentials null, passkeys and backup codes at zero. The deployed endpoint then refused a claim carrying a valid password but a bogus ticket, confirming the ticket gate sits ahead of the password check.
  - **Quality**: 0 TypeScript errors, **139 suites / 1,605 tests** (+23), build clean, 29/29 e2e.

- **Discord unlink & recovery, phase D6 (unreleased)** — built **before** the D3 claim flow, and deliberately so: once Discord is the only way in, an account whose link is lost, wrong, or never made has no route back unless one exists first.
  - **An unlink can never strand an account.** `gm_discord_unlink` detaches the link **and** issues a single-use relink code in one transaction, so a bare deletion is not something the API can express. The error is made structurally impossible rather than documented.
  - **It succeeds on an account that was never linked**, which is the escape hatch D3 needs: **99 of 172 accounts have never logged in** and will not remember a password they never used. An admin can hand them a relink code with nothing to detach.
  - **Peer unlink stays permitted — visibility is the safeguard, not restriction.** `canManageAccount` already lets a `guild_admin` manage a peer in the same guild, and the owner chose to keep that. Every unlink is therefore written to `system_audit_logs` **and announced on the guild's Discord audit channel**, and the confirmation dialog says so before the click. The ledger write comes first and is authoritative; the announcement is best-effort, because a Discord outage must not undo an unlink that already committed.
  - **A relink code alone is not enough.** It is spent together with a **continuation ticket** — a fresh, single-use ticket minted when a Discord sign-in finds no linked account. So the holder must also have just proved control of the Discord account being attached; a code found on a desk is not redeemable. That continuation ticket is new in this phase and is also what D3 (claim) and D4 (onboarding) will use.
  - **Hashed, never plaintext.** The plaintext exists once, in the HTTP response that hands it over — the deliberate opposite of `join_code_plain`, which is stored in the clear because it is a *shared*, reissuable code. Codes expire in 7 days, are consumed under `FOR UPDATE`, and both unlink and relink bump `session_version` so anything minted under the old link stops working.
  - **One table, two kinds.** `account_link_codes` carries `relink` now and `admin_invite` in D5, with CHECK constraints enforcing the shape each kind requires — a relink code without an account, or an invite without a destination, cannot be written. Deny-all (RLS enabled **and** forced, zero policies); both new RPCs are service_role only.
  - **Backup codes now have one implementation and one audience.** `randomBackupCode`, `sha256Hex`, `issueBackupCodes` and the single-use verification moved out of `auth-passkey` into `_shared/backup-codes.ts`, which `auth-discord` also uses — during the transition both doors must accept exactly the same codes. The audit found four subtly different HTML escapers (M11); a credential check is a far worse place for that drift.
  - **Backup sign-in is `super_admin` only.** Every other account has an administrator above it who can unlink and reissue — a stronger path, because a second human is involved. For those accounts a backup code would only be a weaker credential beside the strong one. A non-`super_admin` receives `invalid_code`, byte-identical to a wrong code, so the reply cannot double as a role oracle; the true reason is logged server-side.
  - **Live verification**: every branch of both RPCs exercised in a rolled-back transaction against production — unlink with no prior link, redeem, reuse rejected, unknown code, unlink again, Discord identity already taken elsewhere, missing account — plus `session_version` confirmed to move 0 → 1. The deployed endpoints were then probed: `relink` with a bogus ticket returns `ticket_invalid`; `backup-login` returns `invalid_code` identically for a `guild_admin` and for an unknown account.
  - **Quality**: 0 TypeScript errors, **138 suites / 1,582 tests** (+29), build clean, 29/29 e2e.

- **Discord sign-in, phase D2 (unreleased)** — the first working piece of the migration away from passwords and Passkeys. Additive and reversible: `auth-login` is untouched, and only 7 accounts are linked today.
  - **Why not Supabase's native Discord provider**: `app_metadata` — `app_role`, `account_id`, `session_version` — is this app's **only** authorization channel. The client signs the user out when `app_role` is absent, and `gm_session_is_valid()` compares `session_version`. GoTrue sets none of the three on an OAuth login, so enabling `[auth.external.discord]` would deadlock every session. `auth-discord` instead ends at **`establishShadowSession()`**, the convergence point every existing identity check already funnels through — no change to RLS, roles or claims.
  - **One redirect URI, pointed at the function rather than the app**: Discord demands an exact match, and the app is reachable from the Vercel domain, every preview URL and localhost. With the function as the target, a single registered URI covers every origin and the `code` is exchanged server-side without ever touching the browser.
  - **The ticket carries no session.** `vercel.json` sets `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin`, which severs `window.opener`, so the usual popup + `postMessage` handshake could never fire — this is a full-page redirect. What comes back in the URL is a single-use ticket that attests one thing: that this browser completed a Discord authorization. The session is minted only when the SPA redeems it over POST. A ticket leaked through a `Referer` header is worth one redemption inside two minutes, not a bearer token. The client strips `?gm_auth` from the URL with `replaceState` **before** redeeming, so a reload cannot retry a spent ticket and it never reaches a bookmark or an outgoing `Referer`.
  - **PKCE S256 even though the client is confidential.** One hash closes the authorization-code injection class outright: an intercepted `code` is useless without the verifier, which never leaves the database — the authorize URL carries only its S256 challenge.
  - **Single-use is atomic, not best-effort**: both the state and the ticket are consumed with `DELETE … RETURNING` inside a `SECURITY DEFINER` RPC, so two concurrent redemptions cannot both succeed. A read-then-delete pair would race.
  - **No Discord token at rest**: the access token is revoked at Discord on every exit path out of the identity read, and no column anywhere stores one. Scope is `identify` only — never `email` (no e-mail column exists, and none is wanted) and never `guilds` (the Gateway worker already knows them).
  - **The callback cannot become an open redirect**: it trusts the origin stored at `/start`, validated by `isAllowedOrigin()`, never one supplied to the callback itself, and `buildReturnUrl` drops any path, query or fragment. **Verified against production**: a `/start` carrying `origin=https://evil.example.com` persisted `https://guildmanagement.vercel.app` — the hostile value was never stored.
  - **New tables are deny-all from the start** (`20260926190000_discord_oauth_state.sql`): `discord_oauth_states` and `discord_auth_tickets` have RLS enabled **and forced** with zero policies and a `COMMENT` saying so. Finding M4 flagged eleven pre-existing tables in this posture as an undocumented implicit choice; these are explicit. All three new functions are revoked from `PUBLIC`, `anon` and `authenticated` and granted only to `service_role` — the M15 lesson.
  - **Fixed a leak nobody had noticed**: `discord_link_codes` has had no cleanup of any kind since it was created. The new hourly purge sweeps it along with the two new tables, and removed **10 expired rows** on its first run.
  - **`status !== 'active'` is enforced, not merely returned** (finding M14), so a suspended account cannot sign in through the new door.
  - **Live smoke test against production**: `/start` 302s to Discord with `scope=identify` and an S256 challenge; a forged state redirects to `?gm_auth_error=state_invalid` without attempting an exchange; a bogus ticket returns `ticket_invalid`; an unknown route returns 404.
  - **Quality**: 0 TypeScript errors, **137 suites / 1,553 tests** (+38: 15 unit tests on the pure OAuth helpers, 23 contract tests on the wiring), build clean, 29/29 e2e. The e2e suite caught a real ambiguity — the new button reuses `.gm-login-discord-btn`, so the old bare-class selector matched two elements; both are now asserted specifically.

- **Removal: payments, subscriptions and donations, in full (unreleased)**
  - **Why now**: the owner confirmed no payment was ever collected, which the data corroborates — all **22 `gm_payments` rows were `pending`, none `paid`**, so nothing had to be retained for accounting. The rows were live-mode Stripe Checkout Session ids (`cs_live_…`) for donation tiers, the most recent dated 2026-09-17, one day before `11b52b6` removed the donation UI.
  - **Five Edge Functions deleted from the platform**, taking the deployed set from 19 to 14 — now an exact match for `supabase/functions/`, which closes the repo/platform drift the audit opened with:
    - `gm-create-order`, `gm-order-status`, `gm-stripe-webhook` (source also removed from the repo, with `_shared/stripe.ts`).
    - `gm-revolut-webhook` — **ACTIVE, version 13, `verify_jwt = false`** (i.e. public) with **no source anywhere in this repository**, while `ARCHITECTURE.md` carried a warning box asserting it did not exist. It shipped its own repo-absent `_shared/revolut.ts` with a price ladder (1m 699 / 3m 1699 / 6m 2799 / lifetime 8900) that never matched Stripe's.
    - `webauthn-spike` — a leftover probe, still exposed; the real implementation is `auth-passkey`.
    - Deleting `gm-create-order` also closes a live money-taking surface: its deployed build could still mint a real Stripe Checkout Session for a product that no longer exists, so a stale browser tab could have sent someone to a live payment page with nothing on the other side.
  - **Kept, deliberately, what the roadmap had proposed deleting**: `subscriptions.controller.ts` was **renamed** to `guilds.controller.ts` and stripped, not removed. The tab was mis-named — it is the only surface that assigns a guild's `server_number` (cross-server transfers depend on it, and `members.controller` points admins at it by name) and the only caller of `gm_delete_guild`. Deleting the file would have destroyed both. Its subscription editor also carried a latent bug: it offered a `Lifetime` option that `guilds_subscription_type_check` never accepted, so choosing it failed the save.
  - **Database** (`supabase/migrations/20260926180000_drop_payments.sql`): dropped `gm_payments`, `gm_apply_subscription_payment`, `gm_apply_donation`, `guilds.subscription_type` / `subscription_end` / `plan_key` / `payments_disabled`, and the two CHECK constraints over them.
    - **`gm_reset_demo_tenant_data()` wrote three of those columns**, found by scanning the live schema before writing the migration — the third time that check has caught a cron the previous phase would have broken silently. It is repaired *before* the drop, with a pre-condition that refuses to run if the live definition has drifted and a post-condition asserting on **all five** dropped names (the push removal shipped a repair that passed its own single-name guard while leaving a second reference behind).
    - Verified by executing the repaired function against the post-drop schema inside a rolled-back transaction: `members_seeded: 60`.
    - **`is_subscription_active(text)` is kept**, still `SELECT true`. It is invoked by **44 RLS policies** (the audit estimated ~36); removing it means rewriting every write policy, which is its own lot, not a payment-removal side effect.
  - **Client**: removed `isGuildSubscriptionExpired()` and `isPaymentsDisabled()` (both dead — the first always returned `false`, so the two `canWriteGuild` gates that called it were no-ops), their `window.GM` bridge entries, the sidebar subscription pill, and the subscription mirror in `window.guildsData`.
    - **Also removed two things the subscription card was hiding**: a `body.guild-read-only` lock that **no code ever applied** (34 CSS selectors plus a permanently-hidden `#guild-warning-banner`, all only ever *cleared*), and `ShellView`'s **30-second `setInterval`** that rebuilt the whole sidebar and topbar for the lifetime of the session purely to tick the expiry countdown down.
  - **`terms.html`**, a legally operative document, described a product that no longer exists: Stripe named as Merchant of Record and payment gateway, a PCI-DSS Level 1 badge, "Buy Me a Coffee" voluntary contributions, and §7.3 capping the Operator's liability at "the total amount actually paid by the User … for the specific access pass" — a cap on a sum that is always zero. Section 4 is rewritten as an explicit statement that no payment surface exists, §7.3 is re-anchored to a free service with the non-excludable heads of liability preserved, "General Conditions of Sale" becomes "of Use" (nothing is sold), and the effective date moves to 2026-09-26 since the agreement was materially amended.
  - **Guard test** (`tests/payments_removed.test.js`, 15 assertions): no payment function or Stripe helper on disk, none declared in `config.toml` or the deploy hook, no payment token anywhere in hand-written `src/`, no read-only lock or periodic re-render left in the shell, the guild tab still assigns server numbers and still deletes guilds, the migration repairs the cron *before* the drop and reloads the PostgREST schema cache, and the terms page no longer sells anything.
  - **Documentation**: corrected the Edge Function inventory in `ARCHITECTURE.md` and `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md` to the verified 14, replaced the false `gm-revolut-webhook` warning box with the standing invariant that **every deployed function must have its source versioned in the repo**, and removed the deleted functions from `docs/DEPLOY_RUNBOOK.md` and the `pre-push` deploy list.
  - **Quality & Verification**: `npm run type-check` (0 TS errors), **135 Vitest suites green (1,515 tests)**, production build clean, **29/29 Playwright e2e** against the real production bundle.

- **Security: Credential Column Exposure, Over-Broad Function Grants & CSP Wildcard (unreleased)**
  - **[CRITICAL] `public.accounts` credential columns were readable by every guild admin** (`supabase/migrations/20260926080000_restrict_accounts_column_grants.sql`):
    - `authenticated` held a **table-wide** `GRANT SELECT ON public.accounts`. Combined with the `gm_accounts_select` policy (which by design lets a `guild_admin` read every account of their own guild), any guild admin could `GET /rest/v1/accounts?select=*` and exfiltrate `password_hash` (bcrypt), `password_enc` (`pgp_sym_encrypt` of the **plaintext** password) and `gotrue_secret_enc` for every member of their guild.
    - Measured on production at audit time: **172/172 accounts carried a non-null `password_enc`**, and **98/172 had no bcrypt `password_hash` at all** — those accounts rely solely on the reversible form.
    - Replaced with a column-level allowlist: `id, auth_user_id, status, guild, role, uid, server_number, created_at`. The allowlist is the union of (a) the columns read by RLS policy subqueries that execute as the caller, verified against `pg_policies`, and (b) the columns actually selected by direct client PostgREST reads.
    - Withheld: `password_hash`, `password_enc`, `gotrue_secret_enc`, `failed_login_count`, `last_login_at`, `locked_until`, `session_version`. Verified zero references to any of the seven in `src/` outside generated types.
    - No policy and no function changed: RLS still decides *which rows*; this narrows *which columns*. `SECURITY DEFINER` functions execute as the function owner and never consult these grants, so `auth-login`, `admin-accounts`, `member-portal` and every `gm_*` RPC are unaffected.
  - **[CRITICAL] Anonymous cross-tenant push registration via `save_push_subscription`** (`supabase/migrations/20260926080100_revoke_overreaching_function_grants.sql`):
    - The function was granted to `anon` and fell back to `coalesce(v_guild, 'ALPHA')` when `auth.uid()` is NULL, so an unauthenticated caller could register their own Web Push endpoint into tenant `ALPHA` and receive that guild's event reminders. `p_pseudo` was written verbatim (impersonation) and `ON CONFLICT (endpoint) DO UPDATE` allowed rewriting a victim's row.
    - This is the exact bug class a previous release claims to have purged ("purged all hardcoded tenant fallbacks `COALESCE(..., 'ALPHA')`") — the function was re-created afterwards and reintroduced it.
    - Revoked `EXECUTE` from `anon`, `authenticated` and `PUBLIC`. Web Push is already decommissioned end to end, so nothing regresses; the function and its table are dropped outright in the Web Push removal phase.
  - **Destructive maintenance functions were callable by unprivileged roles**:
    - `gm_cleanup_expired_ocr_cache()` was executable by **`anon`** (verified via `has_function_privilege`) — an unconditional `DELETE` on `ocr_cache`, i.e. anyone holding the public publishable key could wipe the cache and force re-billing of Gemini Vision calls.
    - `gm_reset_demo_tenant_data()` was executable by **`authenticated`** — any logged-in member of any guild could wipe and rebuild the public DEMO tenant on demand.
    - Both revoked from `anon`, `authenticated` and `PUBLIC`. Verified safe: all five `pg_cron` jobs run as `postgres`, not as `authenticated`/`anon`.
  - **CSP: removed the `https://*.supabase.co` wildcard from `script-src` and `connect-src`** (`vercel.json`):
    - Any Supabase project can serve attacker-controlled JavaScript from a public Storage bucket, so the wildcard trusted **every Supabase project on the internet** and would have turned an otherwise inert HTML injection into script execution. The app loads no external script at all (`index.html` has a single `<script type="module">` and zero inline scripts).
    - `script-src` is now exactly `'self'`; Supabase is pinned to this project's host in `connect-src`.
  - **Removed two tests that locked the CSP weakness in**: `tests/cdn_packaging_csp_hardening.test.js` and `tests/security_headers_audit.test.js` both asserted `expect(csp).toContain('https://*.supabase.co')`. They now assert the pinned host **and** `expect(csp).not.toContain('*.supabase.co')`, so the wildcard cannot silently return.
  - **Quality & Verification**:
    - `npm run type-check` (0 TS errors), 134 Vitest suites green (1,504 tests passed), production build clean.
    - Column allowlist, function privileges and cron job ownership were each verified against the live production database before the migrations were written, not inferred from the migration chain.

- **Removal: Web Push and the PWA, in full (unreleased)**
  - **Why the whole PWA and not just push**: the service worker had no `fetch` handler and never touched the Cache Storage API (a deliberate invariant, so the Supabase API could never be served stale). With push decommissioned it therefore provided no offline capability, no caching and no notifications -- only installability. Nothing was left to keep.
  - **The service worker is now a self-destructing tombstone, not a deleted file** (`src/sw/service-worker.ts`, 456 bytes compiled):
    - Deleting `sw.js` would NOT have uninstalled anything: a worker already installed on a member's device keeps running forever, independently of the page that registered it. The file therefore keeps being served at `/sw.js`, but its only behaviour is `registration.unregister()` -- on the next in-scope navigation the browser re-fetches the script, sees the bytes changed, installs it, and it removes its own registration. That update check is driven by the browser, not the page, which is why it still works with all registration code deleted.
    - It also purges Cache Storage defensively, so a stale cached `index.html` cannot outlive the worker.
    - Reloads open tabs via `clients.navigate()` (best-effort) so they stop being controlled immediately rather than at their next navigation.
    - **Must keep shipping for at least one full release cycle** before `src/sw/`, the esbuild block in `scripts/build.js` and the Vite dev plugin are deleted, so every returning device has a chance to pick it up.
  - **Client removals**: `src/modules/push/PushService.ts` (already a set of empty method bodies), `src/core/pwa/pwa.ts`, the `GM_PUSH` and `GM.pwa` bridges, `initPWA()`, `pushService.init()`, `manifest.webmanifest` and its `<link rel="manifest">` in both `index.html` and `terms.html`.
  - **Removed the whole dead push-preferences chain**, which spanned four layers and had zero callers at the top: `PortalView` (no UI left) -> `portal.service.ts` `getPushPrefs`/`setPushPrefs` -> `member-portal` actions `get-push-prefs`/`set-push-prefs` + `SetPushPrefsSchema` -> RPCs `gm_get_push_prefs`/`gm_set_push_prefs`.
  - **Deleted 612 KB of install-only PNG icons**: `icon-192.png`, `icon-512.png`, `icon-192-maskable.png`, `icon-512-maskable.png`, `apple-touch-icon.png`. Only the manifest and the iOS install flow consumed them, so deleting beats the compression pass the audit had proposed. `favicon.ico` and `favicon.png` are kept -- they are not PWA assets.
  - **Database** (`supabase/migrations/20260926120000_drop_web_push.sql`): dropped `push_subscriptions` (7 rows) and `player_push_prefs` (21 rows) plus `save_push_subscription`, `gm_get_push_prefs` and `gm_set_push_prefs`. `push_subscriptions` also came off the `db-guard` tenant-table list.
  - **Fallout repair, found only by scanning the live database** (`20260926120100` + `20260926120200`): PL/pgSQL resolves table references at EXECUTION time, so the drop succeeded silently while leaving two functions broken.
    - `gm_reset_demo_tenant_data()` deleted from **both** dropped tables. It runs on the `daily-demo-tenant-reset` pg_cron job at 03:00 UTC, so the public DEMO tenant would have silently stopped resetting. Repaired by rewriting the live definition with both statements stripped, guarded so it fails loudly rather than silently no-op'ing. Verified by executing it: `{ok: true, weeks_seeded: 5, members_seeded: 60}`.
    - The first repair migration was incomplete because its diagnostic used `regexp_match`, which returns only the FIRST match per function, and its guard asserted on one table name. `20260926120200` completes it and asserts on both. The incomplete migration was deliberately **not** rewritten in place -- it had already been applied, and editing an applied migration is the repo/database drift this audit exists to remove.
    - `gm_guild_benchmark()` selected from `push_subscriptions` and exposed a `push_subs` column. It has zero callers anywhere (the Portal's "Guild Benchmarks" tiles are an unrelated client-side computation), so it was dropped rather than repaired -- one of the dead RPCs left by the removed Settings/matchup tab.
  - **Regenerated `src/types/database.generated.ts`** from the live schema. It had gone stale, which in turn was masking dead `E2eeMessage` / `E2eeConversation` aliases in `src/types/database.ts` for Leader Hall tables dropped back in September. That file's header promises a dropped table "fails the build here instead of drifting silently" -- it did, once the generated types were current.
  - **Fixed the update banner** (`version-check.ts`): clicking "Later" left `newVersionBannerShown` at `true` for the lifetime of the tab, so `checkForNewVersion()` short-circuited forever and a user who dismissed the prompt once was never offered the update again. It now re-arms.
  - **Replaced the offline toast, which was lying** (`src/core/net/network-status.ts`): the old copy promised "Local changes will sync when online", but there is no write queue and no background sync anywhere in the app -- offline writes are simply lost. It now says changes will not be saved until the connection returns.
  - **Added the `Cache-Control` headers `vercel.json` never had**: `immutable` for the content-hashed `/assets/*`, `no-cache` for HTML and `/sw.js`, `no-store` for `/version.json`. With no service worker, freshness rests entirely on the CDN, and nothing in the repo pinned it.
  - **Tests**: deleted `push_view.test.js` and `pwa_icons.test.js`; rewrote `service-worker.test.js` to lock in the tombstone (unregisters, no push pipeline, purges but never populates caches, nothing registers a worker, manifest and icons absent) instead of asserting the push handlers exist; updated `app_cutover`, `portal_matrix` and `portal_view`, inverting the `SetPushPrefsSchema` presence assertion into an absence one.
  - **Quality & Verification**: `npm run type-check` 0 errors, 132 Vitest suites green (1,500 tests), production build clean, and **all 29 Playwright e2e tests pass** against the real production bundle -- including the two rewritten boot specs asserting `/sw.js` still serves the tombstone and that the app registers no worker.

- **Cleanup: dead client state, an unreachable config path, and four divergent HTML escapers (unreleased)**
  - **Unified HTML escaping into one boundary.** There were **four** implementations with **three** different behaviours: `core/utils/text.ts` (escaped `` ` ``, stringified nullish), `core/api/supabase.ts` (identical but returned `''` for nullish), `StatsView.ts` (a local copy that silently **did not escape the backtick**), and `fetch-errors.ts`'s `escapeAttr` (did not escape the apostrophe, so it was only correct inside double-quoted attributes).
    - Worse, `window.GM.escapeHTML` was assigned **twice in sequence** — `main.ts` set the `supabase.ts` version, then `installGmBridge()` immediately overwrote it with the `text.ts` one — so which behaviour the whole legacy bridge got depended purely on load order. That is exactly the "silent, load-order-dependent regression" the old comment in `supabase.ts` warned about, and it was actually happening.
    - There is now a single implementation in `core/utils/text.ts`, re-exported from `core/api/supabase.ts` so existing import sites keep working. It keeps the backtick escape and adopts the safer nullish behaviour: **nullish renders as `''`, never the literal word `"null"`**, matching the convention `formatNumber` already followed. `StatsView`'s divergent copy and `escapeAttr` now both delegate.
  - **Deleted the dead reactive store** (`src/core/store/store.ts`, 86 lines). `appStore` had exactly two references, both in `main.ts` (the import and `window.GM.store = appStore`), and `subscribe()` was never called by any module. Nothing read `window.GM.store` either.
  - **Removed the unreachable Supabase env configuration path** (`getSupabaseConfig`, `validateSupabaseEnv`, and the `createClient` fallback inside `getSupabaseClient`). `main.ts` deliberately builds the client from the compile-time constants ("never `import.meta.env`") and publishes it as `window.GM.db`, so the fallback never ran — and had it run, **the client it produced would have bypassed `installDbWriteGuard`**, silently defeating the multi-tenant write guard. `getSupabaseClient()` now returns the guarded client or `null`, never a fabricated one.
    - `.env.example` no longer advertises `VITE_SUPABASE_URL` / `VITE_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, which nothing read, and documents why: the URL and publishable key are compile-time constants by design, so rotating the key is a code change. `VITE_SENTRY_DSN` is genuinely used and stays.
    - Rewrote `tests/supabase_env.test.js`, whose premise ("Absence of Sensitive Hardcoded Keys") was obsolete on two counts: the key is a *publishable* key designed to ship to browsers and protected by RLS, and the env path it asserted could never execute. It now locks in the real contract, plus a new guard that fails if any *secret* pattern (`sb_secret_`, `sk_live/test_`, `whsec_`, a signed JWT, a service-role key) ever appears anywhere in `src/`.
  - **Corrected six comments that asserted `app.js` is still loaded** (`main.ts`, `portal.controller.ts`). They claimed the classic script "still loads and sets these at parse time", that form wiring was left unbound because "app.js already binds those forms => would double-submit", and that app.js "keeps owning the live roster" — all false since the Strangler cutover, and all liable to make a maintainer leave working code unwired. Purely historical references ("ported from app.js#...") were kept.
  - **Two corrections to the audit's own findings**, both established by reading the files rather than trusting the report:
    - `tests/reactivity.test.js` does **not** test the dead `appStore` — it covers `debounce`/`runInChunks`, the Stats/History TTL freshness gating, cache invalidation on member mutations, request batching and the build pipeline. It is a valuable suite guarding behaviour the performance phase will change, and was **kept**.
    - `tests/env_validation.test.js` is about the **Edge Functions'** Zod env validator (`_shared/env.ts`), not the client env path, and was left untouched.
  - **Dropped the database surface of removed features** (`supabase/migrations/20260926140000_drop_dead_rpcs_and_diplomacy.sql`): the diplomacy feature (`diplomatic_treaties`, 0 rows, no UI, plus its three RPCs), which had kept `gm_is_leader_caller()` alive as the last Leader Hall residue; the four Draft Mercato / SvS / GvG matchup RPCs whose Settings tab was deleted; `get_push_config()`, a Web Push leftover; and the legacy `list_event_sessions` / `list_event_weeks` duplicates. Also revoked HTTP `EXECUTE` (including via `PUBLIC`) on two trigger functions and one maintenance function that were reachable at `/rest/v1/rpc/`.
    - **Scope was established by measurement, not by assumption**: every function in `public` was cross-referenced against RLS policies, other function bodies, `cron.job` and `pg_trigger`, then against callers in `src/`/`supabase/functions/`. That overturned two entries on the original list — `transfer_guild_member` and `request_guild_transfer` are **live** (the client calls the legacy name at `members.controller.ts:722`, and `member-portal` calls the other at `index.ts:684`) — and showed the apparent `gm_list_event_sessions` → `list_event_sessions` dependency to be a substring artefact of its own CREATE header.
    - Verified after applying, the check that the Web Push removal taught: no remaining function references any dropped object, the three live lookalikes are intact, and all three revocations took effect.
  - **Deleted four orphaned design-system primitives** (`Checkbox`, `Input`, `Switch`, `Table`, 481 lines) imported by nothing but their own tests, while `Badge`/`Dialog`/`Toast`/`Tooltip` from the same batch are in real use. Their `gm-ui-*` CSS stays: those classes **are** applied, by hand-written markup in `index.html`, `HistoryView` and `EventsView` that duplicated what the primitives generated.
  - **Removed 80 fully-dead CSS rules** (~10 KB raw, −8.7 KB from the built bundle, −1.55 KB gzip) across `styles.css`, `components.css` and `shell.css`.
    - The first attempt was wrong and was reverted. A naive "class never appears" scan flagged 245 classes, and removing them broke three regression tests, which exposed two systematic defects: it deleted `.gm-skeleton-avatar`, one member of a utility family whose five siblings are in use, and it deleted the base `.confirm-card {}` rule while `.confirm-overlay.visible .confirm-card`, `.confirm-card h3` and `.confirm-card p` survived — **the stylesheet is itself a consumer**, so a class named in any compound selector is not dead.
    - The corrected analysis adds four filters: test files join the corpus (they encode CSS contracts), prefixes/suffixes that are concatenated or interpolated at runtime are excluded, a utility family with any live sibling is kept whole, and any class referenced by a compound selector elsewhere in the CSS is protected. That cut 245 candidates to **89**, and only rules whose selectors are *entirely* dead were removed — grouped rules mixing dead and live selectors were left alone.
    - Verified: 133→0 used classes lost (zero), `@keyframes`/`@font-face`/`@media`/`:root` counts unchanged, braces balanced in every file, and the built login page checked visually in a browser.
    - Worth stating plainly: this is a modest win. The real CSS weight is the icon fonts (310 KB of Phosphor CSS for 3,043 glyphs, of which 56 duotone icons are used) — that is a separate, much larger lever.
  - **Quality & Verification**: `npm run type-check` 0 errors, 132 Vitest suites green (1,472 tests), production build clean, and all 29 Playwright e2e tests pass against the real production bundle.

- **Performance: icon and font consolidation — ~5.6 MB off the first load (unreleased)**
  - **Material Symbols Rounded was a 5.4 MB font download for 64 icons.** The `<link>` in `index.html` and `terms.html` requested the full variable font across every axis (`opsz,wght,FILL,GRAD@20..48,100..700,0..1,-50..200`); `content-length` on the `woff2` Google serves for it is **5,399,596 bytes**. It was also a render-blocking third-party stylesheet on the critical path. The audit had estimated this at 150–200 KB — it was measured, not estimated, and it is by a wide margin the single largest performance defect found anywhere in this codebase.
    - All 64 usages were migrated to Phosphor. 48 ligature→icon mappings were built and **validated against the package's actual 1,530-icon list** before use, rather than guessed.
    - `ShellView`'s five dynamic sites were straightforward: its nav and event definitions already carried a Phosphor `icon` field alongside `m3Icon`, so the migration was switching to the field that was already there.
    - Removed a dual-path leftover in the password-visibility toggle that maintained both an `<i class="ph">` and a Material `<span>`, and migrated the Stats podium's rank ternary (`emoji_events`/`military_tech`/`workspace_premium` → `ph-trophy`/`ph-medal`/`ph-seal-check`).
    - CSS rules were **converted, not deleted**, where they targeted `.material-symbols-rounded` alone (e.g. `.portal-military-tile-footer`) — deleting them would have silently unstyled the elements. Only rules already pairing with `.ph`, or specific to Material's variable-font axes, were dropped.
  - **Dropped the Phosphor duotone family**: 231 KB of CSS and a 164 KB `woff2` for the 55 occurrences that used it, every one of which exists in the regular set.
  - **Fixed seven icon names that do not exist in Phosphor and were therefore rendering as nothing**: `ph-bolt`, `ph-brightness-high`, `ph-lock-keyhole`, `ph-shield-crown`, `ph-swords`, `ph-user-clock`, `ph-webhook`. These were pre-existing bugs, unrelated to this migration, found by validating every `ph-*` name in the codebase against the package. All 176 distinct icon names now resolve.
    - `ph-swords` → `ph-sword` created a collision with the Power OCR scanner, so the Fleet scanner was given `ph-shooting-star` to stay distinguishable.
  - **Trimmed the Phosphor `@font-face` via a small Vite plugin** (`vite.config.ts`). The package declares four sources (woff2/woff/ttf/svg) and Vite resolved all of them, emitting **3.97 MB** of `woff`, `ttf` and `svg` — the SVG alone is 2.9 MB — that no browser ever downloads, since woff2 is first and universally supported. The plugin rewrites the rule to woff2 only and changes `font-display: block` to `swap`: `block` meant every `.ph` icon rendered as *nothing* (not a fallback glyph) until the 147 KB font arrived. Rewriting the stylesheet beats vendoring a copy, since the 1,530 icon rules stay owned by the package.
  - **Dropped Space Grotesk** from the Google Fonts request (4 weights). It was listed only as a second fallback behind Plus Jakarta Sans in `--font-display`, and both came from the *same* request — if one failed to load so did the other, so it could never render.
  - **Measured result**

    | | Before | After |
    |---|---|---|
    | CSS bundle (raw) | 437.91 kB | **240.72 kB** |
    | CSS bundle (gzip) | 68.59 kB | **42.13 kB** |
    | Font assets emitted to `dist` | 6.9 MB | **144 kB** |
    | Icon font actually downloaded | 311 kB | **147 kB** |
    | Material Symbols download | 5.4 MB | **0** |
    | Google font families / weights | 4 / 17 | 3 / 13 |

  - **Quality & Verification**: `npm run type-check` 0 errors, 132 Vitest suites green (1,472 tests), all 29 Playwright e2e green, and the built login page checked visually in a browser — it exercises six former Material ligatures (`person_add`, `shield`, `lock`, `visibility`, `login`, `fingerprint`) and every one renders as a real glyph.
  - Five tests that pinned the old icon markup were updated, including one now asserting that the duotone import does **not** return.

- **Security: credential integrity, ahead of the Discord authentication migration (unreleased)**
  - Phase D1 of the move to "Sign in with Discord". Contains **no Discord code** and ships on its own, but must land first: the account-claim flow will reuse the password check as a one-time proof of ownership, so a broken password check would mean a broken claim.
  - **[HIGH] After an admin reset the password, the OLD password kept working — indefinitely.** `gm_reset_account_password` and `gm_admin_upsert` wrote only `password_enc`, never `password_hash`, while the login checks `password_hash` **first**. Both now write both columns, and a reset also clears `failed_login_count` / `locked_until` (an admin resetting a locked-out user plainly means to let them back in). Confirmed against the live database before and after.
  - **Fixing that naively would have been far worse than the bug, and the guard matters more than the fix.** [session.ts:225-280](src/core/auth/session.ts:225) calls `gm_admin_upsert` with an **empty password** as a role-sync fallback. Adding `password_hash = crypt(p_password, …)` without a guard would have turned every role change into "set this account's password to the empty string" — and `gm_verify_password` would have accepted it, because `crypt('', hash) = hash`. That converts a latent "old password still works" defect into an active "empty password works" hole. An empty password now leaves **both** credential columns untouched, and creating a brand-new account with no credential raises `password_required`.
  - **Extracted the password verification into `gm_verify_password`**; `gm_check_login` becomes a one-line delegation. One implementation, two honest names — a function called "check_login" invoked by something that is explicitly *not* a login is how a second, divergent copy gets written six months later. It also gained a fail-closed branch: an account whose credentials were destroyed by a future Discord claim (both columns NULL) is rejected outright rather than falling through to a comparison.
  - **Removed a `pgp_sym_decrypt` from every single Player Portal request.** `member-portal`'s `getIdentity` read `player_uid` from `gm_get_shadow`, which does not return that column — the value was always `undefined` and resolution always fell through to `acc.uid || accountId`, after paying for one extra round trip and one decryption of the shadow GoTrue secret. The call is gone; behaviour is identical.
  - **Verified by executing the real functions on production inside a rolled-back transaction**, not by reading the SQL: creation writes both columns; an empty-password role sync leaves the old password working and both columns intact; an empty password is refused; after an admin reset the old password is refused and the new one accepted; and an account with both columns NULL is refused. Zero residue afterwards, 172 accounts untouched.
  - **Quality & Verification**: `npm run type-check` 0 errors, 133 Vitest suites green (1,488 tests, +16 in the new `tests/credential_integrity.test.js`), production build clean, 29/29 Playwright e2e green.
  - Baseline worth recording for the migration: **98 of 172 accounts have no bcrypt `password_hash` at all** and depend entirely on the reversible `password_enc` path, which `gm_verify_password` preserves.

- **Performance: view teardown, derived-cache invalidation and the OCR UID pre-check (unreleased)**
  - **Seven views define a `destroy()` and not one was ever called.** Everything they allocate leaked for the whole session. The router now tears down the view it navigates away from, via a declarative `TEARDOWN_BY_TAB` map:
    - **Migration Hub** held two Realtime WebSocket channels. Worse than a leak: its page handler listened for `event: '*'` and re-ran a full `select('*')` of the wiki table on any remote change, so with *n* admins editing, each edit cost *n* full refetches — **including for the n−1 users sitting on a completely different tab**.
    - **Overview** held two `setInterval`s (event countdowns and timezone clocks) that kept firing and touching the DOM of a hidden tab forever.
    - **Sanctions** held a `rad-lang-change` window listener.
    - Deliberately **not** torn down: `ShellView` and `HelpView` are global chrome, not tabs — destroying them on a tab switch would dismantle the navigation and the help popover. Both torn-down views re-arm their resources inside `load()`, so destroy-then-load is safe and idempotent, and switching between aliases of the same view (`overview` / `gm-overview`) does not tear anything down.
  - **Debounced the Migration Hub's Realtime reload** (800 ms), so a burst of edits collapses into one refetch. The guard matters: `debounce` exposes no cancel, so a reload scheduled just before navigating away would otherwise fire **after** `destroy()` and re-subscribe the very channels it had just removed — reintroducing the leak. It now checks `realtimeChannel`, which is precisely the "still mounted" condition.
  - **55 write call sites across four views, and not one invalidated the Stats/History freshness markers.** Editing a war score and switching to Stats within the 15 s TTL showed the pre-edit numbers with no indication they were stale. Rather than wiring 55 sites by hand — which would have drifted — the invalidation lives in **`installDbWriteGuard`**, the one path every tenant write must pass through. It covers `event_participants`, `event_daily_scores`, `war_sessions`, `sanctions` and `weekly_scores`, and invalidates per-guild so one tenant's write never purges another's cache.
    - Invalidation happens when the write is *issued*, not when it resolves: that avoids wrapping the returned builder (whose chain is delicate — `.eq()` is appended after `.delete()`), and the failure mode is harmless, since a write that later fails has merely dropped a freshness marker.
  - **The OCR UID pre-check made three serial round trips per player.** A 100-player roster import meant 200–300 **serialised** requests behind a "Checking UIDs…" label — roughly 16–24 s of frozen UI, growing linearly with the batch. The ban check is now one query for the whole batch (`.in('uid', targetUids)`) and the collision probes run 10 at a time through the same `runInChunks` helper this file already used in five other places. Same results, same per-UID error tolerance.
  - **Two of the audit's findings were corrected by checking rather than trusting them:**
    - *"The cache guard is placed before `render()`, so returning to a tab within 15 s produces no render at all."* Tabs are switched by toggling an `active` class on `.tab-panel` — **the DOM persists**, so the previous render stays visible and the guard is correct behaviour. The audit itself hedged here ("I could not fully trace that"). Verifying it avoided a risky restructuring of the 1,274-line `StatsView`.
    - *"Unbounded history queries are the first thing to break."* Measured on production: the worst guild downloads **879 rows** (317 squad + 562 participation), not the extrapolated 10,400/year. It is a slow-burn problem, not an emergency — and since the aggregation computes a **lifetime** participation rate, bounding it by date would change the business meaning. Left alone deliberately; the correct fix is to move the aggregation into SQL, which is its own piece of work.
  - **Quality & Verification**: `npm run type-check` 0 errors, 134 Vitest suites green (1,508 tests, +20 in the new `tests/view_teardown_and_cache.test.js` plus an OCR batching guard), production build clean, 29/29 Playwright e2e green.

## [3.20.2] - 2026-09-24

- **Bug Fix & Ergonomics: War Manual Score Saving & History Modal Table Scrolling (V3.20.2)**
  - **Manual Score Saving on Active Events (SvS & GvG)**:
    - Fixed empty string score values in participant rows evaluating to `null` via `legacy.parseNumber()`, which triggered PostgreSQL RPC `public.gm_upsert_daily_scores` input validation failure (`invalid_input`).
    - Hardened score parsing to strictly emit valid non-null numbers (`Number(legacy.parseNumber(v) || 0)`), ensuring blank/unplayed days are omitted or safely zeroed without failing the entire participant batch.
    - Wired `change` event listeners alongside 700ms debounced `input` events on all score inputs across `EventsView.ts`, ensuring immediate save triggers on Enter/blur.
    - Added user feedback toasts: `legacy.t('war_save_daily_success')` ("Daily scores saved.") on successful row save, and explicit error notifications on RPC failures.
    - Automatically synced row participation checkboxes and in-memory participant state (`participated = 1`) whenever a positive score is saved.
  - **History Modal Column Synchronization & Horizontal Scrolling**:
    - Decoupled participant status cells (`col_participated`, `late`, `excused`, `sub_present`, `appointed`) from score cells in `HistoryView.ts`. Fixed granular war sessions missing the `participated` cell, resolving the 1-column shift where Day 1 scores misaligned under the "Participated" header.
    - Expanded `.gm-ui-dialog-lg` from `860px` to `1080px` (`width: 95vw`), allowing wide 9-column war tables to display naturally on desktop viewports.
    - Constrained `.gm-ui-dialog-body .gm-table-scroll` to `max-height: 52vh; overflow: auto;`, pinning the horizontal scrollbar directly above the modal footer where it remains permanently accessible regardless of row count.
    - Added sticky table headers (`thead th` with `position: sticky; top: 0; z-index: 2; background: var(--card);`) preventing text bleed-through during vertical scrolling.
    - Added responsive `data-label` attributes across all granular and status table cells.
  - **Quality & Verification**:
    - Added comprehensive regression suite in `tests/war_manual_score_entry_and_history_scroll.test.js` (4/4 tests green).
    - Full quality gate validated: `npm run type-check` (0 TS errors), 134 Vitest suites green (1,504 tests passed), production build clean.

## [3.20.1] - 2026-09-23

- **Bug Fix & Hardening: Discord Profile Verification — Multi-Guild Routing, Mention Suppression & Zero-Clutter Channel Cleanup (V3.20.1)**
  - **Mention & Role Ping Suppression**:
    - Eliminated raw role tags `<@&role_id>` in bot messages across all verification flows; converted to plain bold role labels (`**${role.name}**`).
    - Enforced `allowed_mentions: { parse: [] }` on channel message payloads sent via Discord REST API, preventing accidental `@everyone`, `@here`, role mentions, and member notification spam.
  - **Multi-Tenant Guild Verification Routing**:
    - Replaced single-channel guild constraint with dynamic `resolveTagToGuild()` lookup across configured server tenants (`[PR1M]` -> `ALPHA`, `[OMG]` -> `OMEGA`, `[IMK]` -> `IMK`).
    - Verified players receive their respective guild's Discord role, clean `[TAG] Pseudo #Server` nickname, and automatic roster insertion/updates.
    - Added automated stripping of the `VISITOR` role when a member transitions to a verified guild role.
  - **Strict Visitor Tagging for External Guilds**:
    - Any non-member or external alliance tag (e.g. `[SB]`, etc.) is strictly tagged with `[VISITOR]` nickname prefix (`[VISITOR] <pseudo> #<serverNumber>`) and assigned the `VISITOR` role without altering guild rosters.
  - **Calibrated Gemini Vision OCR**:
    - Refined OCR system prompt with strict verbatim bracket matching instructions to prevent model hallucinations from misreading external guild tags as `IMK`.
    - Added automated extraction of bottom-screen server numbers (`#<digits>`).
  - **Automated `#verification` Channel Cleanup**:
    - Player screenshot uploads are scheduled for auto-deletion after 5 seconds (15 seconds on OCR failure).
    - Bot verification replies are scheduled for auto-deletion after 15 seconds via persistent worker timers and `EdgeRuntime.waitUntil` fallback, maintaining a pristine, zero-clutter channel.
  - **Verification & Test Coverage**:
    - Updated regression suite in `tests/discord_verification.test.js` (27/27 tests green).
    - Full quality gate validated: `npm run type-check` (0 TS errors), 133 Vitest suites green (1,500 tests passed), production build clean.

## [3.20.0] - 2026-09-19

- **Feature: Migration Hub — Collaborative Intel Wiki, Compressed Screenshots & Gemini AI Summaries (V3.20.0)** — empowers guild and server leadership to track, analyze, and coordinate inter-server migrations with a dedicated high-end intel wiki.
  - **Dual-Scope Wiki Architecture (Guild & Server Intel)**:
    - Created `public.migration_wiki_pages` table (`20260920000000_migration_hub_wiki.sql`) with dual scopes: `guild` (alliance-level confidential diplomacy) and `server` (cross-alliance state leadership and war coordination).
    - Enforced Zero-Trust Row Level Security (`FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`) with `gm_can_access_server_wiki(text)` security definer function and tenant-isolated policies preventing cross-tenant data leakage.
  - **Rich Markdown Editor & Client-Side Image Compression**:
    - Implemented `src/core/utils/image-compressor.ts` utilizing HTML5 Canvas for proportional resizing (max 1440px) and WebP/JPEG conversion (0.78 quality), shrinking 4–10MB screenshots to ~80–180KB with drag-and-drop and `Ctrl+V` clipboard paste.
    - Integrated client-side XSS sanitization (`src/modules/migration/views/MigrationHubView.ts`) with custom lightweight Markdown parsing (headers, lists, blockquotes, code blocks, bold/italic, tables), attachment galleries, and full-screen image lightbox.
  - **Gemini AI Intel Summaries & Executive Briefings**:
    - Deployed `supabase/functions/migration-summary/index.ts` Edge Function powered by Gemini 2.5 Flash (`google-genai` SDK).
    - Supports two operational modes: single-page concise executive summaries and scope-wide intel briefings synthesizing active discussions, incoming players, risks, and recommended actions.
    - Prompt Injection Defense: raw wiki content is isolated inside JSON data blocks with strict administrative system instructions.
  - **Realtime Sync & Discord Integration**:
    - Supabase Realtime channel subscription updates the wiki page list and active notes live across all connected leadership sessions.
    - One-click Discord exporter formats wiki pages into clean Markdown ready for announcement channels.
  - **Verification**: `npm run type-check` (0 TS errors), 130 Vitest suites green (1,464 tests passed), production build clean.

## [3.19.0] - 2026-09-19

- **Feature: Robust IANA Timezone Engine & Automated Overview Clocks** — introduces Daylight Saving Time (DST) resilient timezone management and automatic officer clocks across the platform.
  - **Database Migration**: added `timezone text` column to `public.guild_members` (`20260919190000_member_iana_timezone.sql`) and updated `gm_update_player_timezone` RPC with `SECURITY DEFINER` and Zero-Trust service-role permissions.
  - **Core Timezone Engine**: created `src/utils/timezone.ts` utilizing native `Intl.DateTimeFormat` for live UTC offset calculation, browser auto-detection, local time formatting, and curated regional timezone groupings.
  - **Admin Roster & Player Portal**: added regional timezone selector with "Auto-detect" button in `showEditMemberDialog` and the Player Portal settings card, updating live badges and coverage histograms without modifying player pseudos.
  - **Command Center Automation**: upgraded `OverviewView.ts` and `overview.service.ts` to automatically populate timezone clocks for active R4/R5 officers, with real-time ticker and "Auto-sync with Officers" control.
  - **Testing & Verification**: added 17 unit tests in `tests/timezone.test.js` covering DST shifts, half-hour offsets, and officer clock generation; 130/130 Vitest suites green (1,463 tests).

- **Platform Overhaul: Comprehensive Audit Remediation & Modernization (V3.19.0)** — full execution of the 18-ticket backlog derived from the September 2026 comprehensive audit of the FGF Guild Management Tool production PWA, strengthening security, database performance, client observability, accessibility, and architectural resilience.
  - **Security & Zero-Trust Row Level Security (RLS)**:
    - `[TICKET-01] [SEC-01]`: Applied `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` across all 26 active public Postgres tables (`20260920100000_force_rls_all_tables.sql`), guaranteeing that table owners (`postgres`) and background scripts cannot bypass tenant isolation policies.
    - `[TICKET-02] [SEC-02]`: Harmonized `normalizeRole()` to canonical roles only (`super_admin`, `server_admin`, `guild_admin`, `member`) across `src/core/auth/roles.ts` and `src/core/utils/text.ts`, eliminating ambiguous legacy fallbacks.
    - `[TICKET-03] [SEC-03]`: Hardened HTML injection defense across 15 views and helpers with static imports of `escapeHTML()` / `esc()`, preventing XSS on player nicknames and user inputs.
    - `[TICKET-09] [SEC-05]`: Hardened role resolution to prioritize verified GoTrue JWT claims in memory over unverified `localStorage` data across `supabase.ts`, `auth.controller.ts`, `gm-bridge.ts`, and `router.ts`.
    - `[TICKET-17] [SEC-06]`: Upgraded HTTP security headers in `vercel.json` with strict `Strict-Transport-Security` (HSTS 2 years + preload), `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy` (COOP `same-origin`), `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy` (CORP `same-origin`), and CSP `upgrade-insecure-requests;`.
  - **Performance & Database Optimization**:
    - `[TICKET-06] [PERF-01]`: Rescheduled `fgf-event-reminders` pg_cron job to 5-minute ticks (`*/5 * * * *`) (`20260920120000_reschedule_event_reminders_cron.sql`) to prevent notification loss and bounded execution windows.
    - `[TICKET-07] [PERF-02]`: Bundled all 5 external CSS stylesheets into Vite via `src/main.ts`, eliminating unminified link tags and duplicate distribution files.
    - `[TICKET-08] [SEC-04]`: Packaged `@phosphor-icons/web` locally via npm, purged external CDN scripts from `index.html`, and removed `cdn.jsdelivr.net` / `unpkg.com` from `vercel.json` CSP.
    - `[TICKET-10] [PERF-03]`: Implemented SHA-256 deduplication cache for Gemini Vision OCR (`public.ocr_cache` with 7-day TTL, <50ms response on cache hits) in `supabase/functions/ocr-guild-members/index.ts`.
    - `[TICKET-16] [PERF-04]`: Added composite foreign key coverage indexes on high-volume tables (`event_participants(guild, pseudo)`, `shadowfront_squads(guild, pseudo)`, `sanctions(guild, pseudo)`, `guild_transfers(source_guild, target_guild, resolved_by)`) and dropped redundant indexes on `event_status` (`20260920140000_fk_indexes_and_perf_optimizations.sql`).
  - **Observability & Error Reporting**:
    - `[TICKET-04] [OBS-01]`: Created client-side error reporting RPC `public.gm_report_client_error` with IP rate limiting and tenant isolation (`20260920110000_client_error_logging_rpc.sql`) wired into `src/core/logger/logger.ts`.
    - `[TICKET-05] [OBS-02]`: Integrated `@sentry/browser` client monitoring in `src/core/monitoring/sentry.ts` with comprehensive PII, password, and JWT token scrubbing.
  - **Accessibility & UX**:
    - `[TICKET-11] [A11Y-01]`: Hardened `src/components/ui/Dialog.ts` to full WCAG 2.2 AA compliance: bidirectional focus trap, `#app[aria-hidden="true"]` background inertness, semantic `dialog`/`alertdialog` roles, and focus restoration on dismiss.
  - **Testing, Quality & Build Hygiene**:
    - `[TICKET-12] [MAINT-01]`: Excluded test configs (`playwright.config.js`, `vitest.config.js`) from `dist/`, purged orphaned files, and archived obsolete maintenance scripts to `scripts/archive/`.
    - `[TICKET-13] [TEST-01]`: Consolidated Playwright E2E testing suite with optimized `serve` command and 3 new end-to-end specifications (Dialog a11y, Custom Message Modal, Session Lifecycle).
    - `[TICKET-14] [TEST-02]`: Hardened `discord-webhook-proxy` Edge Function with IP sliding-window rate limiting (30 req/min) and Discord 429 `Retry-After` propagation, backed by load test suite.
  - **Architecture Documentation**:
    - `[TICKET-15] [DOC-01]`: Authored 5 new Architecture Decision Records (`ADR-010` to `ADR-014`), updated `docs/adr/README.md` index, and modernized `ARCHITECTURE.md`.
    - `[TICKET-18] [REL-01]`: Bumped application version to `v3.19.0`, synchronized `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, and `constants.ts`, and published dual changelogs.
  - **Verification**: `npm run type-check` (0 TS errors), 129 Vitest test suites green (1,446 tests passed), and 100% clean production build.

## [3.18.4]

- **Feature: Video & Screenshot OCR Overhaul — 60-Image Upload Pipeline, Sharpness Keyframing & Zero-Trust Roster Guardrails (V3.18.4)** — modernizes the OCR engine for video screen-recordings and large screenshot batches to eliminate missing players, prevent HTTP 413 upload errors, and safeguard roster integrity.
  - **Video Sharpness & Stillness Keyframing**: in `src/modules/ocr/ocr.service.ts` and `src/modules/app/ocr/ocr.controller.ts`, implemented `ocrMeasureFrameSharpness` (Laplacian gradient variance) and ROI cropping ($y \in [0.16, 0.88]$). Replaced legacy scroll-motion sampling with stillness local-peak detection and scroll windowing (capturing the sharpest frame if scrolling $>1.2$s), eliminating motion-blurred captures and omitted members.
  - **Multi-Frame Fuzzy Player Fusion**: implemented `fuseOcrPlayers(rawPlayers, existingMembers, guildTag)` to deduplicate players across overlapping frames using Levenshtein distance ($\ge 0.80$), preserving highest scores and detected ranks across the batch.
  - **60-Capture Upload Capacity & Client Compression**: raised upload cap from 25 to 60 images (~300+ player capacity). Pre-compresses all screenshots to max 1600px at 82% WebP/JPEG quality in parallel before upload, shrinking raw 4-10MB mobile screenshots to ~180KB and preventing Supabase 8MB payload limit crashes. Batches images in chunks of 2 with concurrency control.
  - **Zero-Trust Partial Scan Guardrail**: in `src/modules/app/ocr/ocr.render.ts`, added automatic scan coverage detection (`coverageRatio < 0.75`). If a partial scan is detected, missing roster members are preserved by default (`checked = false`), labeled with `Kept (Partial Scan)` chips, and an amber alert banner advises the user to prevent accidental mass-deletions.
  - **Gemini 2.5 Flash Prompt Tuning**: updated `ocr-guild-members` Edge Function with `max_output_tokens: 8192` and explicit instructions to scan every visible row from top to bottom, including low/zero scores and Unicode nicknames.
  - **Verification**: `npm run type-check` — 0 TS errors; **1359/1359 Vitest tests green across 114 suites**; production build clean.

- **Fix: Members OCR Runtime TypeError on Rank Validation & Table Colspan Alignment** — resolved the fatal crash preventing Members OCR from parsing guild roster screenshots.
  - **Edge Function Rank Validation Fix**: in `supabase/functions/ocr-guild-members/index.ts`, defined `VALID_RANKS_SET = new Set<string>(VALID_RANKS)` and replaced invalid `VALID_RANKS.has(...)` calls on the `as const` array with `VALID_RANKS_SET.has(...)` for player `rank` and `endingRank`. Deployed the updated Edge Function `ocr-guild-members` to production.
  - **Removed Members Column Alignment**: in `src/modules/app/ocr/ocr.render.ts`, added the missing `<td class="ocr-cell-rank">` badge in removed member rows and updated `colspan` from 5 to 6 on warning and section header rows, ensuring perfect visual alignment with the 6-column Members OCR table header.
  - **Verification & Regression Test**: added unit test in `tests/ocr.test.js` validating that rank parsing uses `VALID_RANKS_SET.has` across all rank tiers; 0 TypeScript errors, **1394/1394 Vitest tests green across 117 test suites**, production build clean.

- **Feature: Automated Discord Server Bootstrap & Dynamic Provisioning Engine (`/setup-server`) (V3.18.4)** — empowers guild leadership to entirely format, configure, and bootstrap a blank Discord server with a standardized FGF operational hierarchy tailored dynamically to their guild name, tag, and game server number.
  - **Dynamic Blueprint & Templating Engine**: `discord-verification-worker/server-provisioner.js` generates fully personalized category names, channel slugs, and roles without hardcoding (e.g. `🔴 ALPHA` -> `📡・alpha-chat`, `🗓️・pr1m-leave-and-absence`, `👑 [PR1M] R5`, `⭐ [PR1M] R4`, `🛡️ [PR1M] Member`, `👽 Visitor`).
  - **Standardized 7-Category Structure**: provisions 7 categories, 28 text channels, and 3 voice rooms covering `🛂 WELCOME` (with OCR verification target), `👽 CROSS SERVER VISITORS`, `GAME UPDATES`, `⌛ EVENT REMINDERS` (Calamity, GvG, Dominion), `🪐 SERVER #<num>` community channels, `🚨 DOMINION WARZONE` (restricted SvS rooms), and `🔴 <GUILD_NAME>` (private HQ).
  - **Zero-Trust Permission Matrices**: strictly isolates `@everyone` and `👽 Visitor` from private guild channels; restricts `#r4-chat` and `R4 VC` to R4 Officers & R5 Leaders; enables attachment permissions in `#verification` so new recruits can submit profile screenshots.
  - **Administrator Safeguards & Interactive Confirmation**: `/setup-server` requires Discord Administrator permissions (`default_member_permissions: 8`) and presents an interactive verification prompt (`[ 🚀 Confirmer et Déployer ]` / `[ ❌ Annuler ]`) with optional `clean_mode: true` to clear default channels safely.
  - **Anti-Rate-Limit Throttling & Live Progress Bar**: sequential execution pipeline with configurable delays protects against Discord API 429 rate limits while updating an interactive progress bar embed (`[████████░░░░░░░░] 50%`) in real time.
  - **Automatic FGF SaaS Wiring**: automatically binds created channels and roles into Supabase `guild_config` (`discord_verification_channel_id`, `discord_audit_channel_id`, `discord_member_role_id`), instantly activating OCR verification and absence alerts without manual ID copying.
  - **Verification**: `npm run type-check` — 0 errors; **1392/1392 Vitest tests green across 117 suites** (+8 new tests in `tests/discord_server_provisioner.test.js`); production build clean.

## [3.18.3]

- **Feature: Interactive Discord Bot Overhaul & Channel Auto-Clean Architecture (V3.18.3)** — comprehensive upgrade to the always-on Gateway worker and Discord slash interaction suite, providing zero-clutter channel verification, leaderboards, real-time war check-ins, and officer tools.
  - **Zero-Clutter Channel Verification & Privacy**: in `discord-verification-worker/index.js`, successful screenshot verifications now auto-delete the uploaded user screenshot after 3 seconds to protect sensitive in-game player details (UID, fleet levels, assets) from public exposure; failures auto-delete after 60 seconds to prevent channel pollution.
  - **Direct Message (DM) Welcome & Fallback**: verified members automatically receive a private Discord DM with their updated FGF combat summary (power, glory, role, assigned nickname); if direct messages are blocked, a non-intrusive public mention auto-destructs after 20 seconds.
  - **Officer Audit Logging**: automatically dispatches rich embed verification records to the designated officer audit channel (`discord_audit_channel_id` / `discord_officer_channel_id`), archiving the member's pseudo, UID, power, and role.
  - **Guild Leaderboards (`/top`)**: introduces the `/top` slash command supporting Overall Power, Glory Score, and Fleet Rating categories with medal formatting (🥇, 🥈, 🥉) and formatted numerical tiers for the guild roster.
  - **Enriched Member Profiles (`/profile`)**: `/profile` embeds now dynamically calculate the target member's live local time based on their configured timezone offset (e.g. `16:30 (UTC+2)`) and cross-reference active leaves to display their live availability status (`🟢 Active` or `🔴 Absent until YYYY-MM-DD`).
  - **Interactive Call-to-Arms War Check-In (`/call-to-arms`)**: officers can initiate live battle roll-calls with interactive native Discord buttons (`🟢 Present`, `🟡 Late (15-30m)`, `🔴 Absent`); dynamically aggregates the total mobilized fleet rating and cumulative combat power in real-time as players check in.
  - **Absence Briefings & Alerts (`/absences`)**: officers can inspect active absences today or planned leaves over the next 7 days via `/absences [today|upcoming]`; new absence declarations via `/absent` automatically alert the officer channel.
  - **Quick Stats Update (`/update-stats`)**: allows verified guild members to update their roster power or glory score directly via Discord.
  - **Verification**: `npm run type-check` — 0 errors; **1384/1384 Vitest tests green across 116 suites** (+15 new regression tests in `tests/discord_bot_advanced.test.js`); production build clean.

## [3.18.2]

- **Feature: Discord reminder message personalization overhaul (Combined Solutions A, B, C & D) (V3.18.2)** — modernized reminder customization with horizontal tab navigation, 1-click token pills, real-time Discord live preview, and 1-click reset to default templates.
  - **Tabs Navigation (Solution C)**: horizontal tab bar (`.discord-custom-tabs`) allows guild admins to configure each reminder individually (`30m`, `5m`, `Event Start`, `Defense`, `Garrison`), eliminating long, vertical 21-input forms.
  - **Tag Insertion Toolbar (Solution A)**: 1-click pill buttons (`.discord-pills-bar`, `.discord-pill-btn`) under each input insert Discord placeholders (`{guild_tag}`, `@everyone`, `{event_name}`, `{date}`, `{round}`) at the active cursor position, eliminating manual syntax errors.
  - **Discord Live Preview (Solution B)**: right-hand realistic Discord dark theme card (`#313338`) simulating the bot message header, notification ping with highlighted role badges (`.discord-mention`), embed card with dynamic border accents (amber, red, emerald, cyan), and real-time live typing updates.
  - **Reset to Default**: 1-click reset button (`.discord-reset-btn`) clears custom overrides per reminder and restores the native template in the live preview.
  - **Strict Security & Backward Compatibility**: all preview inputs are escaped via `esc()` before formatting (XSS prevention); all inputs remain in the DOM across all tabs, preserving full backward compatibility with `guild_config` (`tpl_${prefix}_${key}_content|title|desc`) and automated test suites.
  - **Verification**: `npm run type-check` — 0 errors; **1369/1369 Vitest tests green across 115 suites**; production build clean.

- **Fix: Discord notification system end-to-end resolution & PWA push removal (V3.18.2)** — resolved Discord webhook delivery failures, wired event modal triggers, and completely removed redundant mobile PWA web push notifications.
  - **Fixed (Discord webhook settings UI & persistence)**: added Default Discord Webhook URL input (`#discord-webhook-url`) and test trigger (`#btn-test-default-webhook`) to the Admin Discord interface in `index.html`; registered `discord_webhook_url` in `VALUE_FIELDS` in `src/modules/app/admin/guild-requests.controller.ts`; wired `#btn-dispatch-gvg-tasks` to build and send the current day's GvG tasks directly to Discord with instant feedback toasts.
  - **Fixed (webhook fallback resolution)**: in `supabase/functions/_shared/reminder-rules.ts`, updated `resolveGuildWebhook` to treat empty and whitespace-only specific webhooks as unset, properly falling back to `discord_webhook_url` or event category webhooks instead of silencing events.
  - **Fixed (lock false-sent bug)**: in `supabase/functions/event-reminders/index.ts`, eliminated `sentSuccess = true` when webhook URLs are missing or dispatch fails, preventing the cron worker from permanently locking reminders without actual delivery.
  - **Fixed (webhook proxy regex)**: in `supabase/functions/discord-webhook-proxy/index.ts`, updated `DISCORD_WEBHOOK_PATH_REGEX` to accept trailing slashes and added caller tenant fallback.
  - **Fixed (Discord message customization modal wiring)**: wired click event listeners on all `[data-custom-msg]` buttons (`armsrace`, `dtr`, `shadowfront`, `calamity`, `gvg`, `svs`) to `openCustomMsgModal(prefix)` inside `initGuildRequestsUi()`, resolving the edit/pencil icon doing nothing when clicked.
  - **Deployed (Supabase Edge Functions)**: deployed the latest `event-reminders` (v68) and `discord-webhook-proxy` (v19) to Supabase production, activating the webhook fallback resolution and eliminating false "sent" locks for automated cron dispatches.
  - **Verification**: `npm run type-check` — 0 errors; **1369/1369 Vitest tests green across 115 suites**; production build clean.

## [3.18.1]

- **Fix: Player portal routing & empty admin dashboard prevention for members (V3.18.1)** — resolved normal members landing on an empty Admin Dashboard with 0s upon sign-in or session refresh.
  - **Fixed (post-login routing)**: `handleSuccessfulLogin()` in `src/modules/app/auth/auth.controller.ts` now branches immediately on `role === 'member'`, persisting `gm_role = 'member'`, `gm_portal_session = '1'`, and calling `revealPortal()`, preventing any execution of `revealAdmin(role)` for members.
  - **Fixed (session restoration)**: `restoreSession()` now unconditionally routes any user whose resolved role is `member` directly to `revealPortal()` and sets `gm_portal_session = '1'`, eliminating the prior requirement of a pre-existing `gm_portal_session` flag in localStorage that previously dumped members into `revealAdmin(role)` on page reloads.
  - **Fixed (boot navigation guard)**: in `src/main.ts`, `restoreSavedTab()` is now bypassed on boot if `res.role === 'member'` or `res.portal === true`, preventing automatic switching to the admin `overview` tab for authenticated members.
  - **Fixed (defense-in-depth)**: `revealAdmin(role)` now logs a warning and redirects to `revealPortal()` if invoked with `'member'`, while hiding `#player-portal-view` when revealing the admin view; `restoreSavedTab()` and `onTabActivated()` in `src/modules/app/router.ts` block activation of admin tabs when `gm_role === 'member'`; `handlePortalLookupSuccess()` in `src/modules/app/auth/portal.controller.ts` explicitly hides `#login-view` and `#dashboard-view`.
  - **Verification**: `npm run type-check` — 0 errors; **1370/1370 Vitest tests green across 115 suites** (+6 new regression tests in `tests/app_auth_bootstrap.test.js` and `tests/app_router.test.js`); production build clean.

- **Fix: SvS & GvG daily win/loss 3-state toggle cycle & NULL reset persistence (V3.18.1)** — streamlined daily outcome recording with intuitive Neutral → Won → Lost → Neutral cycling.
  - **Fixed (database NULL clear)**: `gm_upsert_war_session` in `supabase/migrations/20260915130000_fix_war_session_day_results_clear.sql` now checks `CASE WHEN p_daily_results ? 'dayX' THEN v_d{X} ELSE war_sessions.day{X}_result END` instead of using `COALESCE(v_dX, war_sessions.dayX_result)`, which previously blocked resetting any day result back to `NULL` (neutral) and caused the toggle to get permanently stuck on 'lost'.
  - **Fixed (UI/UX 3-state cycling & styling)**: Day toggles D1..D6 default to **Neutral** (soft subtle border, `—` badge in default tone); 1st click marks **Won** (green tint, `W` success badge); 2nd click marks **Lost** (red tint, `L` danger badge); 3rd click smoothly returns to **Neutral** (clears back to `—`). Added dynamic tooltips (`Day {d}: Neutral (Click: Won)`, `Day {d}: Won (Click: Lost)`, `Day {d}: Lost (Click: Neutral)`), `data-result` attribute, `user-select: none`, hover micro-interaction (`translateY(-1px)`), and focus rings.
  - **Fixed (in-memory state merge)**: initialized local session state in `cycleWarDay` when `war.session` is empty, and merged server responses non-destructively so rapid consecutive clicks across multiple days never drop in-flight clicks.
  - **Verification**: `npm run type-check` — 0 errors; **1364/1364 Vitest tests green across 115 suites** (+1 new suite / +8 regression tests in `tests/war_day_toggles.test.js`); production build clean.

- **Fix: OCR German localization multiplier parsing & runaway trillion inflation defense (V3.18.1)** — resolved German damage/score leaderboard OCR misinterpreting `T` (Tausend = 1,000) as trillions.
  - **Fixed (German localization & multiplier resolution)**: in German/European game clients ("Schadensrangliste", "Gesamtschaden"), `T` / `Tsd` stands for **Tausend** (1,000 / 10^3) rather than Trillion. Gemini Vision previously lacked explicit localization instructions and misinterpreted `T` as Trillion (10^12) or Tera, inflating scores into trillions/billions while ranks below (`5.663`) remained in thousands.
  - **Fixed (prompt & schema)**: enriched `SYSTEM_INSTRUCTION` in `supabase/functions/ocr-guild-members/index.ts` with explicit section "2.
